Half the girls in the room had not heard Kitty’s proposal that Ingred should be chosen, and some of the others, listening imperfectly, had gathered that she was not able to go to the match, so without giving her a further thought they raised hands in favor of Bess, and the matter was carried.
“But indeed I’m no good at writing or describing things!” protested Bess.
“Yes, you are! You’ve got to try, so there!” cried her friends triumphantly. “You’ll do it just as well as anybody else would.”
Ingred turned away with a red-hot spot raging under her blouse. That she, the warden of the form, should have been passed over in favor of a girl whose sole qualification seemed to be that she could offer some of the others a lift in her car, was a very nasty knock. Was Bess to supplant her in everything?
===Chinese===
“我附议。”弗朗茜（Francie）宣布道，“姑娘们，举手！这场盛事就请贝丝来‘当家’。”
屋里半数姑娘压根儿没听见凯蒂提议让英格丽德来写；另外有几个听了个半懂不懂，只当她去不成比赛，于是也不再为她多想，齐齐举手赞成贝丝，事情就这样定了案。
“可我实在不擅长写东西、也不擅长描摹场面呀！”贝丝推辞道。
“你行！就得试一试，没二话！”朋友们得意洋洋地喊道，“你会写得跟谁都不差。”
英格丽德扭过身去，衬衫底下有一处地方烧得火辣辣的。她堂堂一班的班代表，竟被撇在一边，让位给一个姑娘——而此人唯一的资格，似乎就是能用自家的汽车捎带其他几位同学。这一记闷棍实在够呛。难道贝丝要在事事处处都压她一头？

===Original===
“Perhaps you’d like to make her warden instead of me!” she remarked bitterly to Belle Charlton, who stood near. “I’m perfectly willing to resign if you’re tired of me!”
Belle only giggled and poked Joanna Powers, who said:
“Don’t be nasty, Ingred! Bess is a sport, and we most of us like her.”
“I can’t see the attraction myself!” snapped Ingred.
She did not want to go to the hockey match now, and made up her mind obstinately that nothing in this wide world should decoy her to it. Bess came to school next morning armed with full permission to use her father’s car and to invite as many of her schoolfellows as it would accommodate. She cordially pressed Ingred to join the party.
===Chinese===
“你们干脆让她顶了我的班代表算了！”她酸溜溜地对站在近旁的贝尔·查尔顿（Belle Charlton）说，“要是嫌了我，我万分乐意辞职！”
贝尔只是咯咯直笑，捅了捅乔安娜·鲍尔斯（Joanna Powers），后者开口道：
“别这么刻薄嘛，英格丽德！贝丝是个爽快人，我们大半都喜欢她。”
“我是看不出她有什么可爱之处！”英格丽德抢白道。
她如今不想去看曲棍球赛了，还执拗地打定主意：这世上任凭什么也休想诱她前去。第二天早晨，贝丝揣着父亲的十足许可来上学——汽车归她用，车里坐得下多少同学，就可以邀请多少。她热诚地拉英格丽德入伙。

===Original===
“I’m not going to the match, thanks,” replied the latter frigidly.
“But there’s heaps of room﻿—there is indeed, without a frightful squash.”
“There’s something I want to do at home on Saturday.”
“Couldn’t you do it in the morning? The form will be disappointed if you don’t go﻿—and, I say﻿—” (shyly) “I wish you’d write that wretched report instead of me. I hate the idea of doing it!”
“The form won’t care twopence whether I go or stay away, and as they’ve chosen you to write the report you’ll have to write it or it’ll be left undone,” retorted Ingred perversely.
===Chinese===
“我不去看比赛，谢谢。”后者冷冰冰地答道。
“可是车里宽敞得很——真的宽敞，一点儿也不用挤得难受。”
“星期六我在家里有点事要办。”
“不能挪到上午去办吗？你要是不去，全班都会失望的——还有，我说——”（她怯生生地）“真希望那篇讨厌的报道由你来替我写。一想到要写我就发怵！”
“我去不去，班里才不会在乎半个铜子儿。既然选了你写报道，你就非写不可，不然它就得落空。”英格丽德别别扭扭地回敬道。

===Original===
Bess, looking decidedly hurt, turned away. Her little efforts at friendship with Ingred were invariably met in this most ungracious fashion. She could not understand why her kindly-meant advances should always be so systematically repulsed. Ingred, on her part, stalked off with the mean feeling of one who at bottom knows she is in the wrong, but won’t acknowledge it even to herself. Under the sub-current of indignation she realized that she would have liked Bess immensely if only the latter had not taken up her residence at Rotherwood. That, however, was an offense which she deemed it quite impossible ever to forgive.
Ingred went about her work that morning in a very scratchy mood, so much so as to attract the attention of Miss Strong, who possibly felt a little prickly herself, since even teachers have their phases of temper. It was at that time a fashion in the form for the girls to keep all sorts of absurd mascots inside their desks, the collecting and comparison of which afforded them huge satisfaction. Now Miss Strong happened to be lecturing on “The Age of Elizabeth,” a subject so congenial to her that she was generally most interesting. But today she had reached a rather dry and arid portion of that famous reign, and even her powers of description failed for once and the lesson became a mere catalogue of events and dates. Ingred, bored stiff with listening, secretly opened her desk, and, taking a selection of treasures from it, began to fondle them surreptitiously upon her lap. It was, of course, a quite illegal thing to do. She glanced at them occasionally, but for the most part kept her eyes upon her teacher. Beatrice, however, who sat near and had an excellent view of Ingred’s lap, gazed at it with such persistent and marked attention that she attracted the notice of Miss Strong, who followed the direction of her looks and pounced upon the offender.
“Ingred Saxon, what have you there? Bring those things to me immediately and put them on my desk!”
===Chinese===
贝丝一脸分明受了伤害的神情，转身走开。她一次次向英格丽德表示友好，回回都遭到这样不客气的待遇。她不明白，自己好意的亲近为何总是被这样有章有法地顶回来。英格丽德呢，悻悻地大步走开了，心里怀着一种不大光彩的感觉——那种人明知自己理亏，却连对自己都不肯认账的感觉。在愤懑的暗流底下，她其实明白：倘若贝丝没有住进罗瑟伍德，她准会非常喜欢这个姑娘。然而那一桩，在她看来是永远、永远无法宽恕的罪过。
那天上午英格丽德做功课，满心烦躁，浑身带刺，连斯特朗小姐（Miss Strong）都被惊动了——这位小姐自己兴许也有点不顺气，教师毕竟也有闹脾气的时候。那时班里正时兴往课桌里藏各式各样可笑的吉祥物，搜集与比较这些宝贝给了她们极大的乐趣。恰巧斯特朗小姐近来在讲“伊丽莎白时代”，这题目正对她脾胃，平日里讲得格外有味。可这一天，她讲到了那个著名朝代中一段枯燥干瘪的部分，连她描摹生辉的口才也破天荒失了灵，一堂课变成了流水账，净是事件和年月。英格丽德听得腻烦至极，悄悄掀开课桌，从里面拣出几样宝贝，偷偷放在膝上摩挲。这自然是十足犯规的事。她只偶尔瞟它们一眼，大半时候眼睛还盯着老师。可碧翠丝坐在近旁，把英格丽德的膝头看得一清二楚；她盯着那儿看个不停、看得那样惹眼，反倒引来了斯特朗小姐的注意。老师顺着她的目光一找，便一把逮住了犯规者。
“英格丽德·萨克森，你那儿是什么东西？立刻把它们拿到我这儿来，摆到我的讲桌上！”

===Original===
With a crimson face Ingred obeyed, and handed over into the teacher’s custody:
1. A black velvet cat.
2. A small golliwog.
3. A piece of four-leaved clover.
4. A stone with a hole in it.
5. An ivory pig.
===Chinese===
英格丽德红着脸照办，交到老师监管之下的东西有：
1. 一只黑天鹅绒小猫。
2. 一个小黑脸丑娃。
3. 一片四叶幸运草。
4. 一块带孔的小石头。
5. 一头象牙小猪。

===Original===
Miss Strong smiled cynically.
“At fifteen years of age,” she remarked, “I should have thought a girl would have advanced a little further than playthings of this description. The Kindergarten would evidently be a more fit form for you than **Va**! You lose five order marks.”
Five order marks! Ingred gasped with amazed indignation. One at a time was the usual forfeit, but to lose five “at one fell swoop” seemed excessive, and would make a considerable difference to her weekly record. She blazed against the injustice. No girl in the form had ever had so severe punishment.
===Chinese===
斯特朗小姐讥诮地一笑。
“都十五岁了，”她说，“我还以为一个姑娘总该比玩这类玩具再长进一些。照这样看来，幼儿园显然比五甲班更适合你！扣你五分操行分。”
五分操行分！英格丽德又惊又气，倒抽一口冷气。往常一次不过扣一分，这回“一口气”竟扣掉五分，未免太狠，还要给她的每周成绩记上不小的亏空。她为这不公愤懑不已。班里从没有哪个姑娘受过这么重的罚。

===Original===
“Oh, Miss Strong!” she protested hotly. “*Five!* I haven’t really done anything more than heaps of the others. It’s not fair!”
Now if Ingred had really hoped to get her sentence remitted she could not have done a more absolutely suicidal thing. A mistress may overlook some faults, but she will not stand “cheek.” The discipline of the form was at stake, and Miss Strong was not a mistress to be trifled with. Her little figure absolutely quivered with dignity, and though physically she was shorter than her pupil, morally she seemed to tower yards. She fixed her clear dark eyes in a kind of hypnotic stare on Ingred and remarked witheringly:
“That will do! I don’t allow *any* girl to speak to me in this fashion! You’ll take a cross for conduct as well as losing the five order marks. You may go to your seat now.”
===Chinese===
“哦，斯特朗小姐！”她激动地抗议，“*五分*！我实在没有比许多别人多干什么出格的事。这不公平！”
倘若英格丽德当真指望这笔处分能获减免，那她这一手可谓自寻死路到了家。教师可以宽宥种种过失，却绝容不得“顶嘴”。全班的纪律危在旦夕，何况斯特朗小姐也绝非可以轻慢的老师。她那小小的身躯竟因威仪而微微震颤；论身量她比学生还矮，论气概却仿佛高出好几码。她那双清亮的黑眼睛直勾勾地盯住英格丽德，目光近乎催眠，刻薄入骨地说道：
“够了！我不许*任何一个*姑娘用这种腔调同我说话！操行分照扣，操行栏里再记你一个叉。现在回你的座位去。”

===Original===
Ingred walked back to her desk covered with humiliation. To be publicly rebuked before the whole form was an unpleasant experience, particularly for a warden. Beatrice, Francie, and several others were holding up self-righteous noses, though their desks contained an equal assortment of mascots. Ingred, still seething, made little attempt to listen to the rest of the lecture, and was obliged to pass the questions which came to her afterwards on the subject-matter. She was heartily thankful when eleven o’clock brought the brief ten minutes “break.”
“Well, you *have* been a lunatic this morning!” said Beatrice, passing her, biscuits in hand, in the cloakroom. “What possessed you to go and lose the tennis-court for the form?”
“If you hadn’t stared so hard at me Miss Strong would never have noticed.”
“Oh, of course! Throw the blame on somebody else! You’re always the ‘little white hen that never lays astray.’ ”
===Chinese===
英格丽德走回座位，满面羞惭。当着全班公开受责，本就不是好受的滋味，对一位班代表来说尤其如此。碧翠丝、弗朗茜和另外几个都翘起了自命清高的鼻子，尽管她们课桌里的吉祥物同样琳琅满目。英格丽德余怒未消，几乎没再听后半堂课，过后就讲课内容向她提问时，只得放弃作答。十一点的钟声一响，带来短短十分钟的“课间”，她打心底里谢天谢地。
“哎，你今天上午可真是疯了！”碧翠丝手里捏着饼干，在衣帽间同她擦肩而过，“你中了什么邪，跑去害全班丢了网球场？”
“要不是你死盯着我看，斯特朗小姐压根儿不会察觉。”
“哦，当然了！把过错推到别人头上！你呀，永远是那只‘从不乱下蛋的小白母鸡’。”

===Original===
“Kitty and Evie and Belle and I had arranged a set!” grumbled Cicely Denham. “It’s most unfair, this rule of punishing the whole form for what one girl does!”
“Go and tell Miss Burd so then!” flared Ingred. “It hasn’t been very successful so far to tell teachers they’re not fair, but you may have better luck than I had. She’ll probably say: ‘Oh, yes, Cicely dear, I’ll rearrange the rules at once!’ So like her, isn’t it?”
“Now you’re sark! Almost as sarky as the Snark herself!” commented Cicely, as Ingred, choking over a last biscuit, stumped away.
There is much written nowadays about the unconscious power of thought waves, and certainly one grumbler can often spread dissatisfaction through an entire community. Perhaps the black looks which Ingred encountered from the disappointed tennis-players in her form turned into naughty sprites who whispered treason in the ears of the juniors, or perhaps it was a mere coincidence that mutiny suddenly broke out in the Lower School. It began with a company of ten-year-olds who, with pencil boxes and drawing books, were being escorted by Althea Riley, one of the prefects, along the corridor to the studio. Hitherto, by dint of judicious curbing, they had always walked two and two in decent line and had refrained from prohibited conversation. Today they surged upstairs in an unseemly rabble, chattering and talking like a flock of rooks or jackdaws at sunset. It was in vain that Althea tried to restore order, her efforts at discipline were simply scouted by the unruly mob, who rushed into the studio helter-skelter, took their places anyhow, and only controlled themselves at the entrance of Miss Godwin, the art mistress.
===Chinese===
“我和凯蒂、伊薇（Evie）、贝尔本来约好打一盘球的！”西塞莉·德纳姆嘟囔道，“一个人闯祸全班受罚，这规矩太不公平了！”
“那你去跟伯德小姐说呀！”英格丽德勃然作色，“照目前的情形看，跟老师们说她们不公平，一向没什么好结果，不过你的运气或许比我的好。她多半会这么说：‘哦，好的，亲爱的西塞莉，我马上把规矩改一改！’她就是这么通情达理，对不对？”
“瞧你这话说的，够尖刻的了！都快赶上‘毒舌精’本尊了！”西塞莉评论道。英格丽德噎着咽下最后一块饼干，蹬蹬蹬地走了。
如今论“思想波的无形力量”，文章写得不少；而一个爱发牢骚的人，确实往往能把不满散布到整个群体里去。也许是英格丽德从班上那群看不成网球的姑娘那里领受的黑脸，化作了促狭的小精怪，凑在低年级耳边煽风点火；也许纯属巧合——低年级部突然爆发了一场哗变。事端起于一队十岁的小姑娘：她们带着铅笔盒和图画本，正由级长之一的阿尔西娅·赖利（Althea Riley）护送着沿走廊去画室。以往，靠着力道拿捏得当的勒缰，她们总能两人一排走得规规矩矩，也忍得住不许交谈的禁令。今天她们却涌上楼来，乱糟糟挤作一团，叽叽喳喳，活像日落时分的一群白嘴鸦或寒鸦。阿尔西娅怎么维持秩序也是徒劳，她整饬纪律的努力被这伙乱民干脆嗤之以鼻——她们横冲直撞涌进画室，随随便便占了座位，直到美术教师戈德温小姐（Miss Godwin）进门，才收敛下来。

===Original===
Althea, flushed, indignant, and most upset, sought her fellow-prefects.
“Shall I go and complain to Miss Burd?” she asked.
“Um﻿—I don’t think I should yet,” said Lispeth a little doubtfully. “You see, Miss Burd has given us authority and she likes us to use it ourselves as much as we can, without appealing to her. Of course in any extremity she’ll support us. I’ll pin up a notice in the junior cloakroom and see what effect that has. It may settle them.”
Lispeth stayed after four o’clock until the last coat and hat had disappeared from the hooks in the juniors’ dressing-room. Then she pinned her ultimatum on their notice board:
===Chinese===
阿尔西娅涨红着脸，又气又恼，心绪大乱，去找她级长团里的同伴们。
“我要不要去伯德小姐那儿告一状？”她问。
“唔——我看先不必。”丽丝佩丝（Lispeth）略带迟疑地说，“你要明白，伯德小姐把权限交给了我们，她乐意我们自己尽量行使，不必事事向她求援。当然，真到了万不得已，她会为我们撑腰。我先在低年级衣帽间贴一张告示，看看效果如何，兴许就能镇住她们。”
四点钟以后，丽丝佩丝留了下来，一直守到低年级更衣室的挂钩上最后一件外套、最后一顶帽子消失为止。然后，她把这份最后通牒钉上了她们的布告栏：

===Original===
“In consequence of the extremely bad behavior of certain girls on the stairs this afternoon, the prefects give notice that should any repetition of such conduct occur, the names of the offenders will be taken and they will be reported to Miss Burd for punishment.”
“That ought to finish those kids!” she thought as she pushed in the drawing-pins.
There was more than the usual amount of buzzing conversation next morning as juvenile heads bumped each other in their efforts to read the notice. The result, however, was absolutely unprecedented in the annals of the school. It was the custom of the Sixth Form, and of many of the Fifth, to take their lunch and eat it quietly in the gymnasium. There was no hard and fast rule about this, but it was generally understood to be a privilege of the upper forms only, and intermediates and juniors were not supposed to intrude. Today most of the elder girls were sitting in clumps at the far end of the gymnasium, when through the open door marched a most amazing procession of juniors. They were headed by Phyllis Smith and Dorrie Barnes carrying between them a small blackboard upon which was chalked:
===Chinese===
“鉴于今日下午若干女生在楼梯上的行为极端恶劣，级长特此通告：此类行为如有再犯，必将记下肇事者姓名，报请伯德小姐惩处。”
“这下该叫那帮小崽子消停了！”她一边按上图钉一边想。
第二天早晨，一颗颗小脑袋你挤我、我挤你地争看告示，嗡嗡的议论比平日多了好几倍。然而事情的结果，在校史上前所未有。六年级以及五年级的许多同学，惯常把午饭端到体育馆里安安静静地吃。这并无明文规定，但大家默认这是高年级独有的特权，中间年级与低年级不得擅入。这一天，年长的姑娘们正三三两两坐在体育馆最里头，只见敞开的门口开进来一支蔚为壮观的低年级游行队伍。领头的菲莉斯·史密斯（Phyllis Smith）和多丽·巴恩斯（Dorrie Barnes）两人抬着一块小黑板，上面用粉笔写着：

===Original===
> Down with prefects! 

 Rights for juniors! 

 The whole school is equal!
After these ringleaders marched a determined crowd waving flags made of handkerchiefs fastened to the end of rulers. A band, equipped with combs covered with tissue-paper torn from their drawing-books, played the strains of the “Marseillaise.” They advanced towards the seniors in a very truculent fashion.
“Well, really!” exclaimed Lispeth, recovering from her momentary amazement. “What’s the meaning of all this, I’d like to know?”
“It’s a strike!” said Dorrie proudly, as she and Phyllis paused so as to display the blackboard before the eyes of the Sixth. “We don’t see why you big girls should lord it over us any longer. We’ll obey the mistresses, but we’ll not obey prefects.”
===Chinese===
> 打倒级长！

 低年级要权利！

 全校一律平等！
在这几名头目之后，行进着一支意志坚决的大军，挥舞着用手绢系在尺子一端做成的旗帜。还有一支乐队，装备是蒙着绵纸的梳子——纸是从图画本上撕下来的——正奏着《马赛曲》的调子。她们气势汹汹地朝高年级逼近。
“哎呀，真是岂有此理！”丽丝佩丝从一时的惊愕中回过神来，喊道，“我倒想知道，这是唱的哪一出？”
“这是罢课！”多丽骄傲地说。她和菲莉斯停住脚步，好把黑板亮在六年级众人眼前。“我们不明白，凭什么你们大姑娘还要骑在我们头上作威作福。女老师的话我们听，级长的话我们偏不听。”

===Original===
“You’ll just jolly well do as you’re told, you impudent young monkeys!” declared Lispeth, losing her temper. “Here, clear out of this gymnasium at once!”
“We shan’t! We’ve as good a right here as you!”
“We ought to send wardens to the School Parliament.”
“We haven’t any voice in school affairs!”
“It’s not fair!”
“We shan’t stand it any longer!”
===Chinese===
“你们这帮不知天高地厚的小猴崽子，乖乖听话就对了！”丽丝佩丝动了气，厉声宣布，“喂，马上从这体育馆里滚出去！”
“我们偏不！这儿我们跟你们一样有份！”
“我们也该选班代表进学校议会！”
“学校的事，我们连一句话都说不上！”
“这不公平！”
“我们再也忍不下去了！”

===Original===
The shrill voices of the insurgents reached crescendo as they hurled forth their defiance. They were evidently bent on red-hot revolution. Lispeth rose to read the Riot Act.
“If you don’t take yourselves off I shall go for Miss Burd, and a nice row you’d get into then. I give you while I count ten. One﻿—two﻿—three﻿—four﻿—”
Whether the strikers would have stood their ground or not is still an unsolved problem, but at that opportune moment the big school bell began to clang, and Miss Willough, the drill mistress, in her blue tunic, entered the gymnasium ready to take her next class. At sight of her, Dorrie hastily wiped the blackboard, and the juniors fled to their own form-rooms, suppressing flags and musical instruments on the way. Miss Willough gazed at them meditatively, but made no comment, and the Sixth, hurrying to a literature lesson, had no time to offer explanations.
===Chinese===
造反姑娘们的尖嗓门愈拔愈高，把满腔挑战劈头盖脸地掷出来。她们分明是铁了心，要闹一场轰轰烈烈的革命。丽丝佩丝站起身来，正颜厉色宣读“平乱告示”。
“你们再不自行离开，我就去请伯德小姐，到时候有你们好瞧的。我数十个数。一——二——三——四——”
罢课的队伍究竟会不会坚守阵地，至今仍是一道无解的难题；恰在此时，学校大钟铛铛敲响，体操教师威洛小姐（Miss Willough）穿着蓝色操练服走进体育馆，准备上下一节课。她一露面，多丽慌忙把黑板擦掉，低年级姑娘们一路收好旗帜和乐器，逃回各自的教室。威洛小姐若有所思地目送她们，未作任何评论；六年级匆匆赶去上文学课，也来不及解释。

===Original===
Lispeth, more upset than she cared to own, talked the matter over with her mother when she went to dinner at one o’clock. She was a very conscientious girl and anxious to do her duty as “Head.” As a result of the home conference she went to Miss Burd, explained the situation, and asked to be allowed to have the whole school together for ten minutes before four o’clock.
“It’s only lately there’s been this trouble,” she said. “I believe if I talk nicely to the girls I can get back my influence. That’s what Mother advised. She said ‘try persuasion first.’ ”
“She’s right, too,” agreed Miss Burd. “If you can get them to obey you willingly it’s far better than if I have to step in and put my foot down. What we want is to change the general current of thought.”
===Chinese===
丽丝佩丝心里的慌乱，远比她肯承认的厉害；一点钟去吃午饭时，她把这件事同母亲商量了一番。她是个极有责任心的姑娘，一心想尽好“学生长”的本分。这场家庭会商的结果，是她去找伯德小姐，说明情况，并请求在四点前把全校学生集中十分钟。
“这乱子不过是最近才闹起来的，”她说，“我相信，只要我好言好语地同姑娘们谈谈，就能把威信赢回来。这正是妈妈的劝告。她说：‘先试试说服。’”
“她说得也对。”伯德小姐表示赞同，“如果你能让她们心甘情愿地服从，那要比非得我出面、采取强硬手段好得多。我们需要的是扭转总体的思想潮流。”

===Original===
Speculation was rife in the various forms as the closing bell rang at 3:45 instead of at 4 o’clock, and the girls were told to assemble in the Lecture Hall, and were put on their honor to behave themselves. To their surprise, the mistresses, after seeing them seated, left the room. Miss Burd mounted the platform and announced:
“Lispeth Scott wishes to speak to you all, and I should like you to know that anything she has to say is said with my entire approval and sanction. I hope you will listen to her in perfect silence.”
Then she followed the other mistresses.
All eyes were fixed on Lispeth as she ascended the platform. With her tall ample figure, earnest blue eyes, light hair, and fair face flushed with the excitement of her task she looked a typical English girl, and made what she hoped was a typical English speech.
“I asked you to come,” she began rather shyly, “because I think lately there have been some misunderstandings in the school, and I want, if possible, to put them straight. There has been a good deal of talk about ‘equality,’ and some of you say there oughtn’t to be prefects. I wonder exactly what you mean by ‘equality?’ Certainly all girls aren’t born with equal talents, yet each separate soul is of value to the community and must not go to waste. The test of a school is not how many show pupils it has turned out, but how *all* its pupils are prepared to face the world. I think we can only do this by sticking together and trying to help each other. In every community, however, there must be leaders. An army would soon go to pieces without its officers! The prefects and wardens have been chosen as leaders, and it ought to be a point of honor with you to uphold their authority. I assure you they don’t work for their own good, but for the good of the school. I hear it is a grievance with the juniors that they mayn’t elect wardens for the Council. Well﻿—they shall do that when they’re older; it will be something for them to look forward to! There’s a privilege, though, that we can and will give them. We’re going to start a Junior branch of the Rainbow League, and I think when they’re doing their level best to help others, they’ll forget about themselves. Carlyle says that the very dullest drudge has the elements of a hero in him if he once sees the chance of aiming at something higher than happiness. Please don’t say I’m preaching, for I hate to be a prig! Only we’d all made up our minds to do our ‘bit’ in ‘after the war work,’ and it seems such a pity if we forget, and let the tone of the school drop﻿—as it certainly *has* dropped lately. I’m sure if we all think about it we can keep it up, and Seniors and Juniors can work together without any horrid squabbles. We big girls were juniors ourselves once, and you little ones will be seniors some day, so that’s one way of looking at it. Now that’s all I’ve got to say, except that any Juniors who like can stay behind now and join the Junior Branch of the Rainbow League. We want to get up a special Scrapbook Union, and Miss Burd says she’ll give a prize for the best scrapbook, and also for the best homemade doll. She’s going to have an exhibition on breaking-up day.”
===Chinese===
放学铃在三点三刻而非四点敲响，各班顿时猜测纷纭；姑娘们奉命到大讲堂集合，并以名誉担保守规矩。令她们惊讶的是，各位女教师看着她们坐定之后，竟退出了讲堂。伯德小姐登上讲台，宣布道：
“丽丝佩丝·斯科特（Lispeth Scott）有话要对大家讲，而且我希望你们知道，她要说的任何话，都得到我完全的赞同与授权。我希望大家屏息静听。”
说完，她便跟着其余教师出去了。
丽丝佩丝走上讲台时，所有的目光都聚在她身上。高挑丰润的身量，诚挚的蓝眼睛，浅色的头发，还有因肩负任务而兴奋得绯红的白皙面庞——她看上去是典型的英国姑娘，也作了一篇她心目中典型的英国式演讲。
“我请你们大家来，”她开口时颇有几分腼腆，“是因为我想，近来学校里有了一些误会，我想尽可能把它们澄清。关于‘平等’的议论已经不少，你们中间还有人说，根本就不该设级长。我倒想弄清楚，你们所说的‘平等’究竟是什么意思？诚然，姑娘们的天资生来并不相等，然而每一个独立的心灵对群体都有自己的价值，绝不容白白浪费。检验一所学校的标准，不在于它培养出多少出挑的学生，而在于它*所有*的学生是否都准备好了去面对世界。我想，要做到这一点，唯有大家抱成团，尽力互相帮助。不过，无论哪个群体，总得有领头人。一支军队要是没有军官，很快就得散伙！级长和班代表正是被选出来做领头人的，维护她们的权威，理应是你们人人视为荣誉的事。我向你们保证，她们不是为自己谋好处，而是为学校谋好处。我听说低年级有一桩委屈：她们不能选举班代表进学校议会。唔——等她们年纪大些，自然就可以；那也是她们的一份盼头！不过有一项特权，我们现在就能够、也愿意交给她们。我们要成立彩虹联盟（Rainbow League）的低年级分部。我想，当她们全力以赴去帮助别人的时候，自会忘掉自己。卡莱尔说过：哪怕最木讷的苦工，一旦看见有机会去追求比幸福更高的东西，他身上也自藏着英雄的根苗。请别说我是在说教，因为我顶讨厌自命清高！只是我们大家原先都下了决心，要在‘战后的工作’里各尽一份力；倘若我们忘了，任凭学校的风气滑落——近来它确实*滑落*了——那就太可惜了。我相信，只要我们人人都把这事放在心上，就能把这股风气维持下去，高年级与低年级也能同心协力，不再有那些恶声恶气的口角。我们这些大姑娘也当过低年级，你们这些小不点儿有朝一日也会成为高年级——不妨这样想一想。我要说的就是这些了，只不过，愿意留下的低年级同学，现在就可以留下来，加入彩虹联盟低年级分部。我们想专门办一个剪贴簿联合会，伯德小姐说，她要为最好的剪贴簿设一个奖，也为最好的自制娃娃设一个奖。散学日那天，她还要办一个展览。”

## II Opening Day / 第二章 开学日

===Original===
The Girls’ College at Grovebury, under its able headmistress, Miss Burd, had made itself quite a name in the neighborhood. The governors, realizing that it was outgrowing its old premises, decided to erect others, and had put up a handsome building in a good situation near the Abbey. No sooner was the last tile laid on the roof, however, than war broke out, and the new school was immediately commandeered by the Government as a recruiting office, and it had been kept for that purpose until after the Armistice.
The girls considered it a very great grievance to be obliged to remain cramped so long in their old college. The foundation stone of the new building had been laid by Queen Mary herself, and they thought the Government might have fixed upon some other spot in which to conduct business, instead of keeping them out of their proper quarters. All things come to an end, however, even the circumlocution and delays of Government offices, and by the beginning of the autumn term the removal had been effected, and the ceremony arranged for the opening of the new college. Naturally it was to be a great day. The Members of Parliament for Grovebury, and the Mayor, and many other important people were to be present, to say nothing of parents and visitors. The pupils, assembled in the freshly color-washed dressing-rooms, greeted one another excitedly.
“How do you like it?”
===Chinese===
格罗夫伯里（Grovebury）的这所女子学院，在能干的女校长伯德小姐（Miss Burd）治下，早就在左近一带闯出了不小的名头。校董们眼看学院日益超出旧校舍的容量，决定另建新舍，便在修道院（the Abbey）附近一处地段上佳的地方盖起了一栋气派的楼房。谁知屋顶上最后一片瓦刚刚铺好，战争就爆发了，政府当即征用这所新校充当征兵办事处，一直占用到停战以后。
姑娘们被迫挤在旧学院里熬了这么久，人人都觉得冤屈透顶。新楼的奠基石可是玛丽王后（Queen Mary）亲手奠定的，她们心想，政府办事满可以另择地点，何苦偏偏占着她们名正言顺的新校舍不放。不过万事终有尽头，连政府机关的公文旅行和拖拖沓沓也不例外——到秋季学期开学时，搬迁总算完成，新学院的开学典礼也筹备妥当。这自然是个非同小可的大日子：格罗夫伯里选区的议员、市长，还有许多头面人物都将莅临，更不必说家长和来宾了。学生们聚在刚粉刷一新的更衣室里，兴奋地互相招呼着。
“你觉得这儿怎么样？”

===Original===
“Oh, it’s topping!”
“Beats the old place hollow!”
“There’s room to turn around here!”
“And the lockers are just A1.”
“Have you seen the classrooms?”
===Chinese===
“哦，棒极了！”
“把老地方甩出十万八千里！”
“这地方宽敞得能转开身子！”
“储物柜也一等一地好。”
“教室你去看过没有？”

===Original===
“Not yet.”
“The gym’s utterly perfect!”
“And so is the lab.”
“Shame we’ve had to wait for it so long!”
“Never mind, we’ve got into it at last!”
===Chinese===
“还没呢。”
“体育馆简直十全十美！”
“实验室也一样。”
“可惜等了这么久才用上！”
“没事儿，总算搬进来啦！”

===Original===
Among the numbers of girls in the capacious dressing-rooms, Ingred also hung up her hat and coat, and passed on into the long corridor. Like the others she was excited, interested, even a little bewildered at the unfamiliar surroundings. It seemed extraordinary not to know her way about, and she seized joyfully upon Nora Clifford, who by virtue of ten minutes’ experience could act cicerone.
“We’re to be in **Va**,” Nora assured her. “All our old set, that is, except Connie Lord and Gladys Roper and Meg Mason. I’ve just met Miss Strong, and she told me. She’s moved up with us, and there’s a new mistress for **Vb**. Haven’t seen her yet, but they say she’s nice, though I’d rather stick to Miss Strong, wouldn’t you?”
“I don’t know,” temporized Ingred, screwing her mouth into a button.
“Oh, of course! I forgot! You’re not a ‘Strong’ enthusiast﻿—never were! Now *I* like her!”
“It’s easy enough to like anybody who favors you. Miss Strong was always down on me somehow, and I’d rather have tried my luck with a fresh teacher. I wonder if Miss Burd would put me in **Vb** if I asked her.”
“Of course she wouldn’t! Don’t be a silly idiot! I think Miss Strong’s absolutely adorable. Don’t you like the decorations in the corridor? Miss Godwin and some of the School of Art students did them. But just wait till you’ve seen the lecture-hall! Here we are! Now then, what d’you say to this?”
===Chinese===
宽敞的更衣室里女生成群，英格丽德（Ingred）也挂好帽子和外套，随着人流走进长长的走廊。她和旁人一样又兴奋又好奇，面对陌生的环境甚至有点发懵。连路都不认得，这滋味实在新鲜，她欢天喜地地一把拉住诺拉·克利福德（Nora Clifford）——人家凭十分钟的老资历，尽可充当向导了。
“我们要分在**五甲班**（Va），”诺拉言之凿凿，“还是原先那一帮人，只是康妮·洛德（Connie Lord）、格拉迪斯·罗珀（Gladys Roper）和梅格·梅森（Meg Mason）不在。我刚碰到斯特朗小姐（Miss Strong），是她告诉我的。她跟着我们一道升了上来，**五乙班**（Vb）则换了位新老师。我还没见过，据说人不错——不过我还是宁愿跟着斯特朗小姐，你呢？”
“我可说不好。”英格丽德不置可否，嘴巴抿得像颗纽扣。
“哦，对了！我忘了！你本来就不待见斯特朗小姐——从来就没待见过！*我*可是喜欢她的！”
“喜欢偏袒自己的人，谁不会呢。斯特朗小姐不知怎么老是跟我过不去，我倒想换个新老师碰碰运气。不知道我去求伯德小姐，她肯不肯把我调到**五乙班**去。”
“她当然不肯！别犯傻了！我觉得斯特朗小姐可爱极了。走廊里的装饰你喜欢吗？是戈德温小姐（Miss Godwin）带几个美术学校的学生弄的。先别忙，等你见过大讲堂再说！这不就到了！喏，你觉得这个如何？”

===Original===
The big room into which Nora ushered her companion was lighted from the top, and the walls, distempered in buff, had been decorated with stencils of Egyptian designs, the bright barbaric colors of which gave a very striking effect. There was a platform at the far end, where were placed rows of chairs for the distinguished visitors, and also pots of palms and ferns and geraniums to add an air of festivity to the opening ceremony. The long lines of benches in the body of the hall were already beginning to fill with girls, their bright hair-ribbons looking almost like a further array of flowers. Mistresses here and there were ushering them to their places, the Kindergarten children to the front seats, Juniors to the middle, and Seniors to the rear. Ingred and Nora, motioned by Miss Giles to a bench about three-quarters down the room, took their seats and talked quietly with their nearest neighbors. A general buzz of conversation, constantly restrained by mistresses, kept rising and then falling again to subdued whispers. In a short time the hall was full, Miss Perry had opened the piano, and the choir leaders had ranged themselves round her. In dead silence all the girls, big and little, turned their eyes towards the platform. The door behind the row of palms and ferns was opening, and Miss Burd, in scholastic cap and gown, was ushering in the Mayor, the Mayoress, several Town Councilors and their wives, a few clergy, the headmaster of the School of Art, and, to the place of honor in the middle, Sir James Hilton, the Member of Parliament for Grovebury, who was to conduct the ceremony of the afternoon. He was a pleasant, genial-looking man, and though, as he assured his audience, he had never before had the opportunity of addressing a room full of girls, he seemed to be able to rise to the occasion, and made quite a capital speech.
“You’re lucky to have this handsome building in which to do your lessons,” he concluded. “Our environment makes a great difference to us, and I think it is far easier to turn out good work in the midst of beautiful surroundings. Grovebury College has reaped a well-deserved reputation in the past, and I trust that its hitherto excellent standards will be maintained or even surpassed in the future. As member for the town there’s a special word I wish to say to you. Train yourselves to be good women citizens. Some day, when you’re grown up, you will have votes, and in that way assist in the self-government of this great nation. The better educated and the more enlightened you are, the better fitted you will be for your civic responsibility. Every girl who does her duty at school is helping her country, because she is making herself efficient to serve it in some capacity. At present England stands at a great crisis; if we are to keep up the traditions of our forefathers we want workers, not slackers, in every department of life. Even the smallest of those little girls sitting in the front row can do her bit. As for you elder girls, think of yourselves as a Cadet Corps, training for the service of the British Empire, and let every lesson you learn be not for your own advantage, but for the good you can do with it afterwards to the world. I have very great pleasure in declaring this new building open.”
After Sir James had sat down, the Mayor and several other people made short speeches, and when all the clapping had finally subsided, the piano struck up, and the school sang an Empire Song and the National Anthem. Then the door at the back of the platform opened again for the exit of the visitors, who, chatting among themselves, made their way to Miss Burd’s study to be hospitably entertained with tea and cakes. The whole ceremony had barely occupied an hour, and it was not yet four o’clock. The girls, in orderly files, marched from the lecture-hall, and betook themselves first to their new form-rooms, where textbooks were given out with preparation for the next day, and desks allotted; then, when the great bell rang for dismissal, to the playground and cloakrooms, en route for home.
===Chinese===
诺拉领着同伴走进的大厅从顶部采光，四壁刷着浅黄色的墙粉，上面用漏花模板印着埃及风格的图案，色彩浓艳粗犷，效果十分醒目。大厅尽头是一座平台，上面摆着一排排留给贵宾的座椅，还放着盆栽的棕榈、蕨草和天竺葵，给开学典礼平添几分喜庆。厅堂里一长排一长排的长凳上已渐渐坐满了女生，她们鲜亮的发带远远望去，几乎像又添了一片花丛。女教师们分立各处引她们入座：幼儿班的孩子坐前排，低年级坐中段，高年级靠后坐。英格丽德和诺拉由贾尔斯小姐（Miss Giles）指点，在离台约四分之三深处的一条长凳上落了座，同近旁的同学轻声攀谈起来。满厅嗡嗡的谈话声此起彼伏，不时被女教师们压下去，又落成低低的耳语。不多时大厅已座无虚席，佩里小姐（Miss Perry）掀开琴盖，领唱的学生在她身旁一字排开。全场鸦雀无声，大大小小的姑娘都把目光投向平台。那排棕榈和蕨草后面的门开了，伯德小姐头戴方帽、身着学袍，迎进市长与市长夫人、几位市议员及他们的太太、几位牧师、美术学校校长，并把格罗夫伯里选区的议员詹姆斯·希尔顿爵士（Sir James Hilton）——今天下午典礼的主持者——请到正中的荣誉席上。爵士一望便知是个和气可亲的人；他向听众坦言，自己此前从没有机会对满屋子的姑娘演讲，但他显然应付裕如，讲得相当出彩。
“你们能在这样一栋漂亮的楼里念书，真是幸运，”他总结道，“环境对人的影响非同小可，我想，置身优美的环境中，做出出色的成绩要容易得多。格罗夫伯里学院在过去赢得了当之无愧的声誉，我相信，它素来的优良水准今后定能保持，甚至更上层楼。作为本镇选出的议员，我有一句特别的话要送给大家：要把自己锻炼成良好的女公民。有朝一日你们长大成人，将拥有选票，以此参与这个伟大国家的自治。你们受的教育越好、见识越开明，就越能担得起公民的责任。每个在学校里恪尽本分的女生，都是在为她的国家出力，因为她正在把自己磨炼成日后能以某种身份为国效命的人。眼下英国正处于重大关头；要想守住先辈的传统，各行各业都需要实干家，而不是偷懒的人。就连前排座位上顶小的那些小姑娘，也能尽上自己的一份力。至于你们高年级的同学，不妨把自己看作一支学员军团，为效力大英帝国而受训；要让你们学到的每一课，不只是为自己的好处，而是为着日后能用它给世界带来好处。我怀着莫大的荣幸宣布：这栋新楼正式启用。”
詹姆斯爵士落座后，市长和另外几位来宾也做了简短的发言，等掌声终于平息，钢琴奏响，全校齐唱了一支帝国颂歌和国歌。随后平台后方的门再度打开，送贵宾退场；他们一路谈笑，前往伯德小姐的书房，受用殷勤款待的茶和糕点。整场典礼用了不到一个钟头，此时还不到四点。女生们排着整整齐齐的队伍走出大讲堂，先到各自的新教室，领了课本，布置了第二天的预习，分了课桌；然后，放学的大钟一响，便涌向操场和衣帽间，各自踏上回家的路。

===Original===
Ingred, with a goodly pile of fresh literature under her arm, walked slowly downstairs. She was not in any hurry to leave the classroom, and lingered as long as the limits of Miss Strong’s patience lasted. She knew there was a certain ordeal to be faced with her form-mates, and she was not sure whether she wanted to put it off, or to get it over at once.
“Better let them know and have done with it,” she said to herself after a few moments’ consideration on the landing. “After all, it’s my business, not theirs!”
It was a rather airily-defiant Ingred who strolled into the cloakroom and put on her hat. Francie Hall, trying to thread her boot with a lace that had lost its tag, looked up, smiled, and made room for her on the form.
“Cheery-ho, Ingred! How do you like our new diggings? Some removal, this, isn’t it? I must say the place looks nice. It’s topping to be here at last. By the by, I suppose you’ll be getting in Rotherwood soon? Or have you got already?”
Ingred was stooping to lace her shoe, so perhaps the position accounted for her stifled voice.
===Chinese===
英格丽德胳膊底下夹着可观的一摞新课本，慢慢走下楼梯。她并不急着离开教室，在斯特朗小姐耐心许可的限度内一直磨蹭到最后一刻。她知道有一道难关迟早要当着同班同学的面过，却拿不定主意，是拖着好，还是干脆一口气了结。
“还是让她们知道算了，一了百了。”她在楼梯平台上盘算片刻，对自己说，“说到底，这是我的事，跟她们什么相干！”
走进衣帽间、戴上帽子的，是一副满不在乎、略带挑衅神气的英格丽德。弗朗茜·霍尔（Francie Hall）正费劲地往靴眼里穿一根掉了箍头的鞋带，闻声抬头笑了笑，在长凳上给她让出一块地方。
“嘿喽，英格丽德！咱们这新窝你觉得如何？这回搬家够气派的吧？我得说，这地方真不赖，总算搬进来了，太好啦。对了，你们眼看就要搬进罗瑟伍德（Rotherwood）了吧？还是已经住进去了？”
英格丽德正弯腰系鞋带，声音发闷，也许得怪这个姿势。

===Original===
“We’re not going back there.”
“Not going back!” Francie’s tone was one of genuine amazement. “Why, but you said it was being done up for you, and you’d be moving before the term started!”
“Well, we’re not, at any rate.”
“What a disappointment for you!” began Beatrice Jackson tactlessly, as several other girls who were standing near turned and joined the group. “You always said you were just longing for Rotherwood.”
“Do the Red Cross want it again?” queried Jess Howard.
“No, they don’t; but we’re not going to live there. Where are we going to live? At our bungalow on the moors, and I’m a weekly boarder at the hostel. Are there any other impertinent questions you’d like to ask? Don’t all speak at once, please!”
===Chinese===
“我们不回那儿去了。”
“不回去了！”弗朗茜的语气里是实实在在的惊讶，“咦，可你不是说那儿正替你们整修一新，开学前就能搬进去吗！”
“反正我们是不搬了。”
“那你多失望呀！”碧翠丝·杰克逊（Beatrice Jackson）口无遮拦地开了腔，旁边站着的几个女生闻声转过身，围拢过来，“你以前总说，巴不得早点住进罗瑟伍德呢。”
“红十字会又要征那房子了？”杰丝·霍华德（Jess Howard）问。
“不，没有；但我们不住那儿了。我们要住哪儿？住荒原上我们家的小平房，我呢，就在寄宿舍当周寄宿生。诸位还有什么放肆的问题要问吗？请别一齐开口！”

===Original===
And Ingred, having laced both shoes, got up, seized her pile of books, and, turning her back on her form-mates, stalked away without a goodbye. She knew she had been rude and ungracious, but she felt that if she had stopped another moment the tears that were welling into her eyes would have overflowed. Ingred had many good points, but she was a remarkably proud girl. She could not bear her schoolfellows to think she had come down in the world. She had thrown out so many hints last term about the renewed glories of Rotherwood, that it was certainly humiliating to have to acknowledge that all the happy expectations had come to nothing. On the reputation of Rotherwood both she and Quenrede had held their heads high in the school; she wondered if her position would be the same, now that everybody knew the truth.
As a matter of fact, most of the girls giggled as she went out through the cloakroom door.
“My lady’s in a temper!” exclaimed Francie.
“Lemons and vinegar!” hinnied Jess.
“Why did she fly out like that?” asked Beatrice.
===Chinese===
英格丽德系好两只鞋的鞋带，站起身来，抱起那摞书，背对同班同学扬长而去，一声再见也没说。她知道自己失礼又难看，可她觉得，只要再多待一刻，涌上眼眶的泪水就要决堤。英格丽德有许多优点，却是个心气极高的姑娘。她受不了让同学们觉得自己家道中落。上学期她放了那么多话，吹嘘罗瑟伍德修葺之后何等风光，如今不得不承认，那些美好的指望全都落了空，实在难堪。借着罗瑟伍德的名头，她和奎恩丽德（Quenrede）在学校里一向昂首挺胸；如今人人都知道了底细，她不知道自己的地位还能不能保得住。
事实上，她走出衣帽间大门的时候，大多数女生都在吃吃地笑。
“大小姐发脾气啦！”弗朗茜嚷道。
“柠檬拌醋，酸气冲天！”杰丝嘶着嗓子怪笑。
“她干吗发这么大的火？”碧翠丝问。

===Original===
“Well, really, Beatrice Jackson, after all the stupid things you said, anybody would fly out, I should think,” commented Verity Richmond. “I’m sorry for Ingred. I’d heard the Saxons can’t go back to their old house. It’s hard luck on them after lending it all these years to the Red Cross.”
“But *why* aren’t they going back?”
“Why, silly, because they can’t keep it up, I suppose. If you’ve any sense, you won’t mention Rotherwood to Ingred again. It’s evidently a sore point. Don’t for goodness sake, go rubbing it into her.”
“I wasn’t going to!” grumbled Beatrice. “Surely I can make an innocent remark without you beginning to preach to me like this! I call it cheek!”
Verity did not reply. She had had too many squabbles with Beatrice in the past to want to begin a fresh campaign on the first day of a new term. She discreetly pretended not to hear, and addressing Francie Hall, launched into an account of her doings during the holidays.
===Chinese===
“我说，碧翠丝·杰克逊，你说了那么多蠢话，换了谁不火冒三丈才怪，”薇丽蒂·里士满（Verity Richmond）评论道，“我倒替英格丽德难过。听说萨克森一家（the Saxons）回不了老宅了。把房子借给红十字会用了这么多年，末了落到这步田地，也真够倒霉的。”
“可他们*为什么*不回去呀？”
“还能为什么，傻瓜，八成是那房子养不起了呗。你要是识相，往后就别再跟英格丽德提罗瑟伍德。明摆着那是人家的痛处，看在老天爷份上，千万别去戳。”
“我本来也没打算提！”碧翠丝嘟囔道，“难道我随口说句话都不成，非得听你这样训人不可！你这才是岂有此理！”
薇丽蒂没有接茬。她和碧翠丝过去拌嘴拌得够多了，犯不着在新学期头一天就开新一回合。她识趣地装作没听见，转头对弗朗茜·霍尔滔滔不绝地讲起假期里的种种经历。

===Original===
“We’re moving out to Repworth at the September quarter,” she concluded. “And it’s too far for me to bicycle in to school every day, so I’ve started as a boarder at the hostel. I shall go home for weekends, though. Nora Clifford and Fil Trevor are there too. They’ll be glad Ingred’s come. With four of us out of one form, things ought to be rather jinky. Hullo, here they are! I say, girls, let’s go to our diggings.”
The two girls who came strolling up arm-in-arm were the most absolute contrast. Nora was large-limbed, plump, rosy, with short-cut hair, a lively manner, and any amount of confidence. Without being exactly pretty, she gave a general impression of jolly, healthy girlhood, and reminded one of an old-fashioned, sweet-scented cabbage rose that had just burst into bloom. Dainty little Filomena might, on the other hand, be described as the most delicate of tea roses. She was fair to a fault, a lily-white maid with the silkiest of flaxen tresses. Her pale-blue eyes, with their light lashes, and rather colorless little face with its straight features were of the petite fairy type. You felt instinctively that, like a Dresden china vase, she was made more for ornament than for use, and nobody﻿—even schoolmistresses﻿—expected too much from her. Experience had shown them that they did not get it.
For two years, ever since her mother’s death, Fil had been a boarder at the College, and because at first she had been such a pathetic little figure in her deep mourning, the girls had petted her, and had continued an indulgent attitude long after the black dress had been exchanged for colors. If Fil had rather got into the habit of posing as the mascot of the form, she certainly deserved some consideration, for she was a dear little thing, with a very sweet temper, and never made any of the ill-natured remarks that some of the other girls flung about like missiles. She was so manifestly unfitted to take her own part that somebody else invariably took it for her.
Verity Richmond, who, with Nora, Filomena and Ingred, represented **Va** in the hostel, was a brisk, up-to-date, go-ahead girl, full of fun and high spirits. She was a capital mimic, and had a turn for repartee that, quite good-naturedly, laid any adversary flat in the dust. If Nora and Fil were like rose and lily, she was decidedly the robin of the party. Her fair complexion seemed to add force to the brightness of her twinkling brown eyes, and her general restlessness and quick alert ways made one think of a bird always hopping about. Though not quite such a romp as Nora, she was ready for any fun that was going, and intended to get as much enjoyment as possible out of the coming term. She linked herself now on to Fil’s disengaged arm, taking the latter’s pile of books with her own and began towing her two friends in the direction of the hostel.
===Chinese===
“九月季度一到，我们就搬到雷普沃思（Repworth）去，”她最后说道，“从那儿每天骑车上学太远，所以我开始在寄宿舍住宿了，不过周末还是回家。诺拉·克利福德和菲尔·特雷弗（Fil Trevor）也住在那儿。英格丽德来了，她们准高兴。咱们一个班出了四个人，往后保管热闹。嘿，正说她们呢，人来啦！喂，姑娘们，上咱们的窝去吧。”
挽着胳膊踱过来的这两个姑娘，恰成最彻底的对照。诺拉四肢舒展、丰腴红润，剪着短发，举止活泼，浑身是使不完的自信。她虽说算不上貌美，整个人却透出一股健康快活的少女气象，让人想起一朵刚刚绽开的老式包心玫瑰，香馥馥的。而娇小玲珑的菲洛梅娜（Filomena）呢，则不妨说是茶香月季里最娇嫩的一朵。她白皙得几乎过了头，是个百合般洁白的姑娘，一头亚麻色长发柔滑如丝。淡蓝色的眼睛衬着浅色的睫毛，五官端正的小脸略显苍白，正是那种小巧的精灵类型。你会本能地觉得，她就像一只德累斯顿瓷花瓶，中看更甚于中用，谁——连女教师们也算上——都不对她指望太高。经验早已告诉他们，指望了也是白指望。
自从母亲去世，菲尔在学院寄宿已经两年。起初她一身重孝，小小的人儿那副可怜模样，惹得姑娘们个个疼她；即便黑丧服早已换成花衣裳，这份宠纵仍延续了许久。要说菲尔已经颇为习惯以班里的福星自居，她也确实当得起几分体恤，因为她实在是个可人儿，性子温柔得很，从不口出恶言——别的女生里，可是有人把刻薄话当飞镖四处乱掷的。她那副明显护不住自己的样子，总免不了有旁人挺身而出替她护。
薇丽蒂·里士满同诺拉、菲洛梅娜和英格丽德一道，是**五甲班**住在寄宿舍里的代表。她是个爽利、新潮、敢作敢为的姑娘，满肚子风趣和精气神。她模仿谁像谁，又天生一张伶牙俐齿，好性儿地三言两语，就能把对手驳得体无完肤。若说诺拉和菲尔一个如玫瑰、一个似百合，她分明就是这一伙里的知更鸟。白皙的面色衬得那双滴溜溜转的褐色眼睛愈发明亮，她那坐不住的性子和眼观六路的敏捷做派，让人想起一只总在蹦蹦跳跳的小鸟。她虽不及诺拉那般疯玩，但凡有热闹从不落下，打定主意要把新学期的乐趣榨到最后一滴。这会儿她挽住菲尔空着的那条胳膊，把菲尔那摞书并到自己手上，拖着两位朋友朝寄宿舍走去。

===Original===
“I’ve hardly had time even for a squint at our dormitory yet,” she announced. “Mrs. Best said I was late, and made me pop down my bag and fly; but she told me we were all four together, so I went off with an easy mind. I’d been worrying for fear I’d be boxed up with some kids, or sandwiched in among the Sixth. I told you Ingred was to be with us, didn’t I? Let’s go and hunt her out; she’ll have wiped her eyes and got over her jim-jams by now. We’ll have time to do some unpacking before tea, if they’ve carried up our boxes.”
The hostel was a separate house, built at the opposite side of the school playground. It could accommodate thirty girls, and twenty-six were already entered on its register. After a brief peep into the attractive dining-hall, and an equally pleasant-looking boarders’ sitting-room, the three girls went upstairs to a dormitory marked 2. They found Ingred already at work on her task of unpacking, putting clothes away in drawers, and spreading the shelf that served as a dressing-table with an assortment of photos, books, and toilet requisites. She looked rather in the dumps, but it was impossible for anybody to remain gloomy when in the presence of such lively spirits as Nora and Verity, and by the time the gong sounded for tea she had cheered up, and was sitting on her bed discussing school news.
“Look here!” said Verity. “If we want to have a jolly term we four must stick together. Let’s make a compact that, both in school and in the hostel, we’ll support each other through thick and thin. We’ll be a sort of society of Freemasons. I haven’t made up any secrets yet, but whoever betrays them will be outlawed! Let’s call ourselves ‘The Foursome League.’ Now then, put your right hands all together on mine, and say after me: ‘I hereby promise and vow on my honor as a gentlewoman that I’ll stand by my chums in No. 2 Dormitory at any cost.’ That’s a good beginning. When we’ve time, we’ll draw up the rules. Subscriptions? Oh, bother! You can each give sixpence if you like, and we’ll spend the money on a chocolate feast. Remember, Fil, not a word to anybody! It’s to be kept absolutely quiet. There’s the gong. If the tea’s up to the standard of the rest of the hostel, I shan’t object. Glad we’re not rationed now, for I’m as hungry as a hunter.”
===Chinese===
“咱们的寝室我连瞥一眼的工夫都还没捞着，”她宣布道，“贝斯特太太（Mrs. Best）说我到得晚，撂下提包就把我打发走了；不过她说了，咱们四个住一间，我这才安下心来。我先前直发愁，怕跟一群小娃娃关作一堆，或是被塞在六年级中间当夹心。英格丽德要跟咱们一起，这事我早说过了，对吧？走，找她去；这会儿她该擦干眼泪，缓过那股别扭劲儿了。要是箱子都抬上楼了，喝茶前还来得及拆几件行李。”
寄宿舍是操场对面一座独立的房子，可容纳三十名女生，名册上已经登记了二十六个。三个姑娘对着引人喜爱的餐厅和同样清爽可人的寄宿生起居室匆匆一瞥，便上楼进了二号寝室。英格丽德已经在埋头拆行李：衣服收进抽屉，又把照片、书本和一应梳洗用品摆满充作梳妆台的搁板。她看上去蔫蔫的，可在诺拉和薇丽蒂这般生龙活虎的人跟前，谁也阴沉不起来；待开茶点的锣声敲响，她已经缓过劲来，坐在床上跟大家聊起学校的新鲜事了。
“听我说！”薇丽蒂道，“咱们要想过个痛痛快快的学期，四个人就得抱成团。咱们来立个盟约：不论在学校里还是在寄宿舍里，都要同甘共苦、互为后盾。咱们就算是个共济会式的小团体。秘密我还没编出来呢，不过谁敢走漏，就逐出盟外！咱们就叫‘四人同盟’（The Foursome League）。来，把你们的右手都叠到我手上，跟我念：‘我以淑女的名誉起誓立愿：无论付出什么代价，都要力保二号寝室的伙伴。’这开头不错。等有了空，咱们再拟章程。会费？哎呀，麻烦！愿意的话每人交六便士，这笔钱拿去办一场巧克力盛宴。记住了，菲尔，对谁也不许走漏半个字！这事要绝对保密。锣响了。要是茶点配得上这寄宿舍其余的水准，我就没什么可挑剔的。幸好如今不限量配给了，我可真是饿得前胸贴后背。”

## XIII Quenrede Comes Out / 第十三章 奎恩丽德初入社交界

===Original===
The Saxon family celebrated Christmas at the bungalow with mixed feelings. As Ingred said, it was like the curate’s egg﻿—parts of it were very nice. It was the first Christmas they had spent all together for many years, and if they could only have forgotten Rotherwood, and their altered circumstances, they would have enjoyed it immensely. Mrs. Saxon, the unfailing sunshine-radiator of the household, tried to ignore the tone of discontent in her husband’s voice, the grumpy attitude of Egbert, Quenrede’s fit of the blues, and Athelstane’s rather martyred pose. She insisted on bundling everybody out for a blow on the moors.
“If we’d been living in Grovebury,” she remarked, “we should probably have taken a jaunt to Wynch-on-the-Wold as a special treat. Let us think ourselves lucky in being on the spot and only having to turn out of our own door to be at once in such lovely scenery. It’s like having a country holiday at Christmas instead of midsummer﻿—a thing I always hankered after and never got before!”
Certainly winter on the wold held a charm of its own. The great waste of brown moor stretching under the gray sky showed rich patches where yellow grass and rushes edged dark boggy pools, the low-growing stems of sallows and alders were delicate with shades of orange and mauve; here and there a sprig of furze lingered in flower, and black flights of starlings and fieldfares, driven from colder climates in quest of food, swept in long lines across the horizon. The weather was open for the time of year, the wind strong but not too keen, and had it not been for the lowness of the sun in the sky the day might have been autumn instead of December. It was glorious to walk to the top of Wetherstone Heights and see, miles away, the spire of Monkswell Church and the gleam of the distant river, then to hurry back in the gloaming with the rising mists creeping up like advancing specters, and to find the lamps lighted and tea ready in the cheery bungalow. Nobody wanted to quarrel with Yule cake and muffins, and even Mr. Saxon temporarily forgot his worries and relapsed into quite amusing reminiscences of certain adventures in France.
If only our spirits would keep up to the point to which, with much effort, we screw them, all would be well: unfortunately they often have a tiresome knack of descending with a run. When tea was finished and cleared away Mr. Saxon found the presence of his family a hindrance to reading, and at a hint from their mother the younger members of the party took themselves off into the little drawing-room. Here, round a black fire, which, despite Hereward’s poking, refused to burn brightly, the grumble-cloud that had been lowering all day burst at last.
===Chinese===
萨克森（Saxon）一家在平房小屋里过圣诞节，心中百味杂陈。用英格丽德（Ingred）的话说，这个节就像副牧师的那枚鸡蛋——有些部分还是顶好的。这是多年来他们头一回全家团聚过节，只要能忘掉罗瑟伍德（Rotherwood）、忘掉今非昔比的境况，本可以过得快活极了。萨克森太太是家里那台永不熄火的阳光辐射器，竭力不去理会丈夫话音里的不满、埃格伯特（Egbert）的闷闷不乐、奎恩丽德（Quenrede）的低落忧郁，还有阿特尔斯坦（Athelstane）那副受难者的做派，硬是把一家人统统轰出门，到荒原上吹风去。
“要是我们还住在格罗夫伯里（Grovebury），”她说，“这会儿八成把去沃尔德畔温奇（Wynch-on-the-Wold）逛上一趟当作特别的犒赏呢。咱们就往好里想吧：住在这儿是福气，一出自家门便是这般美景。这就像把乡间假日从仲夏挪到了圣诞节——我一向心心念念、却从来没盼着过的事！”
荒原上的冬天自有一番迷人处。灰蒙蒙的天穹下，大片褐色的旷野铺展出去，黄草和灯心草镶着幽暗的泥沼水洼，洇出斑驳的暖色；矮丛的柳树和桤木，枝条上染着橙红与藕紫，细腻可喜；东一簇西一簇的荆豆还零星开着花；成群的黑压压的椋鸟和田鸫为了觅食从更冷的地方涌来，排成长阵掠过天际。就这个时节来说，天气算是暖和的，风虽猛，倒不算刺骨，若不是太阳挂得这样低，简直要叫人当作秋天，而非十二月。登上韦瑟斯通高地（Wetherstone Heights）之巅，望见几英里外芒克斯韦尔教堂（Monkswell Church）的尖顶和远处河流的粼粼微光，实在痛快；再趁暮色匆匆赶回，身后升腾的雾气像步步进逼的幽灵一般漫上来，到家时小屋里灯火已亮、茶点已备，更是暖心。圣诞果糕和热松饼端上来，谁也不忍跟它们过不去，连萨克森先生也一时忘了烦恼，絮絮讲起在法国的几段奇遇，竟也妙趣横生。
只可惜人的精气神儿费了老大力气拧上去之后，未必守得住——它们偏有种恼人的本事，说塌就一泻到底。茶点用过、收拾停当，萨克森先生便嫌一屋子家人碍着他读书；母亲递了个眼色，小的几个便挪进小客厅去了。围着那炉烧不旺的黑炭火——任凭赫里沃德（Hereward）怎么捅，它也不肯烧亮——郁积了一整天的怨气之云，到底炸开了。

===Original===
“If we’d only got the Rotherwood billiard table there’d be something to do!” groused Egbert gloomily.
“There isn’t a corner in this poky hole where a fellow can fiddle with photography,” chimed in Athelstane, “even if there was time to do it. When I get back from Birkshaw it’s nothing but grind, grind, grind at medical books all the evening.”
“Rather have your job than mine, though,” said Egbert. “You haven’t to sit under the Pater’s eye all day long, and have him down on you like a cartload of bricks if you make the slightest slip. I’m the worst off of the whole lot of us!”
“What about me at that odious Grammar School?” asked Hereward, pressing his claims to the palm of dissatisfaction.
“Or me at the hostel!” urged Ingred, not to be outdone.
“I don’t think you, any of you, realize how slow it is just to stop at home!” sighed Quenrede. “There were sixteen dozen things I’d made up my mind to do, and I can’t do one of them. It’s going to be a hateful New Year for all of us﻿—just a New Year of going without and scraping and saving and economizing﻿—ugh! What a life!”
===Chinese===
“要是把罗瑟伍德的台球桌带来了，好歹也有点事干！”埃格伯特阴郁地抱怨。
“这个憋屈的小破屋里，连个能摆弄照相的角落都没有，”阿特尔斯坦插话，“就算有工夫摆弄也白搭。我从伯克肖（Birkshaw）回来，一晚上就只有啃啊啃啊啃那些医学书。”
“不过我宁可要你的差事，”埃格伯特说，“你不用整天坐在老爸的眼皮子底下，稍微出一点岔子，他就劈头盖脸像一车砖似的砸下来。咱们几个里头数我最惨！”
“那我在那所讨厌的文法学校（Grammar School）又怎么说？”赫里沃德也来争这顶“最委屈”的桂冠。
“还有我在寄宿舍呢！”英格丽德也不肯服输。
“我看你们谁也不明白，光是待在家里有多难熬！”奎恩丽德叹道，“我原本铁了心要做十六打那么多的事，如今一件也做不成。咱们的新年注定招人嫌——整个一个缺这少那、抠抠搜搜、省吃俭用的新年——呸！这叫什么日子！”

===Original===
“Life’s mostly what we make it,” said Mother, who had quietly joined the circle. “After all, what we think we want doesn’t always give the greatest happiness. Suppose each of us tries to let this be the best year we’ve ever had? Very little in the way of material wealth may come to us, but the other kind of wealth is far better worth working for. I think this hard time gives us the chance to show what we’re made of. During the fighting, the lads at the front went steadily through severe privations, and the women at home worked in the same brave, cheery fashion. Now the strain of the war is over, are we going to let all this splendid spirit drop? Suppose we fight our own battles as we fought our country’s? Let me feel I’ve still got a family of soldiers to be proud of.”
“You’re the Colonel, then, of the new corps,” said Egbert, with an affectionate bear-hug to the slight figure that was already making the black fire break into a blaze. “You’ve pluck enough for the whole clan, little Mother o’ mine! You shall sound your slogan and lead the attack on Fate till we get back to Rotherwood! There!”
“I’m aiming at higher things than Rotherwood, darling boy!” said his mother gravely.
“*I* know!” whispered Quenrede, squeezing the dear hand that reached out and clasped her own. “I won’t be a selfish beast any more. I won’t indeed. Economizing shall be my New Year’s cross!”
===Chinese===
“日子多半是自己过出来的，”母亲不知几时悄悄坐进了圈里，“说到底，我们自以为想要的东西，未必带来最大的幸福。不如咱们每个人都试着把这一年过成有生以来最好的一年？物质上的富足兴许轮不着咱们，可另一种财富，更值得下力气去挣。我想，这场苦日子正给了咱们机会，看看自己究竟是什么材料做的。打仗的时候，前线的小伙子们硬是稳稳当当扛过了一个个难关，后方的女人们也同样勇敢乐观地劳作。如今战争吃紧的日子过去了，难道反倒让这股子精气神散掉不成？不如拿出打国仗的劲头，来打自己的仗？让我觉得，我手底下还有一屋子值得骄傲的兵。”
“那您就是这支新军团的上校喽。”埃格伯特说着，深情地把那个瘦小的身影搂进怀里——她正俯身拨火，把那半死不活的黑炉火拨得腾起了焰。“咱们全族的勇气您一个人就绰绰有余，我的小妈妈！您只管吹响号令，带领我们向命运开战，直到打回罗瑟伍德！就这么定了！”
“我瞄准的可是比罗瑟伍德更高的目标，好孩子！”母亲正色道。
“我可明白！”奎恩丽德轻声说，紧紧攥住那只伸过来握住她的、亲爱的手，“我再也不当自私鬼了，真的不当了。省吃俭用，就是我新年里背的十字架！”

===Original===
“If we’re going to count up crosses,” proclaimed Athelstane humorously, “the orphan’s fine voice while I’m studying is mine!”
“But *she* probably counts it her choicest blessing!” exclaimed Ingred.
And then the whole family broke out laughing, and Mother’s little lecture ended in fun. It made its impression upon individual members all the same.
===Chinese===
“要是大家都来数十字架，”阿特尔斯坦煞有介事地宣布，“我背的那份，就是“孤儿”（The Orphan）的那副好嗓子——专挑我用功的时候唱！”
“可人家没准儿还把那副嗓子当成头一等的福气呢！”英格丽德叫道。
于是全家哄然大笑，母亲的小训话就在笑闹里收了场。话虽如此，它还是悄悄印在了各人心里。

===Original===
The six miles which separated the Saxons from Grovebury seemed to have set up an effectual barrier between them and the old world in which they had moved before. Many people who had been friendly in the Rotherwood days did not trouble to come so far as Wynch-on-the-Wold to pay calls, and the numerous invitations which had formerly been extended to the young folks decreased this Christmas to very few.
First and foremost amongst these scanty festivities came Mrs. Desmond’s dance. It was a grownup affair, and she had sent printed invitations to Egbert, Athelstane and Quenrede. The latter, who only knew the Desmonds slightly and was always overwhelmed in their presence, developed a sudden and acute fit of shyness and implored to be allowed to refuse.
“If it had been the Browns’ or Lawrences’ I’d have loved it,” she urged, “but you know, Mumsie, how Mrs. Desmond absolutely withers me up! I never can say six words when she’s there. I’d run five miles to avoid meeting her: you know I would! She’s so starchy.”
“You see very little of your hostess at a dance. Don’t be silly, Queenie!” insisted Mrs. Saxon. “I say you’re to go, so there’s an end of it.”
“I’ll go for an evening’s martyrdom, then, not for enjoyment!” wailed her daughter dolefully.
A first grownup dance is often a terrible ordeal to a girl of eighteen, and Quenrede, though she had put on a few airs to impress the schoolgirls at the Rainbow League sale, was at bottom woefully bashful. She was still in the stage when her newly-turned-up hair looked as if it were unaccustomed to be coiled round her head; she had a painful habit of blushing, and had not yet acquired that general savoir faire which comes to us with the passing of our teens. To be plunged for a whole evening into the society of a succession of strangers seemed to her anything but an exhilarating prospect.
===Chinese===
萨克森家与格罗夫伯里之间隔着的六英里路，仿佛在他们和从前活动的那个旧世界之间筑起了一道实实在在的屏障。许多在罗瑟伍德时代交情不错的人，懒得大老远跑到沃尔德畔温奇来串门；从前纷纷落到年轻人头上的邀请，这个圣诞节也骤减得寥寥无几。
这寥寥几场应酬里，头一份要紧的就是德斯蒙德太太（Mrs. Desmond）的舞会。那是大人们的场合，她给埃格伯特、阿特尔斯坦和奎恩丽德都送来了印好的请柬。奎恩丽德与德斯蒙德一家（the Desmonds）只有泛泛之交，在他们跟前总觉局促，这下忽然犯起一阵厉害的怯场，恳求母亲准她辞掉。
“要是布朗家（the Browns）或是劳伦斯家（the Lawrences）办的，我准乐意去，”她央告道，“可您知道的，妈咪，德斯蒙德太太简直能把我说蔫了！有她在场，我连六个字都吐不出来。为着躲她，叫我跑五英里都行：您知道我肯的！她那个人，古板得要命。”
“舞会上，你根本没多少工夫跟女主人打交道。别犯傻了，奎妮（Queenie）！”萨克森太太不容分说，“我说了你去，这事就这么定了。”
“那我去也是受一晚上的刑，不是去取乐的！”女儿凄凄惨惨地哀叹。
对十八岁的姑娘来说，头一场成人舞会往往是场可怕的酷刑。奎恩丽德虽然在彩虹联盟（The Rainbow League）的义卖会上为了唬住那帮女学生端过几分架子，骨子里却怕生得厉害。她还处在那样一个阶段：新盘起的头发，看上去还不习惯在她头上盘着似的；她有爱脸红的毛病，一红起来就没个完；也还没学会那套待人接物的从容老练——这是要过了十几岁才慢慢长到人身上的本事。要整整一晚上扎进一茬接一茬的陌生人堆里，在她看来，怎么也算不上一桩叫人振奋的前景。

===Original===
“If I could just dance with our own boys!” she sighed.
“I’d pity you if you did!” declared Ingred, pausing in an effort to make Athelstane’s steps more worthy of a ballroom. “Why, half the fun will be your different partners. I only wish I’d your chance and was ‘coming out’ too!”
“I’m sure you’re welcome to go instead of me,” proclaimed Quenrede petulantly.
All the same she watched the preparations for the event with considerable girlish interest. Mother, whose ambitions at first had run to a dress from town, regretfully decided that the family finances could only supply a homemade costume, and set to work with fashion book and sewing-machine to act amateur dressmaker, a thrilling experience to unaccustomed fingers, for paper patterns are sometimes difficult to understand, seams do not fit together as they ought, and the bottom hem of a skirt is the most awkward thing in the world to make hang perfectly straight. Quenrede, standing on the table, revolved slowly while Mrs. Saxon and Ingred stuck in pins and debated whether a quarter of an inch here and there should be raised or lowered. Ingred showed far more cleverness in sewing than her sister; her natty fingers could contrive pretty things already in the shape of collars and blouses.
===Chinese===
“要是能光跟咱们自家的男孩跳就好了！”她叹道。
“你要真那样，我才替你可惜呢！”英格丽德正停下来，一心想把阿特尔斯坦的舞步调教得勉强配得上舞厅。“嗬，一半的乐子就在舞伴各不相同呀。我巴不得有你这个机会，也来‘出道’一回呢！”
“你尽管替我去，欢迎还来不及呢。”奎恩丽德没好气地宣布。
话虽如此，她仍怀着十足的女孩子的好奇，盯着这场盛事的种种筹备。母亲起初一门心思想从城里买件礼服，无奈掂了掂家底，只供得起一件自制的，只得遗憾地翻开时装书、踩起缝纫机，当起了业余裁缝。对生手来说，这是趟险象环生的经历：纸样有时叫人看不懂，接缝对不到该对的地方，裙摆底边要缝得笔直垂顺，更是天底下头一桩难事。奎恩丽德站在桌子上缓缓打转，萨克森太太和英格丽德往她身上别大头针，商量着这儿该提四分之一英寸、那儿该落四分之一英寸。论针线，英格丽德比姐姐巧得多，那双灵巧的手已经能做出领子、罩衫这类俏丽物件了。

===Original===
“You’d make an admirable curate’s wife!” Quenrede laughingly assured her. “*I* shall have to marry a rich man and get my things from London.”
“It will probably be the other way,” declared Mother. “Stand still, Queenie, I can’t measure properly if you *will* dance about!”
Though she was ready with a joke, as a matter of fact Quenrede was having a severe struggle not to be snappy. For years and years she had planned her “coming out,” and she had decided upon a ball at Rotherwood, and an absolute creation of a gown that was to be sent for from Paris. There would have been some éclat then in emerging from the chrysalis stage of the schoolroom and becoming a butterfly of society. To make her first grownup appearance at Mrs. Desmond’s dance and in a homemade dress seemed not so much a “coming out” as an “oozing out.” There are degrees in butterflies, and she feared her appearance would resemble not the gorgeous “Red Admiral” or “Painted Lady,” but the “Common White Cabbage.” If it had not been for the New Year’s resolution, some traces of her disappointment would have leaked out, but she kept the secret bravely to herself. The family indeed knew she was not anxious to go, but set her unwilling attitude down to mere shyness. Her mother never guessed at the real reason.
There was a tremendous robing on the evening of January the ninth, with Mother and Ingred for lady’s-maids, and “The Orphan” hovering about, offering to bring pins or hot water on the chance of getting a peep at the proceedings. Mrs. Saxon stepped back, when all was complete, and viewed the result somewhat in the spirit of an artist who has finished a picture. It is an event in a mother’s life when her first little girl grows up and becomes a young lady. Tonight Quenrede was to be launched on the stream of society. Looked at critically, her appearance was very satisfactory. Though the new dress might not be up to the level of a fashion-plate, it certainly became her, and set off the pretty fair face, white neck, and coils of gleaming flaxen hair.
===Chinese===
“你将来准是位顶呱呱的副牧师太太！”奎恩丽德笑着打包票，“我嘛，只好嫁个有钱人，衣裳什物全上伦敦置办。”
“八成正好倒过来。”母亲断言，“站好，奎妮，你偏要这么扭来扭去，我可没法量准！”
她嘴上说笑，其实正狠命按捺着不让自己发作。多少年来，她把“出道”的事盘算了一遍又一遍：早已认定要在罗瑟伍德开一场舞会，要专门从巴黎订做一件堪称杰作的礼服。那样从书房这茧壳里钻出来、蜕成社交界的一只蝴蝶，才叫风光。如今却在德斯蒙德太太的舞会上、穿着自家缝的裙子头一回以成人身份露面，与其说是“出道”，不如说是“渗出”。蝴蝶也分三六九等，她生怕自己这副模样，不像华贵的“红将军”（Red Admiral）或“彩装淑女”（Painted Lady），倒像那只平平无奇的“菜粉蝶”（Common White Cabbage）。若不是立过新年的决心，她的失落多少会漏出些痕迹来，可她硬是勇敢地把这桩心事锁在了自己肚里。家里人固然知道她不情愿去，却只当她害臊。母亲做梦也没猜到真正的缘由。
一月九日晚上，家里上演了一场盛大的着装仪式，母亲和英格丽德充当贴身侍女，““孤儿””在旁边打转，一会儿要递针、一会儿要端热水，就盼着能偷看一眼。一切停当，萨克森太太退后一步端详成果，那神气俨然一位搁下画笔的画家。头一个小闺女长大成人、出落成小姐，是做母亲的生平一件大事。今晚，奎恩丽德就要下水启航，驶入社交的河流了。挑剔地看，她的模样也叫人满意：新裙子虽够不上时装图版的水准，却着实衬她，把白皙姣好的脸庞、莹洁的脖颈和一圈圈亮泽的亚麻色头发都托了出来。

===Original===
“Your gloves and shoes and stockings are all right, and you’ve got a nice handkerchief, and your fan,” reviewed Mother, wrapping an evening cloak round her handiwork. “Goodbye, my bird! Enjoy yourself, and don’t be silly and shy.”
“I shall keep awake till you come back!” Ingred assured her.
It was something at any rate to be going with Egbert and Athelstane. Among the stream of strangers there would be at least two home objects upon which she might occasionally cast anchor. The thought of that buoyed her up as the taxi whirled them down hill to Grovebury.
The Desmonds were giving the dance as a coming-out for one of their own daughters, and their house was *en fête*. An awning protected the porch, red cloth carpeted the steps, a marquee filled the lawn, and a stringed band from Birkshaw had been engaged to play the latest dance music.
===Chinese===
“手套、鞋子、长袜都没问题，手绢也漂亮，扇子带上了。”母亲逐样清点，一边给她的“作品”裹上晚宴斗篷。“再见啦，我的小鸟儿！痛痛快快玩，别傻乎乎地害羞。”
“我一定醒着等你回来！”英格丽德向她保证。
无论如何，能同埃格伯特和阿特尔斯坦一起去，总算有个依靠。在陌生人的洪流里，至少还有两个自家的浮标，可以让她时时抛锚停泊。想到这一层，出租汽车载着他们一路下坡往格罗夫伯里飞驰时，她心里才算有了点底气。
德斯蒙德家办这场舞会，是为自家一个女儿出道，整座宅子张灯结彩。门廊上搭着凉棚，台阶铺了红呢毯，草坪上支起大帐篷，还从伯克肖请来一支弦乐队，专奏时新的舞曲。

===Original===
Quenrede passed calmly enough through the ordeals of leaving her cloak in the dressing-room (where a crowd of girls were prinking, and there was no room for even a glance in the mirror), and the greeting from her host and hostess in the drawing-room. It was in the ballroom afterwards that her agony began. Egbert and Athelstane were whisked away from her to be introduced to other girls, and utter strangers, whose names she seldom caught, were brought to her, took her program, recorded their initials and passed on to book other partners. The few people in the marquee whom she knew were too far away or too occupied to speak to her, so she stood alone, and heartily wished herself at home.
It was better when the dancing began, though her partners scared her horribly. They all made exactly the same remarks about the excellence of the floor, the taste of the decorations, and the beauty of the music, and asked her if she had been to the pantomime, and whether she played golf. Small talk is an art, and though Quenrede had many interests, and in ordinary circumstances could have discussed them, tonight she felt tongue-tied, and let the ball of conversation drop with a “yes” or “no” or “very.” Dances with strangers who expected her to talk were bad enough, but the gaps in her program were worse. No doubt Mrs. Desmond tried to look after all her guests, but several gentlemen had disappointed her at the last minute, and there were not quite partners enough to go round. At a young people’s party Quenrede would have cheerily danced with some other girl in like plight, but at this stiff grownup gathering she dared not suggest such an informality, and remained a wallflower. She caught glimpses occasionally of Egbert and Athelstane, the former apparently enjoying himself, the latter looking as solemn as if he were in church.
“I know the poor boy’s counting his steps and trying not to tread on anybody’s toes!” thought Quenrede. “Ingred said his partners would have to pull him around somehow.”
Supper was a diversion, for she was taken in by quite a nice redheaded boy, a little younger than herself, who, after a manful effort to talk up to her supposed level, thankfully relapsed into details of football-matches. Being a nephew of the house, he proved an adept in attracting the most tempting dishes of fruit or trifle to their particular table, and even basely commandeered other people’s crackers for her benefit. She bade him goodbye with regret.
===Chinese===
在更衣室里寄放斗篷（一屋子姑娘挤着妆扮，连朝镜子里瞥一眼的空当都腾不出），再到客厅里向男女主人问好——这几道关卡，奎恩丽德还算平平静静地过了。真正的受难是在之后的舞厅里。埃格伯特和阿特尔斯坦转眼被人拉走，介绍给别的姑娘去了；一个个全然陌生的男人被领到她跟前——名字她多半没听清——拿过她的舞伴卡，写下姓名缩写，便又转身去约别人。帐篷里她认得的那寥寥几位，不是隔得太远，就是忙得腾不开身，没人来搭话，她只好独自站着，一心只盼自己这会儿是待在家里。
乐声一起、舞步开场，情形总算好些，尽管那些舞伴个个叫她心惊。他们说出口的话一模一样：地板真是上乘，装饰真有品位，音乐真美；又问她看过童话剧没有，打不打高尔夫。应酬闲谈是一门艺术；奎恩丽德平日兴致不少，若在平常本可侃侃而谈，今晚却舌头发僵，一句“是的”“不是”“挺好”，便任凭话头像球一样落了地。跟一门心思指望她开口的陌生人跳舞已经够受，舞伴卡上空着的格子更是难熬。德斯蒙德太太无疑想照料周全每位客人，无奈几位先生临到头爽了约，男舞伴便不太够分。若是在年轻人的聚会上，奎恩丽德早就痛痛快快邀一位同样落单的姑娘下场；可在这种拘谨的大人场合，她不敢提这般出格的主意，只好坐在一旁当“壁花”。她偶尔瞥见埃格伯特和阿特尔斯坦：前者显然自得其乐，后者板着面孔，严肃得像在教堂里做礼拜。
“我知道，这可怜虫准是在数着舞步，生怕踩了谁的脚尖！”奎恩丽德心想，“英格丽德早说过，他的舞伴得连拖带拽才行。”
晚餐总算是个调剂：带她入席的是个怪讨人喜欢的红头发男孩，比她小着几岁；他先是憋足了劲，想撑出几分配得上她“身价”的谈吐，随即如释重负，一头扎进足球比赛的细枝末节里去。他身为这家的外甥，把最馋人的水果、甜奶糕“招引”到自家桌上的本事炉火纯青，甚至不惜昧着良心，把别桌的拉炮也强征过来孝敬她。道别时，她倒真有几分不舍。

===Original===
“I say, I wish my card wasn’t full! I’d have liked a dance with you!” he murmured wistfully as they left the supper-room.
If only she had known people better, and the atmosphere had not seemed so stiff and formal, and she had not been so miserably shy, Quenrede might have enjoyed herself. As it was she began counting the hours. In one of the wallflower gaps of her program she took a stroll into the conservatory. It looked like fairyland with the colored lanterns hanging among the palms and flowers. Somebody else was apparently enjoying the pretty effect﻿—somebody who turned round rather guiltily as if he were caught; then at sight of her smiled in relief.
“I thought you were one of my hostesses come to round me up to do my duty,” he confessed. “I’m a duffer at dancing, so I’ve taken cover in here. I see you don’t remember me, but we’ve met before﻿—at Red Ridge Barrow. My name’s Broughten.”
“Why, of course! You had a piece of candle and showed us inside the mound. I ought to have known you again, but﻿—you look so different﻿—”
“In evening dress! So do you; but I recognized you in a minute. Look here” (in sudden compunction), “am I keeping you from a partner?”
===Chinese===
“喂，可惜我的舞伴卡排满了！真想跟你跳上一支！”走出晚餐厅时，他怅然地低声说。
倘若她认得的人多一些，气氛不那么僵硬拘谨，她自己又没这么可怜地怕生，奎恩丽德本可以玩得尽兴。眼下，她开始一个钟头一个钟头地数着熬。趁舞伴卡上一段没人理的空当，她信步踱进了花房。彩灯掩映在棕榈与花丛之间，望去俨然仙境。显然另有一人也在受用这番美景——听见动静，那人像被当场拿住似的，颇有几分心虚地转过身来；一见是她，才松了口气，绽开笑容。
“我还当是哪位女主人来抓我回去尽义务呢，”他坦白道，“我跳舞笨得像头熊，便躲进这儿来了。看来你不记得我了，咱们从前见过——在红岭古冢（Red Ridge Barrow）。我叫布罗滕（Broughten）。”
“哎呀，当然记得！你拿着一截蜡烛，领我们进了坟丘里头。我本该一眼认出你的，只是——你这模样变得太不一样了——”
“穿上晚礼服了嘛！你也是；可我还是一眼就认出了你。哎，”他忽然过意不去，“我是不是耽误你赴舞伴的约了？”

===Original===
“No more than I am keeping you!” twinkled Quenrede, pointing to the empty line on her program. “I’m not dancing this, so I came here to﻿—to enjoy myself.”
Her companion laughed in swift comprehension.
“I don’t know how other people may find it,” he confided, “but hour after hour of this sort of thing gets on my nerves. A tramp over the moor is far more my line of amusement. I was wishing I might go home!”
“So was I!”
===Chinese===
“你也一样没被耽误呀！”奎恩丽德指着舞伴卡上的空行，眼睛俏皮地一亮，“这一支我不跳，才上这儿来——来享受享受。”
同伴心领神会，朗声笑了。
“不知别人滋味如何，”他吐露心声，“这种场面一钟头接一钟头地熬下去，真叫人心烦。到荒原上长途跋涉，才更合我的脾性。我刚才正巴不得回家呢！”
“我也是！”

===Original===
“But there’s still at least another hour and a half.”
“With extras, more!” admitted Quenrede.
He held out his hand for her program. “I’m an idiot at dancing, but would you mind sitting out a few with me?”
“If you won’t talk about the floor and the decorations and the band, and ask me whether I’ve been to the pantomime, or if I like golf!”
“I promise that those topics shall be utterly and absolutely taboo. I’m sick of them myself.”
===Chinese===
“可至少还得再熬一个半钟头。”
“算上加演的，还不止呢！”奎恩丽德承认。
他伸手来讨她的舞伴卡。“我跳舞是个大笨蛋，不过，你肯不肯陪我坐掉几支？”
“只要你不谈地板、装饰和乐队，也别问我看没看童话剧、爱不爱打高尔夫！”
“我保证，这几个话题彻头彻尾、完完全全地封杀。我自己也听腻了。”

===Original===
Quenrede’s shyness, which was only an outer casing, had suddenly disappeared in the presence of a fellow-victim of social conventions, and conversation came easily, all the more so after being pent-up all the evening. Henry Desmond, wandering into the conservatory presently, remarked to his partner, sotto voce:
“That Saxon girl’s chattering sixteen to the dozen now! Couldn’t get a word out of her myself!”
When Quenrede, sometime about five o’clock in the morning, tried to creep stealthily to bed without disturbing her sister, Ingred, refreshed by half a night’s sleep, sat up wide awake and demanded details.
===Chinese===
奎恩丽德的怕生本不过是一层外壳，此刻遇上了同被社交规矩坑害的难友，那壳倏地褪了，话匣子一开便关不上——憋了整整一晚上，更是滔滔难歇。过了一会儿，亨利·德斯蒙德（Henry Desmond）踱进花房，压着嗓子对舞伴说：
“那个萨克森家的丫头，这会儿倒叽里呱啦说个没完！我自己可连一句话都没能从她嘴里掏出来！”
清晨五点来钟，奎恩丽德蹑手蹑脚地溜回房，想不惊动妹妹就上床，谁知睡了半夜、养足精神的英格丽德一骨碌坐起，睡意全消，非要她从头交代不可。

===Original===
“Sh! Sh! Mother said we weren’t to talk now, and I must tell you everything afterwards. Oh, I got on better than I expected, though most of the people were rather starchy. How did my dress look? Well﻿—*promise* you won’t breathe a word to darling Mother﻿—it was just passable, and that’s all. Some girls had *lovely* things. I didn’t care. The second part of the evening was far nicer than the first, and I enjoyed the dances that I sat out the most. The conservatory was all hung with lanterns. There; I’m dead tired and I want to go to sleep. Good night, dear!”
“But you’ve ‘come out!’ ” said Ingred with satisfaction as she subsided under her eiderdown.
“Oh yes, I’m most decidedly ‘out,’ ” murmured Quenrede.
===Chinese===
“嘘！嘘！妈妈说过现在不许讲话，回头我再一五一十讲给你听。哦，我过得比预想的强，虽说大多数人都有点古板。我那条裙子瞧着怎么样？唔——你发誓不跟亲爱的妈妈透半个字——勉强过得去，仅此而已。有些姑娘的裙子可真叫漂亮。我不稀罕。晚上后半场比前半场痛快多了，我最尽兴的，偏偏是坐着没跳的那几支。花房里挂满了彩灯。好了，我困得要死，要睡觉了。晚安，亲爱的！”
“不管怎么说，你总算‘出道’了！”英格丽德心满意足地说着，缩回鸭绒被底下。
“哦，是啊，我千真万确是‘出’了。”奎恩丽德喃喃道。

## XXI The Abbey Recital / XXI 修道院独奏会

===Original===
Now that Ingred had at last made friends with Bess, she found they had innumerable subjects of interest in common. They were both keen tennis players, dabbled a little in art, pursued Nature study, liked acting, when they had any opportunity of showing their talents in that line, and were enthusiastic over music. Bess was making as good progress on the violin as Ingred on the piano, so there seemed great possibilities of playing together. Sometimes when Bess brought her instrument to school for her lesson, she and Ingred would try over a few pieces, and other girls who chanced to be near would collect and act audience.
“I vote we get up a musical society next year,” suggested Ingred. “It’s impossible this term﻿—we’ve too much on our hands already﻿—but if the societies are rearranged in September, we’ll agitate to let music take a much bigger place than it has done so far.”
“Yes, that would be glorious!” agreed Bess, with visions of a school choir, and even a school orchestra, dancing before her eyes. “Signor Chianti is leaving Grovebury, so if we have a new violin master next term, I hope it will be somebody who’s enthusiastic and able and willing to organize things.”
“That’s the point, of course. Dr. Linton is very able, but not willing to bother with us beyond our lessons﻿—he’s so frightfully busy. I suppose he feels that after training the Abbey choir, and conducting choral societies to sing his cantatas, he doesn’t care to trouble himself over schoolgirls.”
“He’s a *real* musician, though. I often wish I could study under him. I’d love to play something with him, just once, to see how it feels to have him accompany me. I think it would be so inspiring, it would just make one let oneself go! I stay every Sunday evening after service at the Abbey to hear his recitals. Occasionally somebody plays the violin, and his accompaniment is simply gorgeous. He manages to make it sound like a whole orchestra. I’ve never played with an organ. It’s so much fuller than a piano.”
===Chinese===
如今英格丽德（Ingred）总算和贝丝（Bess）交上了朋友，她发现两人志趣相投，共同话题多得数不清。她们都是热心的网球手，都稍稍涉猎过艺术，都热衷于自然研究，一有展露这方面才华的机会便都乐意登台演戏，而且都对音乐满怀热忱。贝丝的小提琴进步得不逊于英格丽德的钢琴，看来两人大有合奏的余地。有时贝丝带琴到学校来上课，课后便和英格丽德试着合奏几支曲子，碰巧在近旁的女生们也会围拢过来，充当听众。
“我提议明年咱们办一个音乐社，”英格丽德建议道，“这学期是办不成了——手头的事已经够多——可要是九月份各社团重新调整，我们一定要力争一番，让音乐占到比以往大得多的分量。”
“对啊，那可就太棒了！”贝丝应道，眼前仿佛已经翩翩浮现出学校合唱团、乃至学校管弦乐团的图景。“基安蒂先生（Signor Chianti）就要离开格罗夫伯里（Grovebury）了，所以要是下学期来一位新的小提琴教师，我希望那是个有热情、有能力、又肯张罗事情的人。”
“关键当然就在这儿。林顿医生（Dr. Linton）很有本事，可除了上课，他不肯为我们多费心——他忙得要命。我猜他是觉得，既然训练了修道院（the Abbey）的唱诗班，又指挥合唱团演唱他的康塔塔，就不愿再为女学生劳神了。”
“不过，他可是位*真正的*音乐家。我常常盼着能跟他学琴。哪怕只跟他合奏一次也好，感受感受由他伴奏是什么滋味。我想那一定令人振奋，能叫人尽情放开！每个星期天晚祷之后，我都留在修道院里听他的独奏会。偶尔有人拉小提琴，他的伴奏实在妙极了，硬是能弄出整整一个乐队的效果。我还从来没跟管风琴合奏过呢，它可比钢琴丰满多了。”

===Original===
“Yes,” agreed Ingred contemplatively.
Bess’s remarks had given her an idea, but she did not want to communicate it at once to her friend. It was nothing more or less than that she should ask Dr. Linton to allow Bess to play with him some time in the Abbey. She wondered whether she dared. His temper was still decidedly irritable, and it was quite uncertain whether he would receive the suggestion graciously, or snap her head off. She thought, however, it was worth venturing.
“I’ll try to catch him in an amiable mood,” she decided.
In order not to arouse any grounds for irritation, she practiced particularly well, and took her next work to him at a high stage of excellence.
“Bravo!” he said, when she had finished her “Serenade.” “I believe you’ve really got some music in you! You brought out that crescendo passage very well indeed. We want a little more delicacy in these arpeggios, and then it will do. Your touch has improved very much lately.”
===Chinese===
“是啊。”英格丽德若有所思地应了一声。
贝丝这话倒给了她一个念头，只是她不想立刻说给朋友听。这念头无非是：去求林顿医生答应让贝丝哪天到修道院来跟他合奏一回。她拿不准自己敢不敢开口。他的脾气依旧相当火爆，这个提议他会客客气气地接下来，还是把她劈头盖脸训一顿，全然没个准。不过她想，值得一试。
“我得挑他心情好的时候去说。”她打定了主意。
为了不给他的火气留任何由头，她练得格外用心，把下一支曲子练到近乎完美的地步，才拿到他那里去回课。
“好极了！”她弹完那首《小夜曲》，他开口道，“我相信你身体里确实装着点音乐！那段渐强处理得实在好。这些琶音再细腻一点，就无可挑剔了。你的触键近来大有长进。”

===Original===
It was so seldom that her master launched forth into praise, that Ingred colored with pleasure. Now certainly seemed the time, if ever﻿—to put in a word for Bess.
“Oh, Dr. Linton, may I ask you to do something for me?” she blurted out.
He thrust back his hair with a mock-pathetic gesture.
“What is it?” he inquired humorously. “Another autograph album? Or a subscription? I’ve grown cautious by experience, and I don’t answer ‘Yes, thou shalt have it to the half of my kingdom!’ I never give blind promises.”
“It isn’t an autograph album (though I’d be glad to have your name in mine, all the same, if I may bring it some day), it is this: I’ve a friend at school, Bess Haselford, who plays the violin very well. She has lessons from Signor Chianti. She goes to all your recitals, and she would so *love* some time to try a piece over with the organ. Do you think, some day when you are in the Abbey, you could let her? I know it’s fearful cheek to ask you!”
===Chinese===
老师难得这样畅畅快快地夸奖人，英格丽德高兴得脸都红了。要替贝丝进言，眼下显然是再好不过的时机。
“哦，林顿医生，我能求您帮我办件事吗？”她脱口而出。
他装出一副可怜巴巴的模样，把头发往脑后一撩。
“什么事？”他诙谐地问，“又是签名册？还是凑份子？我吃过亏，学乖了，可不会张口就应一句‘好啊，哪怕是我国土的一半，也必赐给你！’我从不盲目许诺。”
“不是签名册（不过话说回来，哪天要是许我带去，我还是很乐意让您在我那本上签个名的），是这么回事：我在学校有个朋友，贝丝·哈泽尔福德（Bess Haselford），小提琴拉得很好，跟基安蒂先生学琴。您的独奏会她场场必到，她多么*盼望*能有机会用管风琴试奏一支曲子。您看，哪天您在修道院的时候，能不能答应她？我知道这么求您实在是太冒失了！”

===Original===
“Why, bring her by all means,” said Dr. Linton heartily. “Let me see, I have an organ pupil tomorrow at 3:30. Suppose you come at half-past four, and I’ll give her ten minutes with pleasure. I can fit it in before the choir practice, I dare say.”
“Oh, thank you!” exclaimed Ingred. “We can come straight on from school.”
It was delightful to have caught Dr. Linton in such an amiable mood. Ingred hastened to tell the good news to Bess, and also to beg the necessary permission from Miss Burd.
Bess, greatly thrilled, turned up next afternoon with her violin and music-case, and when classes were over they walked across to the Abbey. The pupil was just finishing his lesson, and some rather extraordinary sounds were palpitating among the arches and pillars of the old Minster.
===Chinese===
“哎呀，尽管带她来好了。”林顿医生爽快地说，“我想想——明天三点半我有个学生来学管风琴。你们四点半来吧，我乐意给她十分钟。我想还赶得及插在唱诗班排练之前。”
“哦，谢谢您！”英格丽德欢呼道，“我们可以放学后直接过去。”
正赶上林顿医生心情这么好，实在运气。英格丽德赶忙把这好消息告诉贝丝，又去向伯德小姐（Miss Burd）请求必要的许可。
贝丝激动不已，第二天下午便带着小提琴和琴谱夹来了，一下课，两人就往修道院走去。那个学生刚要下课，一些颇为古怪的音响正在这座古老大教堂的拱顶和廊柱之间回荡震颤。

===Original===
“It must take ages to learn to manage all those stops and pedals properly,” commented Bess. “I’m glad a violin has only four strings﻿—they’re quite enough!”
They sat in a pew, and waited till the lesson was over, then ventured into the chancel. Dr. Linton saw them in the looking-glass which hung over his seat, and turning round beckoned them to him.
“So you want to hear what it’s like to play with an organ?” he said kindly to Bess, sounding the notes for her to tune her violin, and at the same time turning over her music. “What have we got here? It must be something I know, so that I can improvise an accompaniment. Let us try this Impromptu. Don’t be afraid of your instrument, and bring the tone well out. Remember, you’re in a church, and not in a drawing-room.”
Bess, fluttered, nervous, but fearfully excited and pleased, declared herself ready, and launched into the Impromptu. Dr. Linton accompanied her with the finished skill of a clever musician. He subdued the organ just sufficiently to allow the violin to lead, but brought in such a beautiful range of harmonies that the piece really became a duet.
===Chinese===
“要学会把那么多音栓和踏板摆弄得服服帖帖，准得花上好多年。”贝丝议论道，“幸亏小提琴只有四根弦——这已经尽够我受的了！”
她们在一排靠背长椅上坐下，等课结束，才大着胆子走进圣坛。林顿医生从他座位上方悬着的镜子里瞧见了她们，回过身来，招手让她们过去。
“这么说，你想知道跟管风琴合奏是什么滋味？”他和蔼地对贝丝说，一边弹出几个音给她对弦，一边翻看她的琴谱。“这儿都是什么曲子？得是我会的，我才好即兴配伴奏。咱们就来试试这首《即兴曲》。别怕你手里的琴，把音色放开来。记住，你这是在教堂里，不是在客厅里。”
贝丝心慌意乱、紧张兮兮，却又兴奋欢喜得不得了，表示自己准备好了，随即奏起那首《即兴曲》。林顿医生以一位高明音乐家炉火纯青的技艺为她伴奏。他把管风琴的声浪压得恰到好处，好让小提琴领奏，却又铺展开那样美妙的一路和声，使这支曲子当真成了二重奏。

===Original===
“Why, that’s capital!” he declared at the conclusion. “What else have you inside that case? We’ll have this Prelude now; it’s rather a favorite of mine. The Bourrée? Oh, we’ll take that afterwards!”
Ingred had only expected Dr. Linton to play one piece with Bess, but he went on and on, and even kept the choir waiting while he made her try the Prelude over again.
“I’ve had quite an enjoyable half-hour,” he said, shutting the books at last. “You’re a sympathetic little player! Look here, the lady who was to have helped me with my recital on Sunday week has failed me. Suppose you take her place, and play the Prelude. It would go very well if we practiced it a few times together.”
“Play at the recital!” gasped Bess.
===Chinese===
“哎呀，精彩！”一曲终了，他大声称许，“你那琴匣里还有什么？现在来这首《前奏曲》吧，它相当合我的意。《布列舞曲》？哦，那个咱们回头再拉！”
英格丽德原以为林顿医生至多和贝丝合奏一支曲子，谁知他一曲接一曲停不下来，甚至让唱诗班干等着，非要她把《前奏曲》再试一遍。
“这半个钟头过得真愉快。”他终于合上琴谱说道，“你是个很有灵气的小琴手！听我说，本来答应下星期天独奏会上帮我忙的那位女士失约了。不如你来顶她的缺，演奏那首《前奏曲》。咱们一起排练几遍，准能合得很好。”
“在独奏会上演奏！”贝丝倒抽一口气。

===Original===
“Why not? Ask your father when you go home, and send me a note tomorrow, for I want to get the thing fixed up. These boys are waiting for me now. I have to train them for an anthem. You can come and practice with me on Friday at the same time, 4:30.”
Dr. Linton dismissed the girls as if he took it entirely for granted that the matter was settled. Bess was almost overwhelmed by the proposal. It was considered a great honor to play in the Abbey, and she had never dreamed that it could fall to her lot to be asked to take part in the Sunday recital. She was not sure how her father and mother would view the idea, but rather to her surprise they both readily acquiesced.
“We shall have to get your grandfather to come over and hear you,” said Mr. Haselford.
“Oh yes! And may I ask Ingred to stay with us for the weekend? You see, she can’t come all the way from Wynch-on-the-Wold for Sunday recitals, and it’s entirely owing to her that I’m playing. I should so like her to be there.”
===Chinese===
“有什么不可以？回家问问你父亲，明天给我捎个条子来，我好把这事定下来。这帮男孩现在正等着我呢，我得领他们练一首赞美诗。星期五同一个时间，四点半，你来跟我排练。”
林顿医生就此把两位姑娘打发走了，仿佛事情已成定局，理所当然。这个提议几乎让贝丝不知所措。能在修道院里演奏一向被视为极大的荣誉，她做梦也没想到，受邀参加星期天独奏会这样的事会落到自己头上。她拿不准父母会怎么看这件事，不料两人都欣然应允了。
“我们得请你祖父过来听你演奏。”哈泽尔福德先生说。
“太好了！那我能请英格丽德来我们家过周末吗？您想啊，她总不能大老远从沃尔德畔温奇（Wynch-on-the-Wold）赶来听星期天的独奏会呀，再说，我能上台全托她的福。我真希望她在场。”

===Original===
Ingred accepted the invitation with alacrity. She had grown very fond of Bess lately﻿—so fond, indeed, that Verity’s nose was put considerably out of joint. Verity, though an amusing school comrade, was not a “home” friend. Apart from fun in their dormitory, she and Ingred had little in common, and had never arranged to spend a holiday together. She was a jolly enough girl, but so fond of “ragging” that it was impossible to do anything but joke with her. Bess, on the contrary, was a real confidante who could be trusted with secrets. The two friends spent an idyllic Saturday together. Mr. Haselford motored over to Birkshaw to fetch his father, and took the girls with him in the car. Mr. Haselford the elder proved a delightful old gentleman, deeply interested in music, and much gratified that his granddaughter was to play at the Abbey.
“It was a happy thought of yours, my dear!” he said to Ingred. “Why, I’ve often attended those recitals, and never guessed little Bess would be asked to take part in one! I sang in Grovebury Abbey choir when I was a boy, and I’ve always had a tender spot in my heart for the old town.”
“And you’re not going to forget it, are you, Grandfather?” said Bess pointedly.
“Well, well, we shall see,” he evaded, stroking her brown hair.
===Chinese===
英格丽德欣然接受了邀请。她近来对贝丝喜欢得了不得——简直喜欢到让薇丽蒂（Verity）心里相当不是滋味的程度。薇丽蒂虽是学校里有趣的伙伴，却算不上“登门串户”的朋友。除了寝室里的嬉闹，她和英格丽德没多少共同语言，也从不曾相约一起度假。她是个够热闹的姑娘，只是太爱打闹逗趣，跟她在一块儿除了说笑简直干不成别的事。贝丝却不然，是位真正能托付秘密的知心人。两个朋友一起度过了一个田园诗般的星期六。哈泽尔福德先生开车去伯克肖（Birkshaw）接他父亲，把两个女孩也带上了车。老哈泽尔福德先生果然是位可爱的老绅士，对音乐深感兴趣，听说孙女将要在修道院演奏，欣慰得很。
“多亏你想出了这个好主意，亲爱的！”他对英格丽德说，“嗨，那些独奏会我常去听，却从没料到小贝丝有一天会受到邀请，登台演奏呢！我小时候在格罗夫伯里修道院的唱诗班里唱过歌，心里始终给这座老城留着一块柔软的地方。”
“那您可不会把这事给忘了吧，爷爷？”贝丝话里有话地说。
“呃，呃，到时候看吧。”他抚着她棕色的头发，避而不答。

===Original===
Even poor delicate Mrs. Haselford made a supreme effort and went to church on Sunday evening. It was a beautiful service, and the old Minster looked lovely with the late sunshine streaming through its gorgeous west window. Some of the congregation went away after the sermon and concluding hymn were over, but a large number stayed to hear the recital. Bess, horribly nervous, went with Ingred to the choir, where she had left her violin. There were to be two organ solos, and her piece was to separate them. She was thankful she had not to play first. She sat on one of the old carved Miserere seats, and listened as Dr. Linton’s subtle fingers touched the keys, and flooded the church with the rich tones of Bach’s Toccata in F Major.
