She wished it had been five times as long, so as to delay her own turn. But a solo cannot last forever, and much too soon the last notes died away. There was a pause while the verger fetched a music stand and placed it close to the chancel steps. Dr. Linton was looking in her direction, and sounding the A for her. With her usually rosy face almost pale, Bess walked to the organ, tuned her violin, then took her place at the music stand. It was seldom that so young a girl had played in the Abbey, and everybody looked sympathetically at the palpably frightened little figure. It was the feeling of standing there facing all eyes that unnerved poor Bess. For a second or two her hand trembled so greatly that she could scarcely hold her bow. Then by a sudden inspiration she looked over the heads of the congregation to the west window, where the sunset light was gleaming through figures of crimson and blue and gold. Down all the centuries music had played a part in the service of the Minster. She would not remember that people were there to listen to her, but would let her violin give its praise to God alone. She did not need to look at her notes, for she knew the piece by heart, and with her eyes fixed on the west window she began the “Prelude.”
Once the first notes were started, her courage returned, and she brought out her tone with a firm bow. The splendid harmonies of the organ supported her and she seemed spurred along in an impulse to do her very best. Ingred, listening in the choir, was sure her friend had never played so well, or put such depth of feeling into her music before. It was over at last, and in the hush of the church, Bess stole back to her seat, while Dr. Linton plunged into the fantasies of a “Triumphal March.”
“I’m proud of you!” whispered Ingred, as they walked down the aisle together afterwards.
===Chinese===
连体弱多病、可怜的哈泽尔福德太太也使出浑身力气，星期天晚上上了教堂。那天的礼拜十分美好，夕阳的余晖透过华美的西窗流泻而入，古老的大教堂显得那样动人。讲道和末一首赞美诗结束后，会众中有几位离去了，但一大群人留下来听独奏会。贝丝紧张得厉害，同英格丽德一起到唱诗班席去——她的提琴就存放在那儿。节目单上有两首管风琴独奏，她的曲子正好排在两者之间。她庆幸自己用不着头一个上场。她在一把古旧的雕花祈祷小凳上坐下，听林顿医生灵巧的手指按上琴键，让巴赫F大调《托卡塔》丰厚的音色涌满整座教堂。她巴不得这支曲子再长上五倍，好把自己上场的那一刻往后推。可独奏终究不能无休无止，末尾的音符消散得实在太快。教堂司事搬来一张谱架，放在圣坛台阶近旁，场中静了片刻。林顿医生正朝她这边望，为她弹响了A音。贝丝平日红扑扑的脸几乎没了血色，她走到管风琴跟前，调好琴弦，然后在谱架前站定。这样年轻的姑娘在修道院登台，实在是罕有的事，人人都怀着善意，打量那个显然吓坏了的小小身影。让可怜的贝丝乱了方寸的，正是独自站在那里、面对全场目光的那种感觉。有那么一两秒钟，她的手抖得几乎握不住弓。忽然灵机一动，她越过会众的头顶望向西窗——落日的光辉正透过绯红、湛蓝与金黄的圣像人物熠熠闪耀。千百年来，音乐始终在这座大教堂的礼拜中占有一席之地。她不去想台下的人是来听她演奏的，只让小提琴独自把颂赞献给上帝。她无需看谱，这支曲子早已烂熟于心；她双目凝住西窗，奏起了《前奏曲》。
头几个音一经奏出，勇气便回到了她身上，她运弓坚定，把音色充分放了出来。管风琴壮丽的和声托扶着她，她仿佛受了激励，鼓起一股劲头，要拿出自己最好的水平。英格丽德在唱诗班席上听着，确信朋友从未演奏得这样好过，也从未往音乐里倾注过这样深的感情。一曲终于奏完，在教堂的静默里，贝丝悄悄溜回座位，林顿医生则一头扎进了《凯旋进行曲》的酣畅乐思之中。
“我真为你骄傲！”事后两人一起沿着通道往外走，英格丽德悄声说。

===Original===
“Oh, don’t! I felt as if it wasn’t half good enough,” answered Bess, giving a nervous little shiver now that the ordeal was over.
When Ingred returned to Wynch-on-the-Wold next Friday afternoon she found the family had some news for her. Old Mr. Haselford had been to Mr. Saxon’s office, and had confided to him a scheme that lay very near to his heart. He had prospered exceedingly in his business affairs at Birkshaw, and he was anxious to do something for his native town of Grovebury, where he had been born and had spent his boyhood. He asked Mr. Saxon to prepare designs for a combined museum and art gallery, which he proposed to build and present to the public.
“I can trust the architect of ‘Rotherwood’ to give us something in the best possible taste,” he had remarked. “I want the place to be an object of beauty, not the blot on the landscape that such buildings often prove. Fortunately I have the offer of a splendid site, so the plans need not be hampered by lack of space. I think we shall be able to show that the twentieth century can produce work of merit on its own lines, without slavishly copying either the classical or the medieval style of architecture.”
Old Mr. Haselford had even gone further.
===Chinese===
“哎呀，快别说了！我只觉得拉得连一半的好都够不上。”贝丝答道，考验既已过去，这才打了个紧张的小寒噤。
随后那个星期五的下午，英格丽德回到沃尔德畔温奇，发现家里有消息要告诉她。老哈泽尔福德先生到萨克森先生（Mr. Saxon）的事务所去过，向他吐露了一桩萦绕心头、极想办成的大计。他在伯克肖的生意兴旺至极，很想为故乡格罗夫伯里做点事情——他生在那儿，童年在那儿度过。他请萨克森先生设计一座博物馆兼美术馆合璧的建筑，由他出资兴建，捐赠给公众。
“‘罗瑟伍德’（Rotherwood）的设计者，我相信他拿出来的东西必定品位一流。”他当时这样说，“我要那座建筑成为一件赏心悦目的美物，而不是这类建筑常落得的下场——给风景抹上一块疮疤。幸好有人给了我一块极好的地皮，设计用不着受场地的拘束。我想我们能证明，二十世纪也能沿着自己的路子造出有价值的作品，不必亦步亦趋地照搬古典式或中世纪式的建筑。”
老哈泽尔福德先生甚至还不止于此。

===Original===
“My son’s part of the business is now entirely at Grovebury,” he continued. “And I feel I should like him to have a house of his own. I have bought five acres of land above the river at Trenton, on the hill, where there is a glorious view of the valley. I don’t ask you to copy ‘Rotherwood,’ for I know no architect cares to repeat himself, but a place in the same style and with equal conveniences would suit us very well. My daughter-in-law could talk over the details. It would make a fresh interest for her. We are all tremendously keen about it.”
The new schemes which occupied the minds of the Haselfords brought great rejoicings to the Bungalow.
“Why, it will almost make Father’s fortune!” triumphed Ingred, still in a state of delighted bewilderment.
“It will certainly be an immense pull to him professionally to have the designing of an important public building,” smiled Mother. “And I think he will be able to plan a house to satisfy Mr. and Mrs. Haselford. It’s just the kind of work he likes.”
===Chinese===
“我儿子那一摊生意如今整个儿都在格罗夫伯里，”他接着说，“我想让他有一座自己的宅子。我在特伦顿（Trenton）临河的山坡高地上买下了五英亩地，从那里俯瞰山谷，景色壮丽极了。我不要求你照抄‘罗瑟伍德’，因为我知道没有哪个建筑师乐意重复自己，不过同一风格、同等舒适的宅子，正合我们的意。细节可以由我儿媳跟你商量，这也好给她添一样新的兴致。我们全家都热心得很。”
哈泽尔福德一家心心念念的这些新计划，给“小平房”（the Bungalow）带来了天大的喜讯。
“哎呀，这差不多要让爸爸发达起来了！”英格丽德得意洋洋地说，人还沉浸在惊喜交加的晕眩里。
“能接手设计一座重要的公共建筑，对他的事业自然是大大的助力。”母亲笑吟吟地说，“我想他也设计得出让哈泽尔福德先生夫妇满意的宅子——这正是他喜欢干的那类活儿。”

===Original===
“Mother, when they leave Rotherwood, shall we have to let it to anyone else, or would it be possible﻿—” Ingred hesitated, with the wish that for nearly a year she had put resolutely away from her trembling on her lips.
“To go back there ourselves?” finished Mother. “If Father’s affairs prosper, as they seem likely to do at present, I think we may safely say ‘yes.’ It never rains but it pours, and just as his profession has suddenly taken a leap forward, his private investments have picked up. Colonial mines, that he thought utterly done for, have begun to work again, and pay dividends. Our prospects now are very different indeed from what they were a few months ago. Don’t look too excited, Ingred! Houses take a long time to build, nowadays, and it may be years before Mr. Haselford’s new place is finished, and we can get repossession of Rotherwood.”
“I don’t care, so long as there’s hope of ever having it again!”
“It’s our own home, and naturally we love it, but we must not forget what a debt of gratitude we owe to the Bungalow. We have been very happy here, and I think we have been thrown together, and have learnt to know one another in a way we should never have done at Rotherwood. All the sacrifices we have made for each other have drawn us far closer as a family, and linked us up so that we ought never to be able to drift apart now, which might have happened if we had all been able just to pursue our own line. We have learnt the value here of simple pleasures, we’ve enjoyed the moors and the flowers and the birds and the stars and all the beautiful things that Nature can give us. The realization of them is worth far more than anything that money can buy, for it’s the ‘joy that no man taketh from you.’ I have grown to love Wynch-on-the-Wold so dearly that I shall beg Father to keep on the Bungalow as a country cottage, and I shall run out here for holidays when I feel Rotherwood is too much for me, and I want to be alone for a while with Nature.”
===Chinese===
“妈妈，等他们搬出罗瑟伍德以后，我们得把它租给别人吗？还是说，有没有可能——”英格丽德欲言又止。那个心愿她毅然搁下了将近一年，此刻却颤巍巍地涌到了唇边。
“你是说，咱们自己搬回去？”母亲替她把话说完，“要是爸爸的事业正如眼下这样蒸蒸日上，我想咱们满可以放心说一声‘能’。好事不来则已，一来便成双：他的事业刚刚跃进了一大步，私人投资也跟着有了起色。他原以为彻底完蛋了的殖民地矿山重新开了工，又开始派发股息。咱们如今的光景，同几个月前真是大不一样了。可别太眉飞色舞的，英格丽德！如今盖房子要花好长的时间，说不定得等上好些年，哈泽尔福德先生的新宅子才盖得好，咱们才收得回罗瑟伍德。”
“我才不在乎呢，只要还有指望重新拥有它！”
“那是咱们自己的家，我们自然爱它，可也不能忘了咱们欠‘小平房’多深的一份情。我们在这里一直很快活，而且我以为，正因为一家人挤在一处同甘共苦，我们彼此的了解之深，是住在罗瑟伍德时万万不会有的。我们为彼此做出的种种牺牲，把这个家拉得比从前紧密得多，把我们紧紧连在了一起，从今往后谁也不该再各奔东西——若当初人人只顾各走各的路，倒真可能散了伙。我们在这里懂得了简朴快乐的价值；我们享受过荒原与花朵、飞鸟与星辰，享受过大自然肯赐予的一切美好事物。能体味到这些，远比金钱买得到的任何东西都珍贵，因为这就是那‘没有人能夺去的喜乐’。我已经爱沃尔德畔温奇爱得这么深，往后要恳求爸爸把‘小平房’留作乡间小舍，哪天我觉得罗瑟伍德应付不来、想独自陪大自然待上一阵子的时候，就跑到这儿来歇假。”

===Original===
“I expect we’ll all want to do just the same!” said Quenrede, looking from the gay flowerbeds, which her own hands had planted, over the hedge to where the brown moors stretched away into the dim gray of the distance. “I thought it was going to be hateful when I came here, but, Muvvie, I think it’s been the happiest year of my life! The country may be quiet, but it has its compensation. We’ll walk to the Whistling Stones again, Ingred, as soon as you break up!”
“And that will be exactly a week next Friday!” rejoiced Ingred.
The school was busy with all the usual activities that seem to happen at the end of the summer term. There was a successful cricket match with the Girls’ High School from Birkshaw, a tennis tournament where Nora and Susie took part after all, and won laurels for the College, a Nature Notebook Competition in which Linda, to everyone’s amazement, bore off the first prize against all other schools in the town.
Then there was the annual function, when parents were invited to see a display of Swedish Drill, listen to three-part songs given by the singing class, admire the drawings and clay models exhibited in the studio, and watch a French play acted by the Sixth. It was at the close of this performance that (when friends had taken their departure, and Dr. Linton, who had conducted the singing class, had closed the grand piano and had hurried across to the Abbey to keep an appointment with an organ pupil) a certain piece of news leaked out, and began to circulate round the school. Verity had the proud importance of carrying it into the hostel.
===Chinese===
“我看我们个个都会想这么做！”奎恩丽德（Quenrede）说。她的目光从自己亲手栽下的绚丽花坛掠过树篱，望向褐色荒原一路绵延、渐渐没入灰蒙蒙远方的地方。“刚来的时候我还以为这日子准讨厌透了呢，可是老妈咪（Muvvie），我觉得这是我一辈子最快活的一年！乡下兴许冷清，可冷清自有冷清的补偿。英格丽德，等你一放假，咱们再一块儿去风哨石（the Whistling Stones）走走！”
“那不正好是下星期五——满打满算还有一礼拜嘛！”英格丽德喜滋滋地说。
到了夏季学期末，学校照例忙于那一应似乎年年少不了的活动：同伯克肖女子中学打了一场得胜的板球赛；网球锦标赛上，诺拉（Nora）和苏西（Susie）终究还是出了场，为学院赢得了荣誉；还有一场自然笔记比赛，琳达（Linda）竟力压全城其他学校捧走头奖，让所有人都惊诧不已。
接着便是一年一度的盛会：家长们受邀观看瑞典体操表演，聆听唱歌班演唱的三声部合唱，观赏画室里展出的素描与泥塑，再看六年级演出的一出法语剧。正是在这场演出收场的时候（来宾们已经散去，指挥唱歌班的林顿医生也合上了大钢琴，匆匆赶往修道院，去赴一个管风琴学生的约），某一条消息泄露了出来，开始在学校里流传。薇丽蒂怀着自豪、煞有介事地把它带进了寄宿舍。

===Original===
“Do you know,” she announced, “that Miss Strong is engaged to Dr. Linton, and they’re to be married in the holidays?”
Nora, who was changing a crêpe de chine dress for a serviceable tennis costume, collapsed on to her bed.
“Hold me up!” she murmured dramatically. “Why, I didn’t know he was a widower!”
“Of course he is,” endorsed Ingred, “and a most uncomfortable one, I should say. I went to his house once for a music lesson, and it looked in a fearful muddle. Good old Bantam! We must give her congrats! She’ll soon get things into order there! I believe she adores little Kenneth. I’ve often seen her taking him about the town. She shall have my blessing, by all means!”
===Chinese===
“大家知道吗，”她宣布道，“斯特朗小姐（Miss Strong）跟林顿医生订婚了，假期里就要结婚啦！”
诺拉正把一身双绉绸连衣裙换成经穿的网球装，闻言一头栽倒在床上。
“快扶我一把！”她拿腔拿调地喃喃道，“天哪，我还不知道他是个鳏夫呢！”
“他当然鳏居，”英格丽德帮腔道，“而且要我说，是个过得最不称心的鳏夫。有一回我上他家去上音乐课，那屋子乱得吓人。好样的老‘矮脚鸡’！咱们得给她道喜！她要不了多久就能把那儿收拾得妥妥帖帖！我看她准是疼极了小肯尼思（Kenneth），我好几回瞧见她带着他在城里转悠。我的祝福尽管给她，一万个乐意！”

===Original===
“We might give her something more substantial than congrats and blessings!” suggested Verity. “I vote we get up a subscription in the form for a decent wedding present!”
“Oh yes! Think of Sarkie as Mrs. Linton! They’ll be the oddest couple! I wonder if she’ll get tired of perpetual music, and if he’ll rage round his own drawing-room and ruffle his hair when he feels annoyed, like he does with his pupils!”
“Perhaps she’ll break him off bad habits! I could trust her to hold her own.”
“Oh, she’ll be the gray mare, don’t you fear! But honestly I’m glad! She has her points, and I hope she’ll be happy.”
===Chinese===
“光道喜和祝福，咱们不如再给她点更实惠的！”薇丽蒂提议，“我主张全班凑份子，送件像样的结婚礼物！”
“哦对！想想萨基（Sarkie）要当林顿太太啦！他们准是最古怪的一对！不知她会不会腻烦那没完没了的音乐，也不知他在自家客厅里着恼的时候，会不会也像对学生那样，暴跳如雷，把头发揉得乱蓬蓬！”
“说不定她还能把他的坏毛病治好呢！反正她绝吃不了亏，这一点我信得过。”
“放心吧，往后当家做主的一准是她！不过说真心话，我很高兴！她有她的好处，但愿她过得幸福。”

===Original===
“I wonder who’ll have her form next term?”
“That doesn’t concern us, for we shall probably be in the Sixth.”
“Help! So we shall! I can’t bring my mind to it yet. It gives me spasms!”
“Quite a blossomy prospect, though!”
===Chinese===
“不知下学期谁接她那个班？”
“那与咱们不相干，我们多半要升六年级了。”
“哎呀！可不是嘛！我到现在还转不过这个念头来，一想就浑身发颤！”
“不过，这前景倒真是一片繁花似锦呢！”

===Original===
On the afternoon before breaking-up day, the School Parliament met for the last time. Lispeth, rather sad, and inclined to be sentimental, reviewed from The Chair the events of the past year.
“It has been pioneer work,” she said. “I dare say we might have done it better, but at least we’ve tried. We laid ourselves out to set a standard for the tone of the school, and I think it has kept up fairly well on the whole. The Rainbow League seems thoroughly established, and likely to go on. May I read you some of the things it has done during the year? We made four pounds for the ‘War-Orphans Fund,’ and sent ninety-seven homemade toys to poor children’s treats. The Posy Union gave nine pots of crocuses and fifty-six bunches of flowers to cripples and invalids; the penny-a-week subscriptions have kept two little girls all the summer at the children’s camp, and the Needlework Guild has made thirty-seven garments. It doesn’t sound much when you put it all in hard black and white like that! I hate reports and statistics of societies, they always sound to me somehow so pharisaical, as if we were saying: ‘Look how good we are!’ You know I don’t mean that. What I *do* mean, though, is that we’ve tried not to run everything entirely for ourselves. A rainbow shines when the world is clearing up, and perhaps our little efforts, small as they are, show that things are moving in the right direction. Next term all of us girls in the Sixth will have left, and a new set will take the lead. I can’t say yet who will be Head of the school, but I don’t fancy there’s very much doubt about it. I hope whoever has the reins will keep up what we have worked so hard for this year.”
Lispeth was looking straight at Ingred as she spoke; her meaning was unmistakable. Ingred blushed a faint rosy pink. It had only just dawned upon her that next term would possibly bring her the greatest honor that the College had to confer.
“Whoever is chosen for head-girl,” she stammered bashfully, “I’m sure will try her very best to work for the good of the school. She couldn’t do more than you’ve done﻿—probably she won’t do half so well﻿—but she’ll make an enormous effort to﻿—shall we say﻿—just ‘carry on’!”
===Chinese===
散学前一天的下午，学校议会（School Parliament）开了最后一次会。丽丝佩丝（Lispeth）颇有几分伤感，难免带些多愁善感，在议长席上回顾了过去一年的种种。
“这是披荆斩棘的开创工作，”她说，“要我说，我们本可以做得更好些，但至少我们尽力了。我们一心要为学校的风气立一个标准，我认为总体上保持得相当不错。彩虹联盟（The Rainbow League）看来根基已固，多半能长久办下去。容我给大家念几件它这一年做的事好吗？我们为‘战争孤儿基金’筹得四英镑，送出九十七件自制玩具，给贫苦儿童的游艺会添趣；花束联合会（Posy Union）送给残疾者和病人九盆番红花、五十六束鲜花；每周一便士的认捐，让两个小女孩整个夏天都住在儿童营地；缝纫会（Needlework Guild）缝制了三十七件衣物。像这样一条条白纸黑字列出来，听上去实在算不得什么！我最不喜欢社团的报告和统计数字，它们在我听来总带点法利赛人的味儿，仿佛我们在说：‘瞧瞧我们多善良！’你们知道我不是那个意思。不过我*真正*想说的是，我们尽力了，没有把一切都只围着自己转。彩虹是在雨过天晴时放光的，也许我们这些微末的努力虽然渺小，却也表明事情正朝着对的方向走。下学期，我们六年级的女生都要离校了，会有一批新人来领头。谁将是学校的学生长，我现在还说不上，不过我想这事没有多大的悬念。但愿接过了缰绳的人，能守住我们这一年辛苦挣下的一切。”
她说话时，丽丝佩丝的目光正正落在英格丽德身上；这意思再明白不过了。英格丽德脸上泛起一抹浅浅的红晕。她直到此刻才恍然醒悟：下学期，学院所能授予的最高荣誉，也许就要落到自己头上。
“不管选中谁当学生长，”她红着脸、讷讷地说，“我敢肯定她都会竭尽心力，为学校的益处效力。她不可能做得比你更多——多半连你的一半也赶不上——可她一定会拼尽全力去——该怎么说呢——‘接着往下干’！”

## I The End of the Holidays / 假期的尽头

===Original===
“Ingred! Ingred, old girl! I say, Ingred! Wherever have you taken yourself off to?” shouted a boyish voice, as its owner, jumping an obstructing gooseberry bush, tore around the corner of the house from the kitchen garden on to the strip of rough lawn that faced the windows. “Hullo! Cuckoo! Coo-ee! *In*-gred!”
“I’m here all the time, so you needn’t bawl!” came in resigned tones from under the shade of a large fuchsia. “You’re enough to wake the dead, Chumps! What is it you want now! It’s too hot to go a walk till after tea. I’m trying to get ten minutes peace and quiet!”
Hereward, otherwise “Chumps,” put his feet together in the second position, flung out his arms in what was intended to be a graceful attitude, and made a mock bow worthy of the cinema stage.
“Have them by all means, Madam!” he replied in mincing accents. “Your humble servant has no wish to disturb your ladyship’s elegant repose. He offers a thousand apologies for his unceremonious entrance into your august presence, and implores you to condescend﻿—*Ow! Stop it, you brute!*”
===Chinese===
“英格丽德（Ingred）！英格丽德，老伙计！喂，我说，英格丽德！你到底把自己藏到哪儿去了？”一个男孩的嗓音大声喊着，嗓音的主人跳过一丛挡路的醋栗树，从菜园那边绕过屋角，冲上正对窗户的那片坑洼草坪。“嘿！布谷！咕——喂！英——格丽德！”
“我压根儿一直就在这儿，你用不着扯着嗓子嚷！”一大丛倒挂金钟的荫凉底下传来一声无可奈何的回应。“你简直能把死人都吵醒，傻蛋（Chumps）！这回又想干什么？天这么热，喝完茶之前哪儿也别想去。我就想安安静静待上十分钟！”
赫里沃德（Hereward）——也就是“傻蛋”——双脚并拢，摆出芭蕾二位的架势，双臂大大一展，自以为姿势优雅，随即来了一个够得上电影银幕水准的滑稽鞠躬。
“夫人尽管安享，悉听尊便！”他拿腔拿调地答道。“您卑贱的仆人绝无打扰夫人清雅安眠之意。他为贸然闯入夫人尊前敬上万分歉意，万望夫人屈尊俯就——哎哟！住手，你这畜生！”

===Original===
Hereward’s burst of eloquence was brought to an abrupt end by the violent onslaught of a fox-terrier puppy which flung itself upon him and began to worry his ankles with delighted yelps of appreciation.
“Stop it! Keep off, I tell you! I *won’t* be chewed to ribbons!” he protested, dodging the attacks of the playful but all too sharp teeth, and catching the little dog by the piece of tarred rope that formed its collar. “Here, you’ll get throttled in a minute if you don’t mend your manners.”
“Give him to his auntie, bless his heart!” laughed Ingred, extending welcoming arms to the fat specimen of puppyhood, and rolling him about on her knee. “Oh, he *did* make you dance! You looked so funny! There, precious! Don’t chump auntie’s fingers. Go bye-byes now. Snuggle down on auntie’s dress, and﻿—”
“If you’ve *quite* finished talking idiotic nonsense to that little beast,” interrupted Hereward sarcastically, “you’ll perhaps kindly oblige me by mentioning whether you’re coming or not!”
===Chinese===
赫里沃德这番滔滔雄辩被一只猎狐㹴小狗崽的猛烈突袭戛然打断——它朝他猛扑过去，欢天喜地地汪汪叫着，啃他的脚踝。
“住手！躲开，我跟你说！我才不要被嚼成碎布条呢！”他一边躲闪那口爱玩却又尖利得很的小牙，一边抓住小狗脖子上充作项圈的那截焦油绳，连连抗议。“行了，你再不收敛点儿，过一分钟就要被勒死了。”
“把他交给他的姑姑吧，小乖乖！”英格丽德笑着张开欢迎的双臂，迎向这只胖乎乎的狗崽子，让他在自己膝上翻来滚去。“哎呀，他刚才可真让你跳了段好舞！你那模样太逗了！好了，小宝贝！别啃姑姑的手指头。睡觉觉喽。乖乖趴到姑姑裙子上，然后——”
“你要是跟那个小畜生的傻话已经说够了，”赫里沃德讥讽地插嘴道，“能不能行个好，赏脸告诉我一声，你到底去不去！”

===Original===
“Not coming anywhere﻿—too hot!” grunted Ingred, resettling her cushion under the fuchsia bush.
“Right you are! Please yourself and you’ll please me! Though I should have thought the run to Chatcombe﻿—”
Ingred sprang to her feet, dropping the puppy unceremoniously.
“You don’t mean to say Egbert’s finished mending the motor bike? You abominable boy! Why couldn’t you tell me so before?”
“You never gave me the chance﻿—just said offhand you wouldn’t go anywhere. Yes, the engine’s running like a daisy, and the sidecar’s on, and Egbert’s fussing to be off. If you really change your mind and want to go﻿—”
But by this time Ingred was round the corner of the house; so, shaking a philosophic head at the ways of girls in general, her brother gathered a gooseberry or two en route, and followed her in the direction of the stable-yard.
===Chinese===
“哪儿也不去——太热了！”英格丽德咕哝一声，把坐垫在倒挂金钟丛下重新挪好。
“行嘞！你随意，我也乐得清静！不过我还以为，查特科姆（Chatcombe）这一趟你总该——”
英格丽德一跃而起，随手把小狗撂在了一边。
“你该不是说，埃格伯特（Egbert）把摩托车修好了？你这可恶的家伙！怎么不早点告诉我？”
“你根本不给我机会——张口就随随便便说哪儿也不去。告诉你吧，发动机跑得顺顺当当，跨斗也装上了，埃格伯特正急着动身呢。你要是真回心转意想去——”
可这会儿英格丽德早就绕过屋角跑没影了。她哥哥只好对着女孩子们做事的派头摇了摇头，摆出一副哲人架势，顺手在路上摘了一两颗醋栗，朝马厩院的方向跟了上去。

===Original===
The Saxons were spending their summer holidays at a farm near the seaside, and for the first time in four long years the whole family was reunited. Mr. Saxon, Egbert, and Athelstane had only just been demobilized, and had hardly yet settled down to civilian life. They had joined the rest of the party at Lynstones before returning to their native town of Grovebury. The six weeks by the sea seemed a kind of oasis between the anxious period of the war that was past and gone, and the new epoch that stretched ahead in the future. To Ingred they were halcyon days. To have her father and brothers safely back, and for the family to be together in the midst of such beautiful scenery, was sufficient for utter enjoyment. She did not wish her mind to venture outside the charmed circle of the holidays. Beyond, when she thought about it all, lay a nebulous prospect, in the center of which school loomed large.
On this particular hot August afternoon, Ingred welcomed an excursion in the sidecar. She had not felt inclined to walk down the white path under the blazing sun to the glaring beach, but it was another matter to spin along the high road till, as the fairy tales put it, her hair whistled in the wind. Egbert was anxious to set off, so Hereward took his place on the luggage-carrier, and, after some backfiring, the three started forth. It was a glorious run over moorland country, with glimpses of the sea on the one hand, and craggy tors on the other, and round them billowy masses of heather, broken here and there by runnels of peat-stained water. If Egbert exceeded the speed-limit, he certainly had the excuse of a clear road before him; there were no hedges to hide advancing cars, neither was there any possibility of whisking round a corner to find a hay-cart blocking the way. In the course of an hour they had covered a considerable number of miles, and found themselves whirling down the tremendous hill that led to the seaside town of Chatcombe.
Arrived in the main street they left the motorcycle at a garage, and strolled on to the promenade, joining the crowd of holidaymakers who were sauntering along in the heat, or sitting on the benches watching the children digging in the sand below. Much to Ingred’s astonishment she was suddenly hailed by her name, and, turning, found herself greeted with enthusiasm by a schoolfellow.
===Chinese===
萨克森（Saxon）一家正在海边一座农庄里过暑假，漫长四年之后，全家人头一回团团圆圆。萨克森先生、埃格伯特和阿特尔斯坦（Athelstane）刚刚复员退伍，还没怎么安顿下来过平民生活。他们先到林斯通斯（Lynstones）与家人会合，随后再回老家格罗夫伯里（Grovebury）。在海边的这六个星期，仿佛是一片绿洲，一边是业已过去的战时忧患岁月，一边是横亘在前方的崭新时代。对英格丽德来说，这是宁静美好的黄金时日。父亲和哥哥弟弟都平安归来，一家人又团聚在如此美丽的风景之中，单是这一点，就足以让她快活到心满意足。她不愿让自己的心思越出假期这个迷人的魔圈半步。而圈子外面，每当她把一切细想一遍，那里横着一片朦胧的前景，前景的正中央，学校赫然耸现，怎么也绕不开。
就在这个炎热的八月午后，坐跨斗出去兜一趟风正合英格丽德的心意。顶着烈日走下那条白晃晃的小路、到刺眼的沙滩上去，她本没有这份兴致；可是沿着大路一路飞驰——照童话里的说法，一直驰到头发都在风里呜呜作响——那可完全是另一回事了。埃格伯特急着动身，赫里沃德便坐上车尾的行李架，发动机放了几声空炮之后，三人一齐出发。这一路跑得痛快极了：满眼都是荒原景色，一侧不时瞥见大海，另一侧是嶙峋的岩峰，四下里石楠花开得像翻涌的浪涛，间或被一道道染着泥炭色的细流截开。就算埃格伯特超了速，他也确有借口——眼前道路空空荡荡；既没有会遮住来车的树篱，也不可能一拐弯就撞上一辆堵住去路的干草车。一个钟头下来，他们已经跑出了相当长的一段路，正顺着通往海滨小镇查特科姆的那道大长坡飞旋而下。
到了大街上，他们把摩托车寄存在一家车行里，信步走向海滨大道，汇入度假的人群——人们有的顶着暑气悠闲漫步，有的坐在长椅上，看孩子们在下面的沙滩上挖沙玩耍。让英格丽德大为惊讶的是，忽然有人喊起了她的名字；她一回头，正遇上一位同窗热情洋溢的招呼。

===Original===
“Ingred! What a surprise!”
“Avis! Who’d have thought of seeing you?”
“Are you staying here?”
“No, only over for the afternoon.”
“We’ve rooms at Beach View over there. Come along and have some tea with us, and your brothers too. Yes, indeed you must! Mother will be delighted to see you all. I shan’t let you say no!”
Borne away by her hospitable friend, Ingred presently found herself sitting on a seat in the front garden of a tall boardinghouse facing the sea, and while Egbert and Hereward discussed motorcycling with Avis’s father, the two girls enjoyed a confidential chat together.
===Chinese===
“英格丽德！真想不到！”
“艾维斯（Avis）！谁能想到会碰见你？”
“你们住在这儿吗？”
“不，只是过来玩一个下午。”
“我们就在那边海景楼（Beach View）租了房间。来，跟我们一块儿喝个茶，把你两个兄弟也带上。对，一定得来！妈妈见到你们准高兴。我可不许你说半个‘不’字！”
英格丽德被这位好客的朋友半拥半拉地带走了，不多时便发现自己坐在一幢面海而立、高高大大的家庭旅馆前院花园的座椅上。埃格伯特和赫里沃德同艾维斯的父亲聊起了摩托车，两个姑娘则凑在一起，说起了体己话。

===Original===
“Only a few days now,” sighed Avis, “then we’ve got to leave all this and go home. How long are you staying at Lynstones, Ingred?”
“A fortnight more, but don’t talk of going home. I want the holidays to last forever!”
“So do I, but they won’t. School begins on the twenty-first of September. It will be rather sport to go to the new buildings at last, won’t it? By the by, now the war’s over, and we’ve all got our own again, I suppose you’re going back to Rotherwood, aren’t you?”
“I suppose so, when it’s ready.”
“But surely the Red Cross cleared out ages ago, and the whole place has been done up? I saw the paperhangers there in June.”
“Oh, yes!” Ingred’s voice was a little strained.
===Chinese===
“只剩没几天了，”艾维斯叹了口气，“然后我们就得撇下这一切回家了。你们在林斯通斯还要待多久，英格丽德？”
“还有两个星期。不过别提回家的事，我巴不得假期永远过不完！”
“我也想啊，可假期不肯遂人愿。九月二十一号就开学了。总算能进新校舍了，多带劲，是不是？对了，如今仗打完了，各家也都物归原主，你们该搬回罗瑟伍德（Rotherwood）去了吧？”
“大概是吧，等房子收拾好了就搬。”
“可红十字（Red Cross）的人不是早就撤走了吗？整座宅子也都翻修一新了呀。六月里我还看见裱糊匠在里面干活呢。”
“哦，是啊！”英格丽德的嗓音有些发紧。

===Original===
“You’ll be so glad to be living there again,” continued Avis. “I always envied you that lovely house. You must have hated lending it as a hospital. I expect when you’re back you’ll be giving all sorts of delightful parties, won’t you? At least that’s what the girls at school were saying.”
“It’s rather early to make plans,” temporized Ingred.
“Oh, of course! But Jess and Francie said you’d a gorgeous floor for dancing. I do think a fancy-dress dance is about the best fun on earth. The next time I get an invitation, I’m going as a Quaker maiden, in a gray dress and the duckiest little white cap. Don’t you think it would suit me? With your dark hair you ought to be something Eastern. I can just imagine you acting hostess in a shimmery sort of white-and-gold costume. *Do* promise to wear white-and-gold!”
“All right,” laughed Ingred.
“It’s so delightful that the war’s over, and we can begin to have parties again, like we used to do. Beatrice Jackson told me she should never forget that Carnival dance she went to at Rotherwood five years ago, and all the lanterns and fairy lamps. Some of the other girls talk about it yet. Hullo, that’s the gong! Come indoors, and we’ll have tea.”
Ingred was very quiet as she went back in the sidecar that evening, though Hereward, sitting on the luggage-carrier, was in high spirits, and fired off jokes at her the whole time. The fact was she was thinking deeply. Certain problems, which she had hitherto cast carelessly away, now obtruded themselves so definitely that they must at last be faced. The process, albeit necessary, was not altogether a pleasant one.
===Chinese===
“能重新住回去，你们准高兴，”艾维斯接着说，“我一直眼馋你们那座可爱的宅子。把它借出去当医院，你们一定恨透了。我想等你们搬回去，准会办起各式各样快活的聚会来，对不对？反正学校里的女生们都这么念叨。”
“现在就订计划，未免太早了点。”英格丽德含糊其辞。
“哦，那当然！不过杰丝（Jess）和弗朗茜（Francie）都说，你们家有一块跳舞再妙不过的地板。我始终觉得，化装舞会简直是天底下顶好玩的事。下回再收到请帖，我要扮个贵格会少女，穿一身灰裙子，戴一顶顶俏皮的小白帽。你说衬不衬我？你这一头深色头发，就该扮个东方女郎。我简直能想象出你穿一身白底金纹、隐隐泛光的衣裳当女主人的样子了。你可得答应我，就穿白配金！”
“好吧。”英格丽德笑了。
“仗打完了，又能像从前那样开聚会了，多叫人高兴啊。碧翠丝·杰克逊（Beatrice Jackson）跟我说，五年前在罗瑟伍德参加的那场狂欢节舞会，她一辈子也忘不了，还有那一盏盏灯笼和彩色小灯。别的女生到现在还时常提起呢。哟，锣响了！进屋吧，我们去用茶。”
那天傍晚坐跨斗回去的路上，英格丽德一直非常安静，坐在行李架上的赫里沃德却兴致高昂，一路上冲着她连珠炮似的讲笑话。其实，她是陷入了深深的思索。有些问题，她从前一直随随便便丢在一边，如今却明明白白地冒了出来，再也无法回避，终究得面对了。这个过程虽然必要，却实在算不上愉快。

===Original===
To understand Ingred’s perplexities we must give a brief account of the fortunes of her family up to the time this story begins. Mr. Saxon was an architect, who had made a good connection in the town of Grovebury. Here he had designed and built for himself a very beautiful house, and had liberally entertained his own and his children’s friends. When war broke out, he had been amongst the first to volunteer for his country’s service, and, as a further act of patriotism, he and his wife had decided to offer the use of “Rotherwood” for a Red Cross Hospital. The three boys were then at school, Egbert and Athelstane at Winchester, and Hereward at a preparatory school; so, storing the furniture, Mrs. Saxon moved into rooms with Quenrede and Ingred, who were attending the girls’ college in Grovebury as day boarders. For the whole period of the war this arrangement had continued; Rotherwood was given over to the wounded soldiers, and Mrs. Saxon herself worked as one of their most devoted nurses.
In course of time Egbert and Athelstane had also joined the army, and with three of her menkind at the front, their mother had been more than ever glad to fill up at the hospital the hours when her girls were absent from her at school. Then came the Armistice, and the blessed knowledge that, though not yet home again, the dear ones were no longer in danger. By April the Red Cross had finished its work in Grovebury; the remaining patients regretfully departed, the wards were dismantled of their beds, and Rotherwood was handed back to its rightful owners.
Naturally it needed much renovation and decorating before it was again fit for a private residence, and paperers and painters had been busy there for many weeks. They had only just removed the ladders by the middle of July.
It was nearly August before Mr. Saxon, Egbert, and Athelstane were finally demobilized, and they had gone straight to Lynstones to join the rest of the family at the farmhouse rooms. What was to happen after the delirious joy of the holiday was over, Ingred did not know. She had several times mentioned to her mother the prospect of their return to Rotherwood, but Mrs. Saxon had always evaded the subject, saying: “Wait till Daddy comes back!” and the welcoming of their three heroes had seemed a matter of such paramount importance that in comparison with it even the question of their beloved Rotherwood might stand aside.
===Chinese===
要明白英格丽德的心事，我们得先简单讲一讲故事开始之前她家的际遇。萨克森先生是位建筑师，在格罗夫伯里城里攒下了相当不错的客源。他在此地为自己设计建造了一座十分漂亮的宅子，且生性好客，自家朋友和儿女的朋友都招待得慷慨大方。战争一爆发，他便是头一批志愿为国效劳的人之一；出于更进一层的爱国心，他和太太又决定把“罗瑟伍德”腾出来改作红十字医院。那时三个男孩都在上学：埃格伯特和阿特尔斯坦在温彻斯特（Winchester），赫里沃德在预备学校。于是萨克森太太收存起家具，带着奎恩丽德（Quenrede）和英格丽德搬进了几间租住的屋子，姐妹俩在格罗夫伯里的女子学院走读。整个战争期间，日子都是这么安排的：罗瑟伍德交给了伤兵，萨克森太太自己也在医院里当护士，还是其中最尽心尽力的一位。
到后来，埃格伯特和阿特尔斯坦也相继投军。家中三个男丁都在前线，做母亲的更是求之不得地用医院里的事务，来填满两个女儿上学不在身边的时辰。接着停战了，传来天大的好消息：亲人们虽然还没能回家，至少不再有性命之忧。到四月，红十字会在格罗夫伯里的工作宣告结束；留下的病人依依惜别，病房里的床铺一一拆撤，罗瑟伍德重新交还到它名正言顺的主人手里。
要重新住人，宅子自然免不了大加修缮粉饰，裱糊匠和油漆匠在里面忙碌了好几个星期，直到七月中旬才刚把梯子搬走。
快到八月，萨克森先生、埃格伯特和阿特尔斯坦才总算复员，他们径直赶到林斯通斯，到农庄的屋子里与全家会合。假期的狂欢喜悦过后会是怎样一番光景，英格丽德并不知道。她几次三番向母亲提起搬回罗瑟伍德的前景，萨克森太太却总是避开这个话头，只说：“等爸爸回来再说！”而迎接家中三位英雄归来，似乎是压倒一切的头等大事，相形之下，就连挚爱的罗瑟伍德何去何从，也尽可以先搁在一旁了。

===Original===
The Saxons were a particularly united family, tremendously proud of one another, and interested in each other’s doings. Their name bespoke their old English origin, which (except in the case of Ingred) was further vouched for by their blue eyes, fair skins, and flaxen hair. Egbert and Athelstane were strapping young fellows of six feet, and thirteen-year-old Hereward was taller already than Ingred. Quenrede, immensely proud of her quaint Saxon name, and not at all pleased that the family generally shortened it to Queenie, had just left school, and had turned up her long fair pigtail, put on a grownup and rather condescending manner, powdered the tip of her classic little nose, and was extremely particular about the cut of her skirts and the fit of her suede shoes. It was a grievance to Quenrede that, as she expressed it, she had “missed the war.” She had longed to go out to France and drive an ambulance, or to whirl over English roads on a motorcycle, buying up hay for the Government, or to assist in training horses, or to help in some other patriotic job of an equally interesting and exciting character.
“It’s *too* bad that just when I’m old enough all the jolly things are closed to women!” she groused. “If Mother had only let me leave school a year ago, I’d at least have had three months’ fun. Life’s going to be very slow now. There’s nothing sporty to do at all!”
Ingred, the youngest but one, and fifteen on her last birthday, was the only dark member of the fair Saxon family. At present she was not nearly so good-looking as pretty Quenrede; her mouth was a trifle heavy and her cheeks lacked color; but her eyes had depths that were not seen in her sister’s, and her thick brown hair fell far below her waist. She would gladly have exchanged it for the lintwhite locks of Hereward.
“Queenie was always chosen for a fairy at school plays,” she grumbled, “and they never would have me, though her dresses would have come in for me so beautifully. I don’t see why some fairies shouldn’t have dark hair! And it was just as bad when we acted *The Merchant of Venice*. Miss Carter gave ‘Portia’ to Francie Hall, and made me take ‘Jessica,’ and Francie was a perfect stick, and spoilt the whole thing! Next time, I declare I’ll bargain to wear a golden wig, and see what happens.”
Ingred had been educated at Grovebury College since the morning when, a fat little person of five, she had taken her place in the Kindergarten. She and Quenrede had always been favorites in the school. In prewar days they had been allowed to give delightful parties at Rotherwood to their form-mates, and though that had not been possible during the last five years, everybody knew that their beautiful home had been lent to the Red Cross, and admired their patriotism in thus giving it for the service of the nation. From Avis’s remarks that afternoon it was evident that the girls at the college expected the Saxons to return immediately to Rotherwood, and were looking forward to being invited to entertainments there during the coming autumn and winter. Ingred had contrived to parry her friend’s interested questions, but she felt the time had come when she must be prepared to give some definite answer to those who inquired about their future plans. She managed to catch her mother alone next morning for a quiet chat.
===Chinese===
萨克森一家是天底下格外齐心的一家人，彼此都骄傲得不得了，对每个人的动静都兴致盎然。他们的姓氏本身就道出了古老的英格兰渊源，一双双蓝眼睛、白皙的皮肤和亚麻色的头发更是佐证——唯独英格丽德是个例外。埃格伯特和阿特尔斯坦都是六英尺高的魁梧小伙子，十三岁的赫里沃德也已经比英格丽德还高了。奎恩丽德刚离开学校，她为自己那个古雅别致的撒克逊名字得意非凡，家里人却总把它简称作奎妮（Queenie），这让她老大不痛快。她把长长的金发辫盘了起来，端出一副大人的、颇为居高临下的做派，在端正小巧的鼻尖上扑了粉，对裙子的剪裁和麂皮鞋的合脚程度挑剔到无以复加。有一桩事让奎恩丽德始终耿耿于怀——用她自己的话说，她“错过了这场大战”。她一心渴望去法国开救护车，或者骑着摩托车在英国的道路上飞驰，替政府四处收购干草，或者去帮着调教马匹，再不济，也做点别的同样有滋有味、惊险刺激的爱国工作。
“简直太不公平了！偏偏等我年纪够了，那些好玩的事儿就对女人统统关上了门！”她牢骚满腹。“妈妈要是早一年答应我离校，我至少还能痛痛快快玩上三个月。往后的日子可要闷死了，眼下压根儿就没有一件带劲的事可干！”
英格丽德在家排行倒数第二，刚过的上一个生日满十五岁，是这门金发碧眼的萨克森家里唯一的深色头发成员。眼下她远没有漂亮的奎恩丽德好看：嘴略微厚了些，脸颊也缺些血色；可她的眼睛里有一种妹妹眼中见不到的深邃，一头浓密的棕发直垂到腰际以下。要能换，她巴不得拿它去换赫里沃德那头亚麻色的头发。
“学校里排戏，仙女回回都挑奎妮，”她嘟囔着发牢骚，“偏偏从来不让我演，其实她的戏服给我穿正合身。我就不明白了，仙女凭什么就不能有几个深头发的！我们演《威尼斯商人》（The Merchant of Venice）那次也一样气人。卡特小姐（Miss Carter）把‘鲍西娅’（Portia）给了弗朗茜·霍尔（Francie Hall），派我演‘杰西卡’（Jessica），弗朗茜演得呆头呆脑，把整出戏都搅黄了！下回啊，我说什么也要预先讲好条件，戴顶金色假发上台，看看会怎么样。”
英格丽德从五岁那年一个早晨起就在格罗夫伯里学院念书了——那时她还是个胖乎乎的小人儿，在幼儿班里占了个位子。她和奎恩丽德一直是学校里的宠儿。战前，她们获准在罗瑟伍德为同班同学举办快活的聚会；过去五年虽然办不成了，可人人都知道她们美丽的家宅借给了红十字会，都敬佩她们这样把宅子献给国家效力的爱国心。从艾维斯那天下午的口气听来，学院里的女生显然认定萨克森一家马上就要搬回罗瑟伍德，还眼巴巴盼着秋冬两季能收到那边的请帖呢。英格丽德好歹把朋友那番刨根问底给挡了过去，可她觉得时候到了：往后再有人打听她们今后的打算，她必须备好一个明确的答复。第二天早晨，她总算寻着机会单独堵住了母亲，要安安静静谈一谈。

===Original===
“Mumsie, dear,” she began. “I’ve been wanting to ask you this﻿—are we going back to Rotherwood after the holidays?”
Mrs. Saxon folded up her sewing, put her thimble and scissors away in her workbasket, and leaned her elbow on the arm of the garden seat as if prepared for conversation.
“And I’ve been wanting to talk to you about this, Ingred. Shall you be very disappointed when I tell you ‘No’?”
“Oh, Muvvie!” Ingred’s tone was agonized.
“It can’t be helped, little woman! It can’t indeed! I think you’re old enough now to understand if I explain. You know this war has hit a great many people very hard. There has been a sort of general financial seesaw; some have made large fortunes, but others have lost them. We come in the latter list. When your father went out to France, he had to leave his profession to take care of itself, and other architects have stepped in and gained the commissions that used to come to his office. It may take him a long while to pull his connection together again, and the time of waiting will be one of much anxiety for him. Then, most of our investments, which used to pay such good dividends, are worth hardly anything now, and only bring us in a pittance compared with former years. Instead of being rich people, we shall have to be very careful indeed to make ends meet. To return to Rotherwood is utterly out of the question, and with the price of everything doubled and trebled, and our income in the inverse ratio, it is impossible to keep up so big an establishment nowadays.”
===Chinese===
“亲爱的妈咪，”她开了口，“我一直想问你一件事——假期过后，我们要搬回罗瑟伍德去住吗？”
萨克森太太收起针线活，把顶针和剪刀收进针线篮，胳膊肘支在花园座椅的扶手上，一副准备好促膝长谈的架势。
“我也正想跟你谈谈这件事呢，英格丽德。要是我说‘不’，你会特别失望吗？”
“哦，老妈咪！”英格丽德的语气痛苦万分。
“没有办法呀，小大人！真的没有办法！我想你如今长大了，只要我解释清楚，你是能明白的。你知道，这场战争把许许多多的人打得抬不起头。世道的钱财就像坐上了跷跷板：有人大发横财，也有人一贫如洗。我们家的名字恰恰落在后一栏。你爸爸去法国那阵子，只得丢下自己的事务所任其自生自灭，别的建筑师趁虚而入，把从前送上门的委托生意都揽了过去。要想把客源重新拉拢回来，只怕得熬上好一阵子，而这段等待的日子，对他将是无穷的煎熬。再者，我们那些从前分红优厚的投资，如今几乎一文不值，比起往年的进项，只剩下一星半点。有钱人是做不成了，往后我们得精打细算，才勉强够收支相抵。搬回罗瑟伍德是万万谈不上了——如今样样东西的价钱翻了一番又一番，我们的收入却成反比地往下缩，这样大一户宅院的人家，如今是怎么也撑持不起来的了。”

===Original===
“Where are we going to live, then?” asked Ingred in a strangled voice.
“At the bungalow that Daddy built on the moors. Fortunately the tenant was leaving, and we had not let it to anyone else. In present circumstances it will suit us very well. Athelstane is to be entered in the medical school at Birkshaw; he can ride over every day on the motor-bicycle. We had hoped to send him to study in London, but that’s only one of the many plans that have ‘gane agley.’ ”
“Are Hereward and I to go in to Grovebury every day?”
“Hereward can manage it all right, but I shall arrange for you to be a weekly boarder at the new hostel. You can come home from Friday to Monday. Now, don’t cry about it, childie!” as a big tear splashed down Ingred’s dress. “After all, we’ve much to be thankful for. If we had lost Father, or Egbert, or Athelstane out in France we might indeed grieve. So long as we have each other we’ve got the best thing in life, and we must all cling together as a family, and help one another on. Cheer up!”
“It will be simply h﻿—h﻿—h﻿—hateful to go back to school this term, and not live at R﻿—r﻿—r﻿—rotherwood!” sobbed Ingred.
===Chinese===
“那我们往后住在哪儿呢？”英格丽德憋着嗓子问。
“住到爸爸在荒原上盖的那座小平房里去。幸好原先的租户正要搬走，我们也没再租给旁人。就眼下的境况来说，那房子对我们再合适不过。阿特尔斯坦就要注册进入伯克肖（Birkshaw）的医学院，他每天可以骑摩托车往返。我们本想送他去伦敦求学，可那不过是许多‘全盘落空’的计划中的一桩罢了。”
“那我和赫里沃德每天都要赶去格罗夫伯里上学吗？”
“赫里沃德每天来回跑没问题，不过我打算安排你到新寄宿舍去做周寄宿生，星期五到星期一可以回家来。好了好了，别为这个哭了，小宝贝！”一颗大大的泪珠正啪嗒一声落在英格丽德的裙子上。“说到底，我们有太多该谢天谢地的事。假如我们在法国失去了爸爸，或是埃格伯特，或是阿特尔斯坦，那才真有我们伤心的时候呢。只要一家人还彼此相守，我们就握住了人生里最宝贵的东西。我们全家必须抱成团，互相搀扶着往前走。打起精神来！”
“这学期回学校去念书，却又住不进罗——罗——罗瑟伍德，实在是太——太——太讨厌了！”英格丽德抽抽噎噎地哭道。

===Original===
Her mother patted the dark head that rested against her knee.
“Poor little woman! Remember it’s just as hard for all the rest of us. We’ve each got a burden to carry at present. Suppose we see who can be pluckiest over it. We’re fighting fortune now, instead of the Hun, and we must show her a brave face. Won’t you march with the family regiment, and keep the colors flying?”
“I’ll try,” said Ingred, scrubbing her eyes with her pocket-handkerchief.
===Chinese===
母亲轻轻拍着靠在自己膝上的那颗黑发脑袋。
“可怜的小大人！记住，对我们其余每个人来说，这日子同样艰难。眼下我们人人肩上都有一副担子。不如咱们来比一比，看谁挑得最勇敢。如今我们要斗的是命运，不再是德国佬了，我们得冲她摆出一副勇敢的面孔。你愿不愿意随家族军团一道行军，让军旗始终高高飘扬？”
“我试试。”英格丽德说着，用手帕使劲擦着眼睛。

## V The Fifth-Form Fête / 第五章 五年级联欢会

===Original===
By a general indulgence issued from headquarters, the dismissal bell rang at 3:45 the next Friday afternoon, instead of, as usual, at four o’clock. The mistresses entered up the marks, put away their books, said “Good afternoon, girls!” and made their exit, leaving the building for once in the sole possession of the pupils. Miss Strong, indeed, who disapproved of the whole business, took the precaution of locking her desk before her departure, a proceeding which provoked indignant sniffs from the witnesses; but, sublimely indifferent to public opinion, she put the key in her pocket, and stalked from the room. The girls gave her a few moments’ grace to get out of earshot, then broke into a babble of conversation.
“Which are we having first, the election or the tea?”
“Oh, the tea!”
“No, no! Business first and pleasure afterwards.”
“I can’t vote till I’ve had some tea.”
“It’s too early!”
===Chinese===
经总部一体通融，那个星期五下午的放学铃破例在三点三刻敲响，而往常总要到四点整。各位老师登记好分数，收起书本，道一声“下午好，姑娘们！”便相继退场，把整座校舍头一回留给了学生独享。斯特朗小姐（Miss Strong）对整桩事情本就不以为然，临走还特意留了个心眼，先把讲桌锁上，此举惹得目击的众人愤愤地直哼鼻子；她却对公众舆论超然得很，钥匙往口袋里一揣，昂首阔步出了教室。姑娘们容她几分钟，待她走得听不见了，随即叽叽喳喳炸开了锅。
“咱们先办哪一样，选举还是茶会？”
“哦，当然是茶会！”
“不，不！先办事，后享乐。”
“不喝点茶，我可投不了票。”
“这也太早啦！”

===Original===
“No, it isn’t! We’re most of us ready for it.”
“Look here!” suggested Ingred. “Let’s settle it this way. Have tea first, then the election, and then some fun afterwards. Don’t you think that would sandwich things best?”
“True, O Queen! I don’t mind what happens afterwards, so long as I get a bun quick!”
“Let’s fetch the prog,” agreed Linda Slater, leading the way towards the cloakroom where the baskets had been stored.
The giggling procession met emissaries from other forms, bent on a like errand, and exchanged a brisk banter as they passed on the stairs.
“We’ve got jam tartlets!”
===Chinese===
“哪儿早了！咱们多半都等不及啦。”
“我说！”英格丽德（Ingred）提议道，“咱们这么办：先喝茶，再选举，末了再热闹一场。你们说，这样一层夹一层地排开，是不是最相宜？”
“说得是，女王陛下！往后出什么事我一概不管，只要快些给我一个小圆面包！”
“咱们取吃食去。”琳达·斯莱特（Linda Slater）赞同道，领头朝存放提篮的衣帽间走去。
这支咯咯直笑的队伍，半路遇上别班派出的使节，也负着同一桩使命，大家在楼梯上错身而过时，脆生生地斗了几句嘴。
“我们有果酱小挞！”

===Original===
“Not as nice as our cheese cakes!”
“Nellie’s brought a whole pound of macaroons!”
“Oh! will you swap with us for rock buns?”
“I should just think not!”
“Dolly Arden has five oranges!”
“Well, we’ve got bananas!”
===Chinese===
“可比不上我们的乳酪挞！”
“奈莉（Nellie）带了整整一磅蛋白杏仁饼！”
“哦！跟我们换岩皮饼吃不吃？”
“想都别想！”
“多莉·阿登（Dolly Arden）带了五个橙子！”
“哼，我们有香蕉！”

===Original===
After successfully fetching the provisions, having routed a marauding band of juniors who were poking inquisitive fingers into the baskets, the members of **Va** returned to the form-room, closed the door, and gave themselves up to festivity. The four girls from the hostel need have had no fear of scarcity, for the others had brought ample to compensate for their deficiency. By general consent all the cakes were pooled, set out on hard-backed exercise books in lieu of plates, and handed round the company. Bess, whose basket contained two thermos flasks, a dozen cheese cakes, and some meringues, was felt to have brought a valuable contribution. It seemed a new experience to be sitting at their desks, drinking tea and eating cakes, instead of doing translation or writing exercises.
“Pity the Snark didn’t stop! She doesn’t know what she’s missing!” remarked Joanna Powers, as she took a meringue.
“Oh, Kafoozalum! We shouldn’t have had much fun if the Snark had stayed! Don’t bring her back, for goodness’ sake, Jo!”
“I wasn’t going to! Besides which, she’s probably halfway down town at present, having tea in a café. She generally does on Fridays.”
“She won’t get a better tea than we’re having!”
“I’ll undertake she won’t! This meringue is absolutely topping! I wonder if there’s another left.”
===Chinese===
吃食顺利取到手——半路还击退了一伙趁乱打劫的低年级小家伙，她们正把好奇的手指头往提篮里乱戳——随后，**五甲班**（Va）的成员们回到教室，关上门，纵情欢庆起来。寄宿舍来的四位女生本不必担心短缺，因为旁人带的茶点绰绰有余，尽够补上她们的空缺。经众口一词的同意，所有糕点统统归公，摆到硬面练习本上权充盘子，挨个儿传给大家。贝丝（Bess）那只提篮里装着两只保温瓶、一打乳酪挞，还有几只蛋白酥，众人只觉这份贡献着实可观。坐在自己课桌前，不去做翻译、写练习，反倒喝茶吃糕点，这滋味像是头一遭。
“可惜‘毒舌精’（the Snark）没留下！她可不知道自己错过了什么！”乔安娜·鲍尔斯（Joanna Powers）说着，拿起一只蛋白酥。
“哎呀老天爷！‘毒舌精’要是留下来，咱们还有个玩儿？看在老天的份上，千万别招她回来，乔！”
“我才没那打算呢！再说，她这会儿多半已经逛到城里，正坐在咖啡馆里吃茶点呢。每逢星期五，她照例如此。”
“她可吃不到比咱们更好的茶点！”
“这个我敢打包票！这蛋白酥绝了！不知道还有没有剩的。”

===Original===
“No, they’re gone, every one of them!”
“Hard luck!”
Though the hour might be early, the girls’ appetites were quite equal to the task of finishing the various delicacies in the way of sweet stuff which they had brought with them. Cakes disappeared like snow in summer, and chocolate boxes, passed round impartially, soon returned empty to their owners. When everything seemed almost finished, Bess produced another hamper, which she had carried up from the cloakroom, and stowed away under her desk. She handed it rather shyly to Beatrice, who happened to be her nearest neighbor.
“Mother sent these, and wants you all to share them,” she remarked.
Beatrice, Francie, and Linda opened the hamper all three together, then with a delighted “O-Oh!” of satisfaction drew out six beautiful bunches of purple grapes. Ingred, finishing her cup of tea, choked and coughed. She knew those grapes well. They grew in the vinery at Rotherwood, and had been the pride of her father and of the head-gardener. She had not tasted one of them for five years, for during the war they had always been given to the patients in the Red Cross Hospital, but she could not forget their delicious flavor. Why had her father let the vinery with the house? The grapes ought to be hers to give away﻿—not this girl’s. Nobody else in the room cared in the least where the fruit came from, so long as it was there. Appreciative eyes looked on in glad anticipation while Beatrice and Francie divided the bunches with as much mathematical accuracy as they could muster at the moment. A portion was laid upon each desk, and the girls fell to.
===Chinese===
“没了，一只不剩，全光啦！”
“真可惜！”
时辰虽然还早，姑娘们的胃口却全然担得起扫荡各色甜食的重任。糕点像夏日残雪，眨眼消融殆尽；巧克力盒一视同仁地传过一圈，很快便空空如也地回到主人手里。眼见一切将尽，贝丝又搬出一只提篮——那是她从衣帽间拎上来、塞在课桌底下的——有些腼腆地递给碧翠丝（Beatrice），后者恰巧坐得离她最近。
“母亲让我捎来的，她想请大家都尝尝。”她说。
碧翠丝、弗朗茜（Francie）和琳达三人一齐动手开了篮盖，随即发出一声心满意足的“噢——噢！”，捧出六串水灵灵的紫葡萄。英格丽德正喝完杯中残茶，猛地呛得咳嗽起来。这些葡萄她太熟悉了。它们长在罗瑟伍德（Rotherwood）的葡萄温室里，一直是她父亲和花匠领班的骄傲。整整五年，她一颗也不曾尝到，因为大战期间它们总是送去红十字会医院（Red Cross Hospital）给伤员；可那股鲜美滋味，她怎么也忘不掉。父亲为什么连房子带温室一并租了出去？这葡萄本该由她来送人——轮不到这个女生。教室里再没有旁人在乎水果的来路，只要有得吃就好。碧翠丝和弗朗茜分串的时候，一双双艳羡的眼睛喜滋滋地候着，两人拿出了当下所能凑齐的全部数学精度。每张课桌都摆上一份，姑娘们纷纷大快朵颐。

===Original===
“Delicious!”
“Never tasted better in my life!”
“Absolutely topping!”
“Makes one want to go and live in a vineyard!”
“They’re exactly ripe!”
“Ingred, you’re not eating yours!”
===Chinese===
“太好吃了！”
“这辈子没吃过这么好的！”
“绝了！”
“简直让人想搬到葡萄园里去住！”
“熟得正是时候！”
“英格丽德，你怎么不吃你那份？”

===Original===
“I don’t want them, thanks,” said Ingred hurriedly. “I don’t indeed. I’ve had enough. Pass them on to somebody else, please!”
“Well, if you really don’t want them, they won’t go a-begging, I dare say!”
Ingred felt as if the grapes would choke her. She could not touch one of them. She hated Bess for having brought them to school, quite irrespective of the fact that she would have done exactly the same in her place, had she been fortunate enough to have the opportunity. Bess, looking shy, and anxious to evade the thanks that poured in upon her, bundled the hamper away under the desk again, and made a palpable effort to change the subject.
“What about this election?” she asked. “Time’s getting on. It’s after half-past four.”
“Good night! Have we been all that time feeding? Here, girls, if you’ve *quite* finished, let’s get to business,” said Avis, rapping on her desk as a signal for silence, and constituting herself spokeswoman for the occasion. “You know what we’ve met here for﻿—to choose a warden to represent us on the School Council. Well, I feel we couldn’t do better than send up Ingred Saxon. She’d look after our interests all right, if anybody would. I beg to propose Ingred Saxon.”
===Chinese===
“我不想吃，谢了，”英格丽德慌忙说，“真的不想。我已经够了。请传给别人吧！”
“行吧，你要真不要，管保不愁没人接手！”
英格丽德只觉得这些葡萄会哽在喉咙里，一颗也碰不得。她恨贝丝把它们带到学校来，全然不顾这样一桩事实：倘若她有幸得到同样的机会，换了她在贝丝的位置，也会分毫不差地这么做。贝丝一脸羞怯，急着躲避纷至沓来的道谢，慌忙把提篮又塞回课桌底下，并且明摆着使起劲儿来要岔开话头。
“选举怎么办呢？”她问，“时候不早了，都过四点半了。”
“我的天！咱们竟吃了这么半天？喂，各位，要是*当真*吃完了，就办正事吧，”艾维斯（Avis）说着敲了敲课桌，示意大家肃静，还自任了这场合的发言人。“咱们聚在这儿为的什么，大家都知道——选一位班代表（warden），到学校议会（School Council）上代表咱们全班。依我看，推英格丽德·萨克森（Ingred Saxon）上去再好不过。要说有人能替咱们看住利益，那非她莫属。我提议英格丽德·萨克森。”

===Original===
“And I beg to second that!” called Nora.
“Hands up, those in favor!”
Such a forest of arms immediately waved in the air that (though in strict order) it seemed hardly necessary for Avis to call out:
“Those against!”
No opposition hands appeared, so without further discussion the election was carried.
“Congrats, Ingred!” said Nora, patting the heroine on the back.
===Chinese===
“我附议！”诺拉（Nora）喊道。
“赞成的举手！”
顷刻间，一片手臂的森林在半空挥舞，害得艾维斯——虽说照章仍须喊上一句——那声呼喊似乎全属多余：
“反对的举手！”
反对的手一只也没冒出来，于是不必再多费唇舌，选举通过。
“恭喜呀，英格丽德！”诺拉拍拍这位当选主角的背说。

===Original===
“I told you it would be a walk over, old sport!” whispered Verity.
“We’d talked it over beforehand, you see, and everybody had agreed to choose you, so it was really only a matter of form,” explained Francie.
“The Sixth are having a ballot,” put in Jess.
“And **Vb** are going to fight like Kilkenny cats over Magsie and Barbara.”
“There’ll be some hullabaloo in several of the forms, I expect.”
“Thanks awfully for electing me,” replied Ingred. “I suppose I ought to make a speech, but I really don’t know what to say!”
===Chinese===
“我说什么来着，老伙计，你稳赢不费劲！”薇丽蒂（Verity）悄声道。
“我们事先都商量好了，大家一致同意选你，所以其实不过是走个过场。”弗朗茜解释道。
“六年级在搞无记名投票呢。”杰丝（Jess）插嘴道。
“还有**五乙班**（Vb），为着玛姬（Magsie）和芭芭拉（Barbara）两人，怕是要像基尔肯尼猫那样斗个你死我活。”
“我看好几个班都得闹翻天。”
“多谢大家选我，”英格丽德答道，“按说我该发表一篇演说，可实在不知道说什么好！”

===Original===
“You’ve got to say it all the same!” laughed Verity. “Members of Parliament always make speeches to their constituents. Here, take the Snark’s desk as your thingumgig﻿—rostrum, or whatever it’s called, and begin your jaw-wag!”
“Friends, Romans, Countrymen, lend me your ears!” squeaked Kitty Saunders.
Pushed forward by a dozen hands, Ingred found herself occupying the mistress’s place, and, facing her audience, made a valiant attempt at oratory. With cheeks aglow, and dark eyes shining like stars, she looked an attractive little figure, and a bright and suitable leader for the form.
“I can’t really think why you should have chosen me,” she began (“don’t be too modest!” yelled a voice from the back), “but as you *have* made me your warden, I’ll take care that all our grievances are very well aired at the School Council.” (“You’ll have your work cut out!” interrupted Francie.) “Of course I know it won’t all be plain sailing, and that the Sixth need a great deal of sticking up to over many matters.” (“That’s so!” came from the front desk.) “But perhaps they’ll be prepared to talk things over now, and make some concessions.” (“Time they did!”) “At any rate, I shall be able to tell them what you all think” (“Flattering for them!”), “and to make things as smooth as possible for **Va** Now, as I’m warden, may I propose that we have some fun before we go? Shall we have music, or games? Hands up for an Emergency Concert!”
===Chinese===
“不知道也得说！”薇丽蒂笑道，“国会议员向例要向选民发表演说。来，把‘毒舌精’的讲桌权当你的那个什么玩意儿——讲坛，反正随便叫什么——开始你的滔滔宏论吧！”
“朋友们，罗马人，同胞们，请侧耳听我说！”凯蒂·桑德斯（Kitty Saunders）尖着嗓子喊。
英格丽德被十几只手推上前，发觉自己已站上老师的位子，便面向听众，鼓起勇气试起雄辩来。她双颊绯红，乌黑的眼睛亮若星辰，瞧着是个惹人喜爱的小小人儿，也正是全班一位神气又相称的领头人。
“我实在想不出你们干吗偏偏选中我，”她开了腔（“可别太谦虚喽！”后排一个声音嚷道），“不过，你们*既已*选我当班代表，我担保把咱们所有的委屈，都好好摆到学校议会上去说道说道。”（“有你忙活的！”弗朗茜插嘴。）“我当然知道这事不会一路顺风，好些事情上，六年级那帮人是得有人顶一顶的。”（“正是！”前排课桌传来一句。）“不过这会儿，她们也许肯把事情摊开来谈，做些让步了。”（“早该如此！”）“无论如何，我总能把大家的心思告诉她们”（“她们可要飘飘然了！”），“替五甲班把事事办得尽量顺当。好了，既然我是班代表，可否提议散场之前先乐一乐？来点音乐，还是做游戏？赞成开紧急音乐会的举手！”

===Original===
“A very neat way of getting out of further speechifying!” said Verity, as by general consent the concert carried the day; “but you shall open it yourself, Madam Warden, so I warn you! You’re not going to be let off, don’t you think it! Silence! Ladies and gentlemen, the first item on the program will be a piano solo by Miss Ingred Saxon, the celebrated musical star, brought over at enormous expense, on purpose for this occasion.”
“You blighter!” murmured Ingred, as the prospective audience shouted “Hear! Hear!”
“Not a bit of it!” purred Verity. “I guess we’ll take sparks out of the Sixth and everybody else.”
**Va** that afternoon was certainly in a position to boast itself. It was the only form in possession of a piano: for by the sheerest accident it had one. The instrument was only a temporary visitor, placed there for convenience while some repairs were being done to a leaking gas-pipe in one of the music rooms. It’s an ill wind, however, that blows nobody good, and it gave **Va** an opportunity that was denied even to the Sixth. Ingred was at once escorted to the piano, and officious hands piled exercise books on a chair to make her seat high enough.
===Chinese===
“好一记漂亮的开溜，省得再讲下去！”薇丽蒂说——音乐会经众人赞成，当场定了案——“不过开场节目还得你亲自来，班代表女士，先给你提个醒！你休想获免，趁早别打这主意！肃静！女士们，先生们，节目单第一项：钢琴独奏，演奏者英格丽德·萨克森小姐——大名鼎鼎的音乐明星，不惜重金、专为本次盛会礼聘而来。”
“你这促狭鬼！”英格丽德低声咕哝，台下的准听众已然高呼“好哇！好哇！”
“才不亏待你呢！”薇丽蒂娇声笑道，“我猜呀，咱们准能把六年级和其余所有人的风头都抢个精光。”
那天下午，五甲班确实有得可夸：全校独此一班，教室里占着一架钢琴，而这纯属十万分的偶然。那乐器不过是位临时客居，只因一间音乐教室的煤气管漏了气要修缮，图省事才暂挪到这里存放。不过俗话说得好，刮得再倒霉的风，也总有个人沾光——它给了五甲班一个连六年级都无缘染指的机会。英格丽德当即被护送到琴前，几双热心过剩的手往椅子上摞练习本，好把她的座位垫得够高。

===Original===
“I can’t remember anything! I can’t indeed!” she protested vigorously.
“Now don’t twitter nonsense!” said Nora. “I’ve heard you play dozens﻿—yes, *dozens*!﻿—of things without music at the hostel, so you’ve just got to try!”
“I shall break down, I know I shall!”
“Then you can begin again at the beginning. Fire away, and don’t be affected!” commanded Nora.
It is one thing to play a piece from memory when you have the room to yourself, and quite another to play it with half a dozen girls hanging over the piano, and the rest of the audience sitting on their desks. Ingred wisely did not venture on anything too classical, but tried a bright “Spanish Ballade,” and managed to get successfully to the end of it without any breakdown. In the midst of the clapping that followed came a loud rap-tap-tap at the door, which immediately opened to admit﻿—much to the astonishment of the Fifth﻿—two of the prefects, and a consignment of Sixth form girls.
===Chinese===
“我什么都记不得！真记不得！”她拼命推辞。
“行了，别叽叽喳喳说傻话！”诺拉说，“在寄宿舍里，我听你不看谱子弹过几十首——没错，*几十首*！——曲子，所以你非试不可！”
“我准会弹砸，我知道我准会！”
“砸了就从头再弹呗。快开始，别装模作样！”诺拉命令道。
独自占着一间屋子时凭记忆弹曲是一回事；半打姑娘趴在琴沿上探头探脑、其余听众坐上课桌，这时再凭记忆弹，就完全是另一回事了。英格丽德很有自知之明，不敢碰太古典的曲目，试了一支轻快的《西班牙叙事曲》，顺顺当当撑到曲终，一处没砸。掌声正酣，门口响起重重的笃笃笃，门随即打开——进来的竟是两位级长，外加一批六年级女生，惊得五年级众人目瞪口呆。

===Original===
“Whatever have we been and gone and done now?” murmured Verity.
“Is music taboo?” asked Ingred guiltily, slipping away from the piano.
The errand of the prefects, however, was evidently one of conciliation, and not of reproof. They were smiling, and looking amiability itself.
“We thought, as you’ve got a piano in your room,” began Lilias Ashby, “that we might as well come and join you, if you don’t mind. Janie’s got a book of songs with her.”
“Oh, by all means, of course!” replied **Va** politely and unanimously. “We’re just having a sort of concert, you know.”
“Sure you don’t mind?”
===Chinese===
“咱们这会儿到底是闯了什么祸呀？”薇丽蒂咕哝道。
“难道弹琴也犯禁？”英格丽德心虚地问，悄悄从钢琴边溜开。
不过，两位级长这趟差事显然是来修好，不是来问罪的。她们笑意盈盈，满脸一团和气。
“我们寻思，你们教室里有架钢琴，”莉莉亚丝·阿什比（Lilias Ashby）开了头，“不嫌弃的话，我们不如过来同你们凑一块儿。珍妮（Janie）随身带了本歌谱。”
“哦，那敢情好，求之不得！”五甲班有礼有节、异口同声地答道，“要知道，我们正开着个小型音乐会呢。”
“真不嫌弃？”

===Original===
“Not a bit of it!”
“Right-o! Run and tell Janie then, Susie, and ask her to bring the others.”
An invasion from the Sixth was indeed an unwonted honor, which probably nothing short of a piano would have accomplished. The hostesses, somewhat overwhelmed, seated the distinguished guests to the best of their ability in the rather limited accommodation, and hospitably passed round their few remaining pieces of chocolate.
“We’ll leave the door open, please,” said Lispeth, “because I promised Miss Burd not to let those intermediates get too outrageous, and I have to listen out for them.”
Janie Potter, with her book of songs, was pushed forward, and began to entertain the company with popular selections of the day, to which they chanted the choruses. She had a good clear voice, and the audience joined with enthusiasm in the various ditties.
The clapping which followed was continued down the landing, and, through the open door, peered the interested faces of most of the members of **Vb** who had come to share the fun.
===Chinese===
“一点儿也不！”
“好嘞！苏西（Susie），快跑去告诉珍妮，请她把其余几位也一并带来。”
六年级大军压境，确是难得一遇的殊荣，若不是这架钢琴，怕是什么也请不动。主人们多少有些招架不住，在这处颇为局促的地界里竭尽所能地安顿贵客，又殷勤地把仅剩的几块巧克力传了一圈。
“门请敞着，”丽丝佩丝（Lispeth）说，“我答应过伯德小姐（Miss Burd），不许那帮中年级学生闹得离了谱，我得竖着耳朵听着那边的动静。”
珍妮·波特（Janie Potter）抱着歌谱被推上前来，开始用当下流行的曲目给众人助兴，大家跟着齐声唱和副歌。她嗓音清亮，听众们兴致勃勃地加入一支又一支小曲。
掌声一路响下楼道；敞开的门边，探进来五乙班大半成员一张张好奇的脸——她们也是来凑热闹的。

===Original===
“May we butt in?” they asked hopefully.
“Not a square inch of room for you,” answered Lispeth, “but you may squat in the corridor outside if you like. Anybody who performs can join the show, but that’s all. I’ll tell you when it’s your turn. It’s **Va** next. Now then,” (turning to the hostesses), “who else can do anything? Francie Hall, come along at once!”
“I can’t! I can’t!” objected Francie. “So it’s no use asking me; it isn’t indeed! I’ll tell you what﻿—Bess Haselford plays the violin, and, what’s more, she’s got it with her, for I saw her put it away in the dressing-room.”
“O-O-Oh! It was my lesson with Signor Chianti this afternoon, that’s why I had to bring it!” said Bess, turning red.
“Go and fetch it, Francie!” ordered Lispeth. “You know where it is.”
Francie returned in a short time, and handed the neat leather case to its owner. Bess, looking flustered and nervous, drew out the violin, and began to tune it.
===Chinese===
“能捎上我们一块儿吗？”她们满怀希冀地问。
“你们呀，连一寸见方的空地都没了，”丽丝佩丝答道，“不过愿意的话，可以蹲在外头走廊里。凡是出节目的都算同台，仅此而已。轮到你们我会喊的。下一个还是五甲班。好了，”（转向主人们）“还有谁会点拿手的？弗朗茜·霍尔（Francie Hall），快上来！”
“我不行！我不行！”弗朗茜推拒道，“所以求我也白搭，真的没用！我出个主意吧——贝丝·哈泽尔福德（Bess Haselford）会拉小提琴，不单会，今儿琴还带在身上呢，我亲眼看见她把琴收进更衣室了。”
“噢——噢——噢！今天下午我上基安蒂先生（Signor Chianti）的课，这才不得不带琴来的！”贝丝说着，涨红了脸。
“去把琴取来，弗朗茜！”丽丝佩丝吩咐道，“你知道搁在哪儿。”
弗朗茜不多时便回来了，把那只精巧的皮琴盒交还主人。贝丝神色慌张紧张，抽出提琴，调起弦来。

===Original===
“I’ve brought your music too!” said Francie, triumphantly opening a folio, “so you’ve no excuse for saying you can’t remember anything. Who’ll play your accompaniment? Here, Ingred!”
“Oh! somebody else would do it far better,” protested Ingred. “Janie﻿—”
“I’m no reader.”
“Lilas?”
“Couldn’t to save my life!”
“Go ahead, Ingred, and don’t waste time!” said Lispeth firmly.
===Chinese===
“你的琴谱我也捎来了！”弗朗茜说着，得意地翻开乐谱夹，“这下你再没借口说记不得什么了。谁来给她伴奏？喏，英格丽德！”
“哦！换别人准弹得比我好，”英格丽德推辞道，“珍妮——”
“我视谱不灵。”
“莉莉亚丝？”
“要我的命也弹不来！”
“就上吧，英格丽德，别耽搁工夫！”丽丝佩丝语气坚决。

===Original===
Ingred sat down to the piano without a smile. Her schoolmates took her unwillingness for modesty, but in her heart of hearts her main thought was: “Why should *I* help this new girl to show off?” She would have played accompaniments gladly for anybody else, but she considered that Bess had already received quite enough attention in one afternoon. For her own credit, however, she must do her best, so she concentrated her energies on the prelude. When the first strains of the violin joined in, her musical ear recognized immediately that Bess’s playing was of a very high quality. The tone was pure, the notes were perfectly in tune, and there was a ringing sweetness, a crisp power of expression, and a haunting pathos in the rendering of the melody that showed the performer to be capable of interpreting the composer’s meaning. In spite of her disinclination, Ingred warmed to the accompaniment. When the violin seemed to be bringing out laughter and tears, the piano must do its part, and not merely supply a succession of unimpassioned chords. Ingred was a good reader for a girl of fifteen, but she surpassed herself on this occasion, and seemed to accomplish the difficult passages almost by instinct. She played the final notes very softly as the last fairy strains of the melody thrilled slowly away.
There was a second of silence, then the girls, inside and outside the room, clapped their loudest.
“It was capital!” declared Lispeth encouragingly. “Bess, we shall want you again for school concerts. You and Ingred ought to practise together. Let me look at your violin. I wish *I* could play like that!”
“Thanks ever so much!” murmured Bess to Ingred, as the latter got up from the piano.
“Oh! it’s all right!” replied Ingred airily, moving away in a hurry to the other side of the room. She did not want Bess to take up Lispeth’s no doubt well meant but rather embarrassing suggestion that they should practise together, and was quite ready with an excuse if it should be proposed.
===Chinese===
英格丽德面无笑意地在琴前坐下。同学们只当她的推却是谦虚，可她内心深处转着的主要念头是：“凭什么要*我*帮这个新来的女生出风头？”换作任何旁人，她都乐意伴奏，只是她认定，一个下午下来，贝丝博得的关注已经够多了。然而为了自家的颜面，她必须全力以赴，于是把全副心力都凝在引子上。小提琴最初的乐声一加入，她那对懂音乐的耳朵立刻听出：贝丝的琴艺十分出众。音色纯正，音准分毫不差，旋律的演绎里有一种清越的甜美、一种干净利落的表现力，还有一缕萦绕不去的哀婉，足见奏琴的人领会得透作曲家的心意。任凭满心不愿，英格丽德的伴奏仍渐渐弹热了。琴声里既有欢笑也有泪影，钢琴便须尽到自己的一份，不能光冷冰冰地铺下一串无动于衷的和弦。就十五岁的姑娘而论，英格丽德的视奏本属上乘，这一回更是超出了自己，艰深的段落仿佛全凭本能便顺了过去。当旋律最后几缕仙音轻颤着徐徐消逝，她把末音弹得极轻极轻。
一秒钟的寂静，随后屋里屋外的姑娘们使出全身力气鼓起掌来。
“太精彩了！”丽丝佩丝鼓励道，“贝丝，学校音乐会往后还要借重你。你和英格丽德该搭伙练琴才是。让我瞧瞧你这把琴。巴不得*我*也能拉成那样！”
“多谢啦，太谢谢了！”英格丽德从琴凳上起身时，贝丝低声对她说。
“哦，没什么！”英格丽德轻描淡写地答道，急急忙忙躲到教室另一头去了。丽丝佩丝那句“该搭伙练琴”的提议，无疑出于好意，却颇叫人发窘；她可不想让贝丝顺势应下，倘若真被提起，借口她都已备好了。

===Original===
“It’s the turn of the Sixth now,” she jodelled.
“**Vb** haven’t done anything yet; I’ll call one of them in,” said Lispeth, stepping out to the landing.
Once through the door, however, her ears were assailed by such an absolute din proceeding from the farther end of the corridor, that she dropped her character of impresario for the duties of head-girl, and calling two of her fellow prefects, went to investigate the cause of the disturbance. She returned in a short time, looking flushed and flurried.
“It’s those wretched kids in **IVb**,” she proclaimed. “They were behaving disgracefully, pelting each other with the remains of their buns, and fencing with rulers. And they actually had the cheek to tell me they weren’t making any more noise than we were with our singing and playing! I sent them home at once, and I think we’d all better go too. Those intermediates always overstep the line if they’ve an atom of a chance. I told them what I thought about them. It’s been quite a ripping concert, and I’m sorry to break it up, but you understand, don’t you?”
“Rather!” replied the others, as they began their exodus into the corridor.
===Chinese===
“现在该轮到六年级了。”她扬着调门喊道。
“五乙班还一个节目没出呢，我去叫一位进来。”丽丝佩丝说着，迈步跨到楼道上。
谁知刚一出门，走廊远处那头传来的喧嚣便劈头盖脸灌进她的耳朵，闹得天翻地覆。她当即撇下音乐会承办人的身份，担起学生长（head girl）的职责，唤上两位级长同僚，前去查明骚乱的起因。不多时她回来了，满脸通红，又气又慌。
“是**四乙班**（IVb）那帮小祸害，”她宣布道，“闹得不成体统——拿吃剩的小圆面包互相乱扔，还拿尺子击剑对打。她们居然好意思跟我说，她们闹出的响动不比我们唱歌弹琴的大！我当即打发她们回家了，依我看，咱们大家也最好散场。那帮中年级学生，但凡有一丝半毫的机会，就准要越界胡闹。我把她们狠狠数落了一顿。音乐会开得实在精彩，我也舍不得就这么散了，可你们明白的，是不是？”
“那可不！”众人齐声应道，鱼贯而出，涌进楼道。

## XIX The Nun’s Walk / 第十九章 修女步道

===Original===
The Saxon family agreed that whatever might be the drawbacks of Wynch-on-the-Wold in wintry weather, it was an idyllic spot in the month of May. The wallflowers which Ingred had transplanted were now in their prime, the apple trees were in blossom, clumps of lilies were pushing up fast, and pink double daisies bordered the front walk. The woods in the combe below the moor were a mass of bluebells, and here and there those who searched might find rarer flowers, orchises, lily of the valley, and true lover’s knot. Friends who had shirked the journey while the winds blew cold, now began to drop in at the bungalow and take tea under the apple trees. Ingred, returning home on Friday afternoons, would find bicycles stacked by the gate and visitors seated in the garden. She greeted them with enthusiasm or the reverse, according to her individual tastes.
“Really, Ingred, they don’t seem to teach manners at the College now!” said Quenrede one day. “The way you scowled at Mrs. Galsworthy and Gertrude was most uncivil. You didn’t look in the very least pleased to see them.”
“I wasn’t! They’re the most stupid people on the face of the earth! And they stayed such ages. I thought they’d never go. Just when I wanted a nice private talk with you and Mother before the boys came back. Why should you look glad to see a person when you’re not?”
“For the sake of manners, my dear!”
===Chinese===
萨克森（Saxon）一家一致公认：沃尔德畔温奇（Wynch-on-the-Wold）这地方在寒冬时节纵有百般不便，到了五月却俨然一处世外桃源。英格丽德（Ingred）移栽的那些桂竹香如今开得正盛，苹果树繁花满枝，一丛丛百合蹿得飞快，粉色重瓣雏菊沿门前小径镶了一道花边。荒原脚下谷地里的树林成了一片蓝铃花的海洋，肯细细搜寻的人，还能在角落里寻见更稀罕的花——红门兰、铃兰，还有唤作“真爱结”的野花。寒风凛冽时躲着不肯上路的那些朋友，如今纷纷到“小平房”来串门，在苹果树下喝茶。英格丽德每逢星期五下午回家，总见大门口码着一排自行车，花园里坐着客人。她迎客热络与否，全看各人合不合她自己的口味。
“说真的，英格丽德，如今学院里看来是不教礼貌这门课了！”一天，奎恩丽德（Quenrede）说道，“你对加尔斯沃西太太（Mrs. Galsworthy）和格特鲁德（Gertrude）横眉立目的那副样子，实在太失礼了。你看上去一点儿也不像乐意见到她们。”
“我本来就不乐意！她们是天底下最无趣的人！还一坐老半天，我以为她们一辈子都不走了。偏偏那时候，我想趁男孩子们回来之前，跟你和妈妈好好说几句体己话。明明不乐意，凭什么还要装出乐意见人的样子？”
“为了礼数呀，亲爱的！”

===Original===
“Then manners really mean humbug,” declared Ingred, who loved to argue. “To say you’re glad to see people, when you’re not, is telling deliberate fibs. Most hypocritical, I call it! Why can’t people tell the truth?”
“Because it would generally be offensive and unkind to do so,” put in Mother, who happened to overhear. “There’s another side to the question, too. When you say﻿—against your will﻿—that you are glad to see somebody, you mean that all the *best* part of you is glad﻿—the kind, generous part that likes to give pleasure, not the selfish lower part that only thinks of its own convenience. So you are not really telling a fib, but being true to your nobler self. A great deal of what people call ‘plain speaking’ is simply giving rein to their most uncharitable thoughts. As a rule, I say Heaven defend me from those ultra-truthful souls who enjoy ‘speaking their minds.’ ”
“But are we to gush over every bore?” asked Ingred.
“There are limits, of course. We can’t let all our time be frittered away by idle friends, but we can generally manage tactfully without offending them. Don’t look so woebegone, childie! Nobody else is coming tonight, and I promise you tea in the woods tomorrow.”
===Chinese===
“这么说，礼数其实就是装腔作势喽。”最爱抬杠的英格丽德宣布道，“明明不乐意见人，嘴上却说高兴，那是存心扯谎。要我说，虚伪透顶！人为什么就不能讲实话？”
“因为讲实话多半伤人，可不厚道。”恰好在一旁听见的妈妈插话道，“这个问题还有另一面呢。当你违背本心、说见到某人很高兴时，你的意思是，你身上*最美好*的那一部分确实高兴——那个善良慷慨、乐于给人快乐的部分，而不是那个只图自己省事的自私的部分。所以这其实算不得扯谎，倒是不辜负你那个更高尚的自我。人们挂在嘴边的所谓‘有话直说’，十有八九不过是放任自己心里最刻薄的念头罢了。依我看，那些以‘心直口快’为乐的过分老实人，还是请老天爷保佑我躲得远远的吧！”
“可难道碰上个闷葫芦也得满脸堆笑？”英格丽德问。
“分寸自然还是有的。咱们的时间不能全让闲客们耗光，但办法总归有，既周旋过去，又不得罪人。别哭丧着脸啦，小乖乖！今晚不会再有人来了，我向你保证，明天咱们到树林里喝茶。”

===Original===
“By ourselves?”
“Unless anyone very nice comes over to join us,” put in Quenrede quickly.
“You girls shall give the invitations. I won’t bring any middle-aged people,” laughed Mother, with a sly glance at Quenrede.
===Chinese===
“就咱们自己？”
“除非有什么顶可爱的人过来凑热闹。”奎恩丽德连忙插嘴。
“发请帖的事就交给你们姑娘家。中年人我一个也不带。”妈妈笑道，朝奎恩丽德狡黠地瞟了一眼。

===Original===
The party in the bluebell woods on Saturday was entirely a family one, with the exception of Mr. Broughten, who rode over on a motor-bicycle ostensibly to lend some microscopic slides to Athelstane, though Ingred suspected there was another attraction in the visit. Quenrede, who professed great surprise, gave him a guarded welcome.
“After all the fuss you made about my manners yesterday, you might have seemed more glad to see him,” sniffed Ingred critically.
“Might I? Well, really, I think I’m going to hang a label round my neck: ‘Pleased to meet you! Let ’em all come!’ It would save trouble. Stick tight to me when we’re gathering bluebells. Three’s better company sometimes than two. Don’t I like him? Oh yes, he’s all right, but I’m not keen on a tête-à-tête.”
===Chinese===
星期六蓝铃树林里的这场茶会完全是家庭聚会，唯一的例外是布劳顿先生（Mr. Broughten）。他骑着一辆摩托车过来，名义上是给阿特尔斯坦（Athelstane）送几片显微镜切片，不过英格丽德疑心这趟拜访另有更撩人的缘由。奎恩丽德口称大感意外，接待他时不冷不热。
“昨天你还为我的礼貌大事数落，轮到你自己，见了他倒满可以显得热络些嘛。”英格丽德挑剔地哼了一声。
“我可以吗？好啊，说真的，我看干脆在我脖子上挂块牌子得了：‘幸会幸会！来者不拒！’——省得麻烦。采蓝铃花的时候，你可要寸步不离地粘着我。有时候，三个人做伴强过两个人。我喜欢他吗？哦，喜欢，他人是不错，可我还没打算跟他两个人对坐谈心呢。”

===Original===
After which hint, Ingred, who had some acquaintance with the perversity of Quenrede’s feminine mind, did exactly the opposite, and, abandoning her basket to the custody of Mr. Broughten, left him helping her sister to gather bluebells, and took herself off with Hereward.
“He’s not half bad!” she ruminated laughingly. “Not of course a fairy prince exactly, or even a Member of Parliament, but the bubbles on the pool by the whispering stones certainly came to ‘*J*,’ and his name is ‘John,’ for I asked Athelstane. There’s the finger of fate about it, and Queenie had better make up her mind.”
With Ingred, however, school matters were at present much more interesting than speculating about her sister’s possible future. It was an interesting term at the College. Cricket and tennis were in full swing, and she took an active part in both. The best of being at the hostel was that the boarders had the benefit of the tennis courts in the evening, and so secured an advantage in the matter of practice over any girls who did not possess a private court at home. So far the College had not competed in tournaments, but Blossom Webster was hopeful that later on in the term some champions might be chosen who would not disgrace the Games Club. Meantime she urged everybody to practice, and coached her favorites with the eye of an expert. Nora was particularly marked out for future distinction. She had made tremendous strides lately, and her swift serves were the terror of her opponents. The hostel felt justly proud of her achievements, and would collect in the evening, after prep., to watch her play a set of singles with Susie Wakefield, who, though older and taller, almost invariably lost.
===Chinese===
得了这句提示，深知奎恩丽德女儿家心思之别扭的英格丽德，偏偏反其道而行之：她把篮子一撂，扔给布劳顿先生保管，任他陪着她姐姐采蓝铃花，自己拉着赫里沃德（Hereward）走开了。
“这人还真不赖！”她笑吟吟地思忖道，“当然，算不得童话里的王子，连国会议员也算不上，可风哨石旁那汪水潭里冒的泡泡，分明数到了‘*J*’字，而他的名字正是‘约翰（John）’——这是我问过阿特尔斯坦的。这里头自有命运之手的拨弄，奎妮（Queenie）姑娘最好早点拿定主意。”
不过对英格丽德来说，眼下学校里的事，可比揣测姐姐将来的归宿有意思得多。这个学期，学院里好戏连台。板球和网球都打得火热，两样她都是积极分子。住寄宿舍最大的好处，是寄宿生晚上可以独享网球场，比那些家里没有私人球场的姑娘多占了大把练习上的便宜。学院至今还没参加过锦标赛，不过布洛瑟姆·韦伯斯特（Blossom Webster）满怀希望，认为学期晚些时候总能选出几名不致辱没体育俱乐部（Games Club）名头的选手。眼下她催着大家勤加练习，又以行家的眼光调教她看中的人。诺拉（Nora）尤其被看好日后能崭露头角。她近来进步神速，那手迅雷般的发球成了对手们的噩梦。寄宿舍上下为她战绩自豪得理直气壮，每晚自习一结束，大家便聚拢来看她跟苏西·韦克菲尔德（Susie Wakefield）打一盘单打——苏西年纪更大、个子更高，却十有八九输球。

===Original===
Susie had good points of her own, however, and with Nora as partner could beat even Blossom and Aline occasionally. No doubt the future credit of the school was in their hands.
One evening it happened that Nora was in a particularly slashing and reckless mood, and she sent no less than three balls flying straight over the wall that bordered the tennis courts. They fell into the premises of old Dr. Broadfield, whose garden adjoined that of the school. They were not the first that had done so, indeed so many balls had gone over lately that the loss was growing serious. At one time the girls had been wont to ring Dr. Broadfield’s front-door bell and beg permission to pick up their property, but they had been received so sourly by his elderly housekeeper, that they hardly dared to ask again.
“Three good balls gone in half an hour!” grieved Verity. “There’ll soon be none left at this rate. I believe there must be a dozen at least lying on the grass over there, only that stingy old thing won’t throw them back. It’s really too bad.”
“How could we possibly get them?” ruminated Doreen.
===Chinese===
不过苏西也有自己的长处，与诺拉搭伙，偶尔连布洛瑟姆和艾琳（Aline）都能打赢。学校将来的荣光，无疑就攥在她们二人手里。
一天晚上，诺拉的球风偏偏又凶又莽，竟一口气把三个球直接抽出了球场边的围墙。球全落进了老布罗德菲尔德医生（Dr. Broadfield）的地界——他家的花园正挨着学校的园子。这也不是头一回了，近来飞过去的球实在太多，损失日渐惨重。起初，姑娘们还惯于去按布罗德菲尔德医生家的门铃，恳求准许捡回自家的物件，可他那位上了年纪的女管家回回都把脸拉得老长，她们几乎不敢再登门了。
“半个钟头就折了三个好球！”薇丽蒂（Verity）痛心疾首，“照这个速度，用不了多久就要弹尽粮绝。我看那边的草地上起码躺着十来个球，偏那老抠门儿就是不肯扔回来。真是气死人了。”
“有什么法子能弄回来呢？”多琳（Doreen）盘算着。

===Original===
“Sham ill, get Dr. Broadfield to attend, and coax them out of him,” suggested Fil.
Doreen shook her head.
“He’s not the school doctor, unfortunately. When Millie sprained her ankle, Miss Burd sent for Dr. Harrison. We might fish for them with a butterfly net tied to the end of a drilling pole, if they’re anywhere near enough.”
“They’re not. I peeped over the wall and they’ve rolled quite a long way off.”
===Chinese===
“装病，请布罗德菲尔德医生来出诊，再从他嘴里把球哄出来。”菲尔（Fil）出主意。
多琳摇了摇头。
“可惜他不是校医。上回米莉（Millie）扭了脚脖子，伯德小姐（Miss Burd）请的是哈里森医生（Dr. Harrison）。要是球滚得够近，倒可以把捕蝶网绑在操练杆头上，像钓鱼似的把它们捞回来。”
“滚得不够近。我从墙头上偷瞧过了，球滚出去老远。”

===Original===
“How weak! What are we to do?”
“There’s nothing for it,” said Ingred slowly, “but to make a sally into the enemy’s trenches and fetch them back!”
“Oh! I dare say! But who’s going to do the sallying business?”
“*I* will, if you like.”
===Chinese===
“真不济！那可怎么办？”
“没办法了，”英格丽德慢悠悠地说，“只有杀进敌军战壕，把球夺回来！”
“哦！说得好听！可这趟杀敌的差事谁去干？”
“*我*去，只要你们乐意。”

===Original===
“*You!*”
“Yes; I don’t mind a scrap.”
“You heroine!”
“Don’t mensh!”
“But suppose you’re caught?”
“I shall have to risk that, of course. I’ll reconnoiter carefully first.”
===Chinese===
“*你*！”
“对；真干上一架我也不怵。”
“你真是女中豪杰！”
“别客气啦！”
“可万一被逮着了呢？”
“那当然只好豁出去赌一把。我会先仔细侦察一番。”

===Original===
The boundary between the College premises and the property of Dr. Broadfield was part of the old Abbey wall. The mortar had crumbled away from the stones, leaving large interstices, so it was quite easy to climb. With a little boosting from Verity and Nora, Ingred successfully reached the top, and peered over into the neighboring garden. Just below her was a rockery, which offered not only an easy means of descent, but a quick mode of egress in the case of the necessity of beating a hasty retreat.
Beyond the flowerbed, and lying on the lawn, were no less than seven tennis balls, marked with the unmistakable blue cross that claimed them for the College. The sight was enough to spur on the faintest heart. Apparently there was nobody in this part of the garden, and no watchful face peered from any of the windows. It was certainly an opportunity that ought not to be missed. Ingred slipped first one foot and then the other over the wall, and dropped on to the rockery. It was the work of a minute to pick up the balls and throw them back to rejoicing friends. If she herself had followed immediately there would have been no sequel to the episode. But happening to look under the bushes, she noticed another ball, and went in quest of it. It seemed a shame to return until she had found any that might have strayed farther afield, so she dived under the rhododendron bushes, and was rewarded with two more balls. She had issued out on to another part of the lawn, and was on the very point of retreating, when she suddenly heard voices on the path between the bushes. To run to the wall would be to cross open country, so, with an instinctive desire to seek cover, she dived into a summerhouse close by, and shut the door. The footsteps came nearer. Were they going to follow her into her retreat, and catch her? It would be too ignominious! Peeping warily through a small window of the summerhouse, she saw two young people, apparently much interested in each other, strolling leisurely up. To her immense relief they did not attempt to enter, but sat down on a seat outside the window. They were so near that she could perforce hear every word, and was an unwilling but compulsory eavesdropper.
At first the conversation consisted mostly of tender nothings: “He” certainly called her “Darling!”; “She” replied: “Oh, Donald, don’t!” and a sound followed so suspiciously like a kiss that Ingred, only a few feet away from them, almost giggled aloud. She wondered how long they were going to keep her a prisoner. It might be very pleasant for themselves to sit “spooning” in the garden on a mild May evening, but if they prolonged their enjoyment beyond eight o’clock, the hostel supper-bell would ring, and any girl not in her place at the table would lose a mark for punctuality.
===Chinese===
学院地界与布罗德菲尔德医生家产业的分界，是一段修道院（the Abbey）的古墙。石缝间的灰浆早已酥碎剥落，留下一个个大豁口，攀爬起来毫不费劲。薇丽蒂和诺拉在下面托了两把，英格丽德便稳稳当当登上墙头，朝隔壁园子里张望。她脚下正对着一座假山石，不但是条现成的下坡路，万一需要仓皇撤退，也是条便捷的逃生道。
花坛那边的草坪上，端端正正躺着不下七个网球，个个画着学院标记的蓝十字，一望便知归属。这幅景象，胆子最小的人见了也要奋勇向前。园子这一角显然空无一人，也没有哪扇窗后探出一张警戒的脸。这等机会，万万错过不得。英格丽德先把一只脚、再把另一只脚翻过墙头，落到了假山石上。拾起球来扔回给墙那边欢呼的伙伴，不过是一分钟的工夫。倘若她自己也立刻翻回去，这事本可再无下文。可她偏偏往灌木底下瞄了一眼，又瞧见一个球，便寻了过去。不把散落得更远的球全数找回就撤，未免可惜，于是她钻进杜鹃花丛，果然又得了两个。她钻出来走到草坪另一头，正要撤退，忽然听见灌木间小径上传来说话声。这时往墙边跑，等于横穿一片开阔地，她出于寻遮蔽的本能，一头扎进近旁一座凉亭，掩上了门。脚步声越来越近。他们会不会一路追进她的藏身之处，把她逮个正着？那可太丢人了！她提心吊胆地从凉亭的小窗往外窥探，看见两个年轻人悠悠然踱来，彼此显然情意正浓。令她大大松了一口气的是，两人并无进门之意，却在窗外一条长椅上坐了下来。距离近得她不得不字字入耳，成了一个心不甘情不愿却又躲不开的偷听者。
起初，两人的谈话大半是甜腻腻的空话：“他”一口一个“亲爱的！”；“她”则答“哦，唐纳德（Donald），别这样！”紧接着响起的那一声，可疑得活脱脱像是一个吻，害得几步之外的英格丽德差点笑出声来。她纳闷这两位打算把她囚禁到几时。五月的和煦夜晚坐在园子里卿卿我我，他们自是惬意，可这番享受若拖过八点，寄宿舍的晚饭铃一响，凡是不在自己座位上的姑娘，守时一栏都要扣掉一分。

===Original===
“He” on the other side of the window, was waxing sentimental about old times and bygone days.
“I’m glad you’re not a nun, darling!” he remarked fatuously. “If you had lived in the ancient Abbey, I shouldn’t have been able to walk about the garden with you, should I?”
“I suppose not,” she ventured, “especially if you’d been a monk.”
“I dare say some of them *did* manage to do a little lovemaking sometimes, though. What’s that story about the ghost?”
“The White Nun, do you mean? The one that haunts the College gardens?”
===Chinese===
窗外那位“他”，正对着往昔岁月大发感伤。
“真高兴你没去当修女，亲爱的！”他傻气十足地感叹道，“你要是当年住在这座古修道院里，我就不能陪你在这园子里散步了，对不对？”
“我想是不能，”她小心翼翼地应道，“尤其要是你当年当了修士的话。”
“不过我敢说，他们里头有些人*确曾*有法子时不时谈上一点儿恋爱。那个鬼故事是怎么说的来着？”
“你是说白衣修女（The White Nun）？就是常在学院花园里出没的那一位？”

===Original===
(Ingred pricked up her ears at this).
“Yes. Isn’t there some legend or other about her?”
“I believe there is, but I’ve forgotten it. I only know she walks on moonlight nights, down the steps by the sundial, and then disappears into the wall near the Abbey. At least she’s supposed to. I’ve never met anybody who’s seen her. Don’t talk of such shuddery things! You make me feel creepy!”
Apparently he offered masculine protection, for another suggestive sound was followed by a giggle and a remonstrance. The hostel bell was ringing, and the Abbey clock was striking eight. Were they going to stay talking all night? Ingred was growing desperate. She wondered how she was going to explain her absence to Mrs. Best. She even debated whether it would be advisable to open the summerhouse door, bolt across the lawn, and trust to luck that the matter was not reported at the College. She had her hand on the latch when the feminine voice outside remarked:
“It’s getting chilly, Donald!”
===Chinese===
（英格丽德听到这里，竖起了耳朵。）
“是啊。关于她，不是流传着什么传说吗？”
“好像是有一个，可我忘了。我只知道她会在月光夜里出来行走，顺着日晷旁的台阶走下来，随后隐入修道院近旁的墙里。至少人家都这么说。我可从没见过哪个真见过她的人。别说这些吓人的事了！听得我浑身发毛！”
他显然适时献上了男子汉的护佑，又一声意味深长的响动过后，换来一声轻笑和一句嗔怪。寄宿舍的铃响了，修道院的钟敲了八下。他们莫非要在这儿聊上一整夜？英格丽德渐渐急得没了法子。她盘算着怎么向贝斯特太太（Mrs. Best）解释自己的缺席，甚至权衡过要不要干脆推开凉亭的门、飞奔横穿草坪、听天由命赌这事不会捅到学院那边去。她的手已经搭上了门闩，窗外那个女声开口了：
“起凉风了，唐纳德！”

===Original===
“Don’t catch cold, darling!” with tender solicitude. “Would you rather go indoors?”
“Hooray!” triumphed Ingred inwardly, though she did not dare to utter a sound.
It took a little while for the lovers to get under way and finally stroll back along the path among the bushes. Ingred gave them time to walk out of sight and hearing, then made a dash for the rockery, scrambled over the wall, tore across the tennis courts, and entered the dining-room nearly ten minutes late for supper. Mrs. Best looked at her reproachfully, and Doreen, who was monitress for the month, took a notebook from her pocket and made an entry therein. Nora and Verity and Fil went on eating sago blancmange with stolid countenances that betrayed no knowledge of their roommate’s doings, but that night, when The Foursomes met in the privacy of Dormitory 2, they demanded an account of her adventure.
She certainly had a piece of interesting news to confide.
===Chinese===
“可别着凉了，亲爱的！”他体贴入微地说，“要不要进屋里去？”
“乌拉！”英格丽德在心里欢呼凯旋，嘴上却半点不敢出声。
这对情侣磨蹭了好一阵，才总算沿着灌木间的小径踱了回去。英格丽德等到他们走得看不见、听不见，才扑向假山石，手脚并用地翻过墙，一阵风似的冲过网球场，进饭厅时晚饭已迟到了将近十分钟。贝斯特太太责备地望了她一眼；当月值周生多琳从口袋里掏出记事本，记上了一笔。诺拉、薇丽蒂和菲尔面色如常地继续吃她们的西米牛奶冻，对室友的行踪不露半点知情之色。可当天夜里，四人同盟（The Foursomes）在二号寝室（Dormitory 2）里关起门来碰头，便逼她交代这番冒险的始末。
她还真有一条有趣的消息要私下相告。

===Original===
“Did you know that a ghost haunts the garden?”
“No! Oh, I say, where?”
“That part by the sundial. I’ve heard it called ‘The Nun’s Walk!’ ”
“So have I; but I never knew there was a ghost!”
“It’s supposed to walk on moonlight nights.”
===Chinese===
“你们知道花园里闹鬼吗？”
“不知道！哎呀，快说，在哪儿？”
“日晷旁边那一片。我听人管它叫‘修女步道（The Nun’s Walk）’！”
“这名字我也听说过，可从来不知道那儿有鬼！”
“据说她专在月光夜里出来行走。”

===Original===
“How fearfully thrillsome!”
“I’ve never seen a ghost!” shivered Fil.
“No more have I﻿—and I’ve never met anyone who exactly has. It’s generally their cousin’s cousin who’s told them about it.”
“There’s a moon tonight,” remarked Nora.
“So there is!”
===Chinese===
“多么惊险刺激呀！”
“我可从没见过鬼！”菲尔打了个寒噤。
“我也没有——而且从没见过谁是真的亲眼见过。一般来讲，都是他们表姐的表姐讲给他们听的。”
“今晚有月亮。”诺拉说。
“真的呢！”

===Original===
The four girls looked at one another, hair brushes in hand. Each had it on the tip of her tongue to make a suggestion.
“I *dare* you to go!” said Verity at last.
“Not alone?”
Fil was clutching already at Nora’s hand.
“Well, no! Hardly alone. I vote we all go together and try if we can see anything.”
===Chinese===
