四个姑娘手握发刷，面面相觑。每人心里都有个提议，正卡在嘴边。
“*谅*你不敢去！”薇丽蒂终于开了口。
“不一个人去？”
菲尔早已一把攥住了诺拉的手。
“呃，不！总不能一个人去。我提议咱们四个一起去，看看能不能见着点什么。”

===Original===
“It would be rather spooksomely jinky!”
“Well, look here, don’t let’s undress properly, but get into bed, and cover ourselves up until Nurse has been her rounds, then we’ll slip downstairs and out through the side door into the garden. Are you game?”
“Who’s afraid?” said Ingred valiantly.
Upstairs in their bedroom, with the gas turned on, it was easy enough to feel courageous. Their spirits rose indeed at the prospect of such an adventure. Nurse Warner, who came into the room a little later, looked round at the four beds, turned out the gas, and departed without a suspicion. She had not been gone five minutes when a surreptitious dressing took place, and four figures in dark coats stole down the stairs. Though the building of the College might be absolutely modern, the garden was a relic of medieval days. It had formerly belonged to the nunnery of St. Mary’s, and had adjoined the Abbey. Parts of the crumbling old wall were still left, and a flagged path led from a sundial to some ruins. In the daytime it was a cheerful place, and a blaze of color. The girls had never before seen it in its night aspect. On this May evening it had a quiet beauty that was most impressive. The full moon shone on the great dark pile of the Abbey towers and the beech avenue beyond. There was just light enough in the garden to distinguish bushes as heavy masses, and to trace the paths from the grass. The air was sweet with the scent of flowers.
===Chinese===
“又鬼气森森又带劲，多妙呀！”
“这么着，咱们先别正经脱衣裳，和衣上床，蒙上被子等护士查完房，再悄悄溜下楼，从边门出去进花园。你们干不干？”
“谁怕谁？”英格丽德豪气干云。
在楼上亮着煤气灯的卧室里，胆气自然说来就来。一想到这样一场冒险，众人兴致愈发高涨。过了片刻，沃纳护士（Nurse Warner）进屋来，朝四张床扫了一圈，关掉煤气灯，毫不起疑地走了。她前脚离开不到五分钟，一场偷偷摸摸的穿衣行动便开始了，四个裹着深色外衣的身影蹑手蹑脚地溜下楼去。学院的楼宇虽是彻头彻尾的现代建筑，花园却是中世纪留下的遗物。它旧属圣玛丽女修道院（St. Mary’s），紧傍着修道院。残颓的老墙还剩下几段，一条石板小径从日晷通向几处废墟。白天里，这是个明艳欢快的去处，花团锦簇、姹紫嫣红。姑娘们还从未见过它夜晚的面容。在这个五月的夜晚，它自有一种令人心折的静谧之美。满月照着修道院塔楼巨大的黑影，也照着更远处的山毛榉林荫道。园中光线恰到好处，刚够把灌木辨成一团团浓重的暗影，把小径从草地间勾出来。空气里漾着花香，甜丝丝的。

===Original===
It is amazing how different conditions can alter a scene: at noon, with the hum from the busy streets, it was commonplace enough; by moonlight it became a mystic bower of enchantment. The girls walked along very quietly, treading on the grass so as to make no noise. A slight mist was rising from the ground near the Abbey; in the rays of the moon it resembled a lake. Everything, indeed, was altered. The outline of the sumach bush was like a crouching tiger; the laburnum tassels waved like skeleton fingers. It seemed a witching, unreal world.
Four rather scared girls crept along, clasping hands for moral support. Each secretly would have been relieved to abandon the quest, but did not like to be the first to turn tail. They had determined to walk from the sundial to the Abbey wall and back again. So far the garden, though mysterious, showed no signs of anything supernatural. They began to pluck up courage, and even to talk to one another in low whispers. At the ruins they turned and looked back towards the sundial. The moonlight streamed along the flagged path, and shimmered on the clumps of early yellow lilies.
What was that, stealing from under the shelter of the hawthorn tree? The girls gasped and almost stopped breathing.
===Chinese===
光景的变换竟能令景色改换如许，实在奇妙：正午时分，隔着街市的嗡嗡市声望去，它平庸无奇；到了月下，它便成了一座勾魂摄魄的魔法秘境。姑娘们悄没声儿地走着，专拣草地落脚，生怕弄出响动。修道院附近的地面浮起一层薄雾，在月光里宛如一泓湖水。一切都变了模样：盐肤木的轮廓像一只蹲伏的猛虎，金链花的花串摇曳如枯骨的手指。这仿佛是一个中了魔的、不真实的世界。
四个心里发怵的姑娘手拉着手，互相壮着胆子往前蹭。每个人私底下都巴不得就此罢手，只是谁也不肯第一个当逃兵。她们已打定主意，从日晷走到修道院墙根，再走回来。到目前为止，园子尽管神秘，却不见半点灵异迹象。她们的胆子渐渐壮了起来，甚至开始交头接耳地低语。到了废墟跟前，她们转身回望日晷的方向。月光顺着石板路流淌，在几丛早开的黄百合上闪闪发亮。
那是什么，从山楂树的荫蔽底下悄悄溜了出来？姑娘们倒抽一口冷气，呼吸几乎都停住了。

===Original===
A tall figure, clothed in some long white garment, was gliding towards them. It kept in the shadow, and they could see no details, only a light mass that was slowly and steadily advancing apparently straight to where they were crouching beside the wall. Fil was trembling like a leaf, Nora declared afterward that her hair stood on end, Ingred and Verity felt shivers run down their spines. Nearer and nearer came the white figure. Its approach was more than flesh and blood could stand. With a wild shriek Fil dashed across the lawn, followed closely by Nora, Ingred, and Verity.
“Girls!” cried a clear and well-known voice. “Girls! Stop! What are you doing here?”
There was no mistaking the tone of command of the headmistress. Four amazed and crestfallen damsels halted and turned back, to find Miss Burd, attired in a white dressing-gown, standing in the moonlight on the grass.
“What is the meaning of this?” she asked. “And why aren’t you all in bed?”
===Chinese===
一个高挑的身影，裹着长长的白袍，正朝她们这边滑行而来。它始终藏在暗影里，看不真切，只见一团白影缓缓地、稳稳地逼近，径直冲着她们贴墙蹲伏的地方而来。菲尔抖得像风里的叶子，诺拉后来一口咬定自己当时毛发倒竖，英格丽德和薇丽蒂只觉寒战顺着脊梁骨直往下窜。白衣身影越来越近。它这般步步进逼，血肉之躯实难承受。菲尔一声狂叫，率先冲过草坪，诺拉、英格丽德和薇丽蒂紧随其后。
“姑娘们！”一个清亮又熟悉的声音喊道，“姑娘们！站住！你们在这儿干什么？”
那是女校长号令的口吻，谁也错认不了。四个又惊又丧气的姑娘收住脚步，讪讪地回转身来，只见伯德小姐一身白色晨衣，站在月光下的草地上。
“这是怎么回事？”她问，“你们几个为什么都还不上床睡觉？”

===Original===
It is always difficult to give explanations, and (to such a matter-of-fact person as Miss Burd) it seemed particularly silly to have to confess that they had come out ghost-hunting, and had mistaken her for a spirit. She emptied the vials of her scorn upon their dejected heads.
“Don’t let me hear of any more nonsense of this sort!” she finished. “I should have thought you were too intelligent to believe in such rubbish. As for leaving your dormitory at this hour, you deserve to be locked in the cycle-shed for the night. I shall, of course, report you to Mrs. Best, and none of you will play tennis for a week, as a punishment.”
Miss Burd, bristling with anger, swept the delinquents before her to the door of the hostel, and watched them flee upstairs, then went to lay the matter before Mrs. Best.
In Dormitory 2, four girls got into bed at topmost speed.
===Chinese===
解释原是难事，而对伯德小姐这样一位一是一、二是二的人来说，不得不招认她们是出来捉鬼、还把她本人错当成了鬼，未免格外愚蠢。她将满怀的轻蔑劈头盖脸倾泻到她们垂头丧气的脑袋上。
“这类蠢事，别让我再听说第二回！”她收了场，“我本还当你们够聪明，不至于信这种无聊鬼话。至于深更半夜擅离寝室——就该把你们锁进自行车棚里过夜才对。我自然会把这事报告贝斯特太太，你们几个禁打一星期网球，权作惩罚。”
伯德小姐怒气冲冲，把一干闯祸的丫头驱赶到寄宿舍门前，看着她们逃上楼去，方才转身去向贝斯特太太禀明原委。
二号寝室里，四个姑娘以最快的速度钻进了被窝。

===Original===
“Of all the ill-luck!” mourned Fil.
“I didn’t know Miss Burd prowled about the garden in a dressing-gown,” exclaimed Ingred.
“She *did* look exactly like a ghost!” confirmed Verity.
“Tennis off for a whole week! Blossom will be furious! It’s too absolutely grizzly for anything!” groused Nora. “I wish the wretched old ghost had been at Jericho before we went to look for it!”
===Chinese===
“真是倒霉到家了！”菲尔哀叹道。
“我哪知道伯德小姐会穿着晨衣在花园里转悠啊。”英格丽德叫起屈来。
“她刚才那样子*真*像鬼！”薇丽蒂作证道。
“一整个星期不准打网球！布洛瑟姆非气炸了不可！简直糟透了，没有比这更惨的了！”诺拉嘟嘟囔囔，“我巴不得咱们去找它之前，那讨厌的老鬼早就被发配到爪哇国去了！”

## XIV The Peephole / XIV 窥孔

===Original===
The Foursome League met in Dormitory 2 after the holidays with much clattering of tongues. Each wanted to tell her own experience, and they all talked at once. Fil had a new way of doing her hair, and gave the others no peace till they had duly realized and appreciated it. Verity had been bridesmaid to a cousin, and wished to give full details of the wedding; Nora had played hockey in a Scotch team against a Ladies’ Club, and had been promised ten minutes in an aeroplane, but the weather had been too stormy for the flight; the disappointment﻿—when she happened to remember it﻿—quite weighed down her spirits.
“If there’s one thing on earth﻿—or rather on air﻿—I’d like to be, it’s a flying woman!” she told her friends emphatically. “I’m hoping aeroplanes will get a little cheaper some day, and rich people will keep them instead of motor cars. Then I’ll go out as an aviatress. It’s a new career for women.”
“I wouldn’t trust myself to *your* tender mercies, thank you!” shuddered Ingred. “You’d soon bring the machine down with a crash, and smash us to smithereens.”
“Indeed I shouldn’t! I’d go sailing about like a bird!”
And Nora, suiting action to words, stood on her bed fluttering her arms, till Verity wickedly gave her a push behind, and sent her springing with more force than grace to the floor.
“You Jumbo! You make the room shake!” exclaimed Ingred. “If that’s how you’re going to land you’ll dig a hole in the ground like a bomb! Do move out, and let me get to my drawer! You’re growing too big for this bedroom!”
===Chinese===
假期过后，四人同盟（The Foursome League）在二号寝室（Dormitory 2）重聚，一个个叽叽喳喳，热闹非凡。人人都想讲讲自己的见闻，于是七嘴八舌，一齐开腔。菲尔（Fil）梳了个新发式，不把大家折腾到正眼端详、交口称赞一番，绝不肯罢休。薇丽蒂（Verity）给表姐当过伴娘，巴不得把婚礼的种种细情一五一十说个痛快；诺拉（Nora）随一支苏格兰球队上阵，同一家女子俱乐部打了曲棍球，人家还许她乘十分钟飞机，偏偏天公不作美，风雨太紧，没能起飞——这份懊恼，每逢碰巧想起来，都压得她闷闷不乐。
“要说这世上——不对，该说这天上——我最想当什么，那就是会飞的女人！”她郑重其事地对朋友们宣告，“我盼着飞机有朝一日能便宜点儿，阔人们不用汽车、改养飞机。到那时候，我就出门去当女飞行家。这可是女人的新行当。”
“多谢了，我可不敢把自己交到*你*那副慈悲心肠手里！”英格丽德（Ingred）打了个寒噤，“不出多会儿，你就会轰隆一声把飞机摔下来，把咱们俩摔成齑粉。”
“我才不会呢！我要像鸟儿一样满天翱翔！”
诺拉说着便身体力行，站上床去扑腾起两条胳膊，冷不防薇丽蒂使坏从背后一推，把她搡下床来——那一下落地，劲头十足，姿态却谈不上半分优雅。
“好一头大象！震得满屋子直晃！”英格丽德嚷道，“照这么降落，你准会像颗炸弹似的在地上砸出个坑来！快挪开，让我开抽屉！你这块头，这间寝室都快盛不下啦！”

===Original===
“Nobody’s looked at my new hair ribbons yet!” interposed Fil’s plaintive voice. “See, I’ve got six! Aren’t they beauties! Pale pink, pale blue, Saxe blue, navy for my gym. costume, black for a useful one, and olive green to go with my velveteen Sunday dress. Don’t you think they’re nice?”
“Ripping!” agreed Nora. “We’ll know where to go when we want to borrow. There, don’t look so scared, Baby! I’ve chopped my hair so short I couldn’t wear a ribbon if I tried! It would be off in three cracks! Stick them back in their box, and don’t tempt me! They’re not in my line! I’m going in for uniform. *You*’re the sort who wears chiffons and laces and all the rest of it, but you’ll see *me* in gilt buttons before I have done, with wings on them, I hope! I may be the first to fly to Mars! Who knows? You shall all have my photo beforehand just in case!”
Everybody at the College, and particularly at the Hostel, agreed that the first few weeks of the new term were trying. After the interval of the holidays, the yoke of homework seemed doubly heavy, and undoubtedly the prep. was stiffer than ever. Only certain hours were set apart for study during the evenings at the hostel, and any girl who could not accomplish her lessons in that time had to finish them as best she could in odd minutes during the day, or even in bed in the mornings if she happened to wake sufficiently early. Fil, who generally succeeded in mastering about half her preparation and no more, railed at fate.
“I’m so unlucky!” she sighed to a sympathetic audience in No. 2. “I knew the first ten lines of my French poetry beautifully, and I could have said them if Mademoiselle had asked me, but of course she didn’t. She set me on those wretched irregular verbs, and they always floor me utterly. As for the *dictée*﻿—I can’t spell in English﻿—let alone French! It’s not the least use for Mademoiselle to get excited and stamp her foot at me. I shall be glad when I’m old enough to leave school. I never mean to look at a French book again!”
“How about English spelling?” suggested Ingred. “You’ll want to write a letter occasionally!”
“I think by that time,” said Fil hopefully, “somebody will have invented a typewriter that can spell for itself. You’ll just press a knob for each word, you know!”
===Chinese===
“还没有一个人瞧过我的新发带呢！”菲尔委屈巴巴的声音插进来，“看，我买了六根！漂亮不漂亮？浅粉的、浅蓝的、萨克森蓝的，配体操服的藏青色，顶顶耐用的黑色，还有配我那条平绒礼拜日裙子的橄榄绿。你们说好不好看？”
“妙极了！”诺拉随声附和，“往后想借发带，可知道该上哪儿借了。好啦，别怕成那样，小宝贝！我的头发剪得毛短，想扎发带也扎不住哇！一转眼就得崩飞！快把它们收回盒子里去，别勾我动心！那玩意儿不合我的路数！我要穿的是制服。*你*这种人尽爱薄纱花边那一套，往后走着瞧，我不穿上带镀金纽扣的制服决不罢休——但愿那纽扣上还镶着翅膀呢！头一个飞上火星的说不定就是我！谁说得准呢？到时候我先给你们每人留张照片，以防万一！”
学院里人人——寄宿舍里尤其如此——都承认，新学期开头的几个星期实在难熬。隔了一个假期，功课这副枷锁显得加倍沉重，预习无疑也比以往更吃紧。寄宿舍每晚只划出固定的几个钟头用来自修，谁要是在这段时间里完不成功课，就得白天见缝插针地赶，或是清早醒得够早的话，干脆躺在床上补。菲尔的预习向来只能拿下一半，再多的没有，于是免不了怨天尤命。
“我的运气怎么这样坏！”她对着二号寝室一众好心的听众哀声叹气，“法语诗头十行我背得滚瓜烂熟，法语老师要是叫我背，我保管一字不错，可她偏偏没叫。她专挑那些可恶的不规则动词来考我，我一碰上它们就一败涂地。至于*法语听写*——我连英语都拼不对，更甭提法语了！法语老师急了跺脚也全然白搭。等我到了年龄、能离校的那天，我才高兴呢。往后我一眼也不再看法国书！”
“那英语拼写怎么办？”英格丽德提醒她，“你总免不了偶尔要写写信吧！”
“我想等到那个时候，”菲尔满怀指望地说，“总有人发明出一种自己会拼写的打字机了。每打一个词，只要按一个键就成，你说是不是！”

===Original===
“There are about 3,000 words in common daily use!” laughed Verity. “If you need a knob for each, your typewriter will have to be the size of a church organ. It’ll want a room to itself!”
“Oh, but think of the convenience of it! No more hunting in the dictionary!” declared Fil.
To add to the aggravations of the new term the weather was doubtful, and seemed to take a spiteful pleasure in being particularly wet on hockey afternoons. Day after day, disappointed girls would watch the streaming rain and lament the lack of practice. To give them some form of exercise they were assembled in the gymnasium, and held rival displays of Indian clubs, Morris dancing, or even skipping. “The True Blues” excelled at high jumping, “The Pioneers” at certain rigid balancing feats, “The Old Brigade” were great at vaulting, and “The Amazons” and “The Mermaids” performed marvels in the way of Swedish Boom exercises.
Still, everybody agreed that though the contests were fun in their way they were not hockey, and the girls would much have preferred the playing-fields, however wet, to the gymnasium.
The girls in the hostel had the hour between four and five o’clock at their own disposal. They were not allowed to leave the College bounds, but they might amuse themselves as they pleased in the garden, playground, or gymnasium. In turns, according to the practising list, they had to devote the time to the piano, and a few even began their prep., though this was not greatly encouraged by Miss Burd, who thought a short brain rest advisable. One afternoon Ingred walked along the corridor with a big pile of music in her arms. Just outside the study she met Verity, and saluted her:
“Cheerio, old sport! Here’s Dr. Linton left his whole cargo behind him today. He rushed off in a hurry and forgot it, and I know he’ll be just raging. I’m going to ask Miss Burd if I may run over into the Abbey and leave it on the organ for him. He has a choir practice tonight, so he’s sure to find it. Will you come with me? Right-o! We’ll both go in and ask ‘exeats.’ ”
===Chinese===
“日常通用的词足足有三千个上下呢！”薇丽蒂笑道，“要是一个词配一个键，你那台打字机就得造得跟教堂的管风琴一般大，还得单给它腾出一间屋子！”
“哦，可你想多省事呀！再也不用翻字典啦！”菲尔理直气壮地宣布。
新学期的烦恼之外，还要添上天气作对：老天爷仿佛存心使坏，一到打曲棍球的下午就下得格外起劲。姑娘们日复一日望着急雨如注，叹惋练不成球。为了让她们好歹活动活动筋骨，学校把她们集中到体育馆里，分队举行对抗表演，项目有印度棒操、莫里斯舞，甚至还有跳绳。“真蓝队”专攻跳高，“先锋队”擅长纹丝不动的平衡绝技，“老近卫队”是腾越的好手，“亚马逊队”和“美人鱼队”则在瑞典式平衡木操上大显神通。
不过大家也一致公认，这些比赛虽各有各的乐趣，终究不是曲棍球。哪怕球场再湿，姑娘们也宁肯上球场，不愿闷在体育馆里。
寄宿舍的女生们，四点到五点那一个钟头归自己支配。她们不许迈出学院地界，但在花园、操场或体育馆里尽可自娱自乐。按练琴轮流表的次序，她们得轮番把这段时间用在钢琴上；有几个甚至着手做起预习来——不过伯德小姐（Miss Burd）不大鼓励，她认为让脑子歇上一歇才是上策。一天下午，英格丽德抱着一摞乐谱沿走廊走来，刚到书房门外便碰上了薇丽蒂，扬手招呼道：
“嘿，老伙计！林顿医生（Dr. Linton）今天把他整整一船货都落下啦。他急匆匆跑掉，忘了个干净，我知道他准会气得冒火。我去问问伯德小姐，能不能让我跑到修道院（the Abbey）去，把东西搁在管风琴上给他留着。他今晚有唱诗班排练，一准看得见。你陪我去吗？好嘞！咱们俩这就进去申请‘外出许可’。”

===Original===
The College was erected upon a plot of land which had originally been part of the Abbey grounds. All the old buildings, formerly inhabited by the monks of St. Bidulph’s, and by the nuns in the adjoining convent of St. Mary’s, had long ago been swept away, and only a few ruined walls marked their sites. The nave of the Abbey, however, had escaped, and was still in use as a parish church, though the beautiful original chancel and transepts had been battered down by Henry the Eighth’s Commissioners. It was only a few hundred yards from the school to the Abbey, and Miss Burd readily gave the girls permission to take Dr. Linton’s music and leave it for him on the organ. It was the first time either of them had been inside the church when no service was going on, and they looked round curiously. The organ was locked, or Ingred would certainly never have resisted the temptation to put on the fascinating stops and pedals. She tried to lift the lid that hid the keyboards, but with no success.
“He might have left it open!” she sighed.
“But the verger would come fussing up directly you began to play,” said Verity.
“I don’t see the verger anywhere about.”
“Why, no more do I, now you mention it.”
“Perhaps he’s slipped across to his cottage to have his tea!”
===Chinese===
学院建在一块地上，那地段原是修道院庭园的一部分。昔日圣比杜尔夫（St. Bidulph’s）修士们，连同毗邻的圣马利亚（St. Mary’s）女修道院里的修女们住过的那些古老屋宇，早就被一扫而空，只余几堵残墙标出旧址。所幸修道院的中殿逃过劫难，至今仍作教区教堂使用——尽管原本秀美的圣坛和耳堂，早叫亨利八世（Henry the Eighth）的专员们砸毁了。从学校到修道院不过几百码，伯德小姐一口应允，让她们替林顿医生把乐谱送过去，搁在管风琴上。两个人还都是头一回趁没有礼拜的当口走进这座教堂，便好奇地四下打量。管风琴上了锁，不然英格丽德说什么也抵不住诱惑，非要按按那迷人的音栓和踏板不可。她试着去掀罩着琴键的盖子，却没有掀动。
“他怎么就不肯留着敞开呢！”她叹了口气。
“可你琴声一响，司事立刻就会急匆匆赶过来的。”薇丽蒂说。
“我怎么没瞧见司事的影儿。”
“咦，你这么一说，我也没瞧见。”
“兴许他溜回小屋喝茶去了！”

===Original===
“Perhaps. I say, Ingred, what a gorgeous opportunity to explore. Let’s look round a little on our own.”
There was nobody to forbid, so they started on a tour of inspection. The places they wanted to look at were those that ordinary churchgoers never have a chance of seeing. They peeped into the choir vestry, and Verity gave rather a gasp at the sight of an array of white surplices hanging on the wall like a row of ghosts. They went down a narrow flight of damp steps into a dark place where the coke was kept, they peered into a dusty recess behind the organ, and into a room under the tower, where spare chairs were stored. All this was immensely interesting, but did not quite content them. Verity’s ambition soared farther. Very high up on the wall, above the glorious pillars, and just under the clerestory windows, was a narrow passage called the Nuns’ Ambulatory. It had been built in the long-ago ages to provide exercise for the sisters in the adjoining convent, to which a covered way had originally led.
“Just think of the poor dears parading round there on wet days when they couldn’t walk in their own garden!” said Verity, turning her head almost upside down in her efforts to scan the passage. “I wonder if they ever felt giddy.”
“There’s a balustrade, of course, but I prefer our modern gym. I believe there’s a walk all over the roof too. Athelstane went up once. He said it was like being on the top of a mountain, and you could look all over the town.”
“What’s that queer stone box thing on the wall?” asked Verity, still gazing upwards.
Ingred followed the line of her friend’s eye to a point above the pillars but below the Nuns’ Ambulatory. Here, built out like an oriel window, was a curious closed-in-gallery of stone, pierced in places by tiny frets. It seemed to have nothing to do with the architecture of the Abbey, and indeed to be a sort of excrescence which had been added to it at some later date. It spoilt the beauty of line, and would have been better removed.
===Chinese===
“兴许是。哎，英格丽德，这可是个探幽揽胜的天赐良机。咱们自己随便转转吧。”
四下无人拦阻，两人便开始了一番巡游察看。她们想看的，正是寻常做礼拜的人永远无缘一见的地方。她们朝唱诗班法衣室里探头，薇丽蒂一见墙上挂着一排白色法衣、活像一列幽灵，不由倒抽一口大气。她们顺一段又窄又潮的台阶下去，钻进堆放焦炭的黑屋子；朝管风琴后面积灰的暗龛里张望；还看了塔楼底下一间存放备用椅子的屋子。这一切都趣味无穷，却仍不能叫她们称心。薇丽蒂的心气儿飞得更高。高高的墙上，在华美的廊柱之上、高侧窗的正下方，有一道窄廊，唤作“修女回廊”（Nuns’ Ambulatory）。那是远古老早的年代修的，好让毗邻女修道院的修女们有处活动筋骨——当初还有一条带顶的廊道直通那边。
“想想那些可怜的人儿吧，雨天进不了自家的花园，只好绕着那儿一圈圈地转！”薇丽蒂说。为了望清那道回廊，她脑袋几乎仰了个底朝天。“不知道她们会不会转得发晕。”
“那儿自然有栏杆，不过我更喜欢咱们现代化的体育馆。我记得屋顶上也有一条环廊。阿特尔斯坦（Athelstane）上去过一回。他说那就像站在高山顶上，整座城尽收眼底。”
“墙上那个石头匣子模样的怪玩意儿是什么？”薇丽蒂问，眼睛还仰着往上看。
英格丽德顺着朋友的视线望去，目光落在廊柱之上、修女回廊之下的一个地方。那儿像凸窗似的探出一个古怪的封闭石廊，壁上处处开着细小的镂孔。它似乎同修道院的建筑全不相干，倒像是后人在某个年月硬添上去的一件赘物，坏了线条的美，拆掉反倒干净。

===Original===
“Oh, that’s the peephole!” said Ingred, lowering her head, for it was painful to stretch her neck in so uncomfortable a position. “It was put up in the seventeenth century, when the whole place was full of those old-fashioned high pews. People were very dishonest in those days, and thieves used to come to church on purpose to pick pockets. So they always used to keep somebody stationed up there, looking down through the holes over the congregation to see that no purses were taken during the service. Nice state of things, wasn’t it?”
“Rather! But I’d love to go up there. I say, the verger’s still at his tea. Shall we try?”
“Right-o! I’m game if you are!”
By the north porch there was a small oak door studded with nails. Generally this was kept locked, but today, by a miracle of good fortune, it happened to be open. It was, of course, a very unorthodox thing for the verger to go away and leave the Abbey unattended, even for half an hour, but vergers, after all, are only human, and enjoy a cup of tea as much as other people who do not wear black cassocks. He was safely seated by the fireside in his ivy-colored cottage at the other side of the churchyard, so the girls seized their golden opportunity. They went up and up and up, along a winding staircase for an interminable way. It was dark, and the steps were worn with the tread of seven centuries, and here and there was a broken bit over which they had to clamber with care. At last, after what seemed like mounting the Tower of Babel, they stumbled up through a narrow doorway into the most extraordinary place in the world. They were in the garret of the roof over the south aisle. Above them were enormous beams or rafters, and below, a rough flooring. It was very dim and dusky, but about midway shone a bright shaft of light evidently from some communication with the interior of the nave. Towards this they directed their steps. It was a difficult progress owing to the huge rafters that supported the roof. A plank pathway about four feet above the floor had been laid across the beams, and along this Ingred decided to venture.
She started, balancing herself with her arms, and kept her equilibrium, though the plank was narrow and sprang as she walked. Verity, who had no head for such achievements, preferred to scramble along the floor, creeping under the rafters, in spite of the thick dust of years that lay there. Eventually they both reached the radius of light, and found another doorway leading down by a few steps into what was apparently a cupboard. In the wall of the cupboard, however, were frets through which the sunlight was streaming. Ingred applied an eye and gave a gasp of satisfaction.
They were in the peephole on the wall of the nave, and could gaze straight down into the church below. It was marvellous what an excellent view they obtained. Nothing was hidden, not even the interiors of the old-fashioned square pews that had lingered as a relic of the eighteenth century. Anybody stationed in this spy-box would certainly be able to keep guard over the congregation, and note any nefarious designs on the pockets of the worshipers.
===Chinese===
“哦，那是窥孔！”英格丽德说着低下头来——脖子仰成那副姿势，实在酸疼。“那是十七世纪安的，当时整座教堂里摆满老式的高背围座。那年头人心不古，小偷专门上教堂来摸人家口袋。于是人们总在上头派驻一个人，透过孔眼俯瞰全体会众，留神别让谁的钱袋在礼拜中间被人摸走。你说说，这算什么世道？”
“可不是嘛！可我真想上去瞧瞧。哎，司事还在喝他的茶呢。咱们试试？”
“好嘞！你敢去我就敢去！”
北门廊旁有一扇钉满铁钉的小橡木门。这扇门平时总锁着，今天却托了天大的福，恰好虚掩。当然，司事撂下修道院不看不管，哪怕只走半个钟头，也是很不合规矩的事；不过话说回来，司事终究也是凡人，同那些不穿黑法衣的普通人一样，也贪恋一杯好茶。此刻他正安安稳稳坐在墓地另一头那座常春藤掩映的小屋里烤火，姑娘们便抓住了这个黄金机会。她们沿一道旋梯往上爬呀，爬呀，爬呀，仿佛没有尽头。楼梯里黑黢黢的，踏级叫七百年的脚步磨得溜光，这儿那儿还缺了破损的一角，得小心攀过去。最后，仿佛登了一趟巴别塔（Tower of Babel）似的，她们跌跌撞撞穿过一道窄门，闯进了全世界最奇特的所在。原来这里是南侧廊屋顶下的阁楼层。头顶上是巨大的梁木椽子，脚底下是粗陋的地板。四处昏昏沉沉，唯有半当腰泻进一柱明亮的光，显然与中殿内部相通。她们便朝那光走去。托着屋顶的椽木又高又大，一路颇为难走。离地板约四英尺高的地方，梁上架着一条木板小道，英格丽德决意就从这上头闯一闯。
她张开双臂保持平衡上了板，板条又窄，一踩还直颤悠，她却始终稳住了身子。薇丽蒂没有走这种险道的本事，宁可顺着地板连爬带钻，从椽木底下匍匐前进，也顾不得那里积着多年的厚尘。末了，两人都到了光亮所及之处，发现又有一道门，下了几级台阶，通进一处看模样是壁橱的地方。然而那壁橱的壁上开着镂孔，阳光正从孔里泻进来。英格丽德凑上一只眼睛，满意得倒抽了一口气。
原来她们已置身中殿墙上的窥孔之中，可以径直俯瞰下面的教堂。视野之佳，令人称奇。什么都藏不住，连那些作为十八世纪遗物留存下来的老式方形围座的内部也一览无余。谁若驻守在这间暗哨小室里，定能看住全体会众，谁对礼拜者的口袋起了歹念，都逃不过这双眼睛。

===Original===
For the moment the church was empty, then footsteps were audible in the porch. Was it the verger returning from his tea? The girls began to flutter at the prospect of his wrath if he discovered them. It was no cassock-clad verger that entered, however, but two young people, far too much interested in each other to gaze upwards towards the frets of the peephole. They thought they had the church to themselves, and walked along conversing in a low tone. The particular shade of flaxen hair in the masculine figure seemed familiar, and Ingred chuckled as she recognized her eldest brother.
“Caught you, old boy! Caught you neatly!” she thought. “Who’s the girl? Oh, I know. It’s one of the Bertrands﻿—Queenie said they were at the Desmonds’ dance, so I suppose he met her there. What a priceless joke! How I shall crow over him for this! They’re actually going to sit down in a pew and talk! Well, this is the limit!”
Quite unconscious that sisterly eyes were watching, Egbert ushered his fair partner into one of the old-fashioned square pews. It was a quiet place to rest, and perhaps the young lady was tired. He sat by her side, very much occupied in explaining something which the girls in the peephole could not overhear. At last the quiet well-trained footsteps of the verger echoed again in the nave. He glanced at the young couple in the pew, and began to dust and rearrange the hymnbooks. Egbert and Miss Bertrand took the hint and departed.
The pair spying through the fretwork above also judged it expedient to beat a hasty retreat. They were terrified lest the verger should remember that he had left the tower door open, and should lock them in. They stumbled back among the rafters, regardless of dust, and groped their rather perilous way down the winding staircase. To their infinite relief the door was not shut, and they were able to creep quietly out and bolt from the Abbey unperceived. They fled along the stone path that edged the churchyard, then stopped under the shelter of a ruined wall to brush the dust off their dresses before reentering the College.
===Chinese===
教堂里一时空无一人，跟着门廊里响起了脚步声。莫非是司事喝完茶回来了？一想到被撞见时他的雷霆之怒，两个姑娘的心便慌慌乱跳。然而进来的并非穿黑法衣的司事，倒是两个年轻人——他们彼此正瞧得入神，哪里想得到抬头望一望窥孔的镂眼。他们只道教堂里没有旁人，一路低声交谈着走来。那男子身影上那一头特别的亚麻色头发看着眼熟，英格丽德认出了自己的大哥，不由得暗自窃笑。
“逮住了，老兄！逮了个正着！”她心中暗想，“这姑娘是谁？哦，我知道了，是伯特兰家（Bertrands）的一位——奎妮（Queenie）说过，她们去德斯蒙德家（the Desmonds）的舞会了，八成他就是在那儿认识她的。多妙的笑料！这下我能好好奚落他一场了！他们居然还要坐进围座里说话去！嘿，真是绝了！”
埃格伯特（Egbert）全然不知有一双妹妹的眼睛正盯着自己，把他那位俏丽的伴侣让进一个老式方形围座。那是个安安静静歇脚的地方，兴许这位小姐也走累了。他在她身旁坐下，专心致志地解释着什么——窥孔里的两位姑娘一句也听不见。终于，司事那轻稳娴熟的脚步声又在中殿里回响起来。他朝围座里的年轻情侣瞟了一眼，动手掸灰，把赞美诗集重新摆弄整齐。埃格伯特和伯特兰小姐（Miss Bertrand）会意，起身走了。
在上头透过镂孔窥探的这一对，也觉得三十六计走为上计。她们吓坏了，生怕司事想起塔门没锁，回头一锁，把她们关在里头。两人顾不得尘土，在椽木间跌跌撞撞往回摸，又摸索着下了那段险象环生的旋梯。万幸门还没有关，她们得以悄悄溜出，神不知鬼不觉地飞奔出修道院。两人沿着墓地边的小石径一路疾走，随后躲进一堵残墙的背风处停下，掸去衣裙上的尘土，再回学院去。

===Original===
“It’s been quite an adventure!” gasped Verity.
“Rather! Particularly catching old Egbert. Won’t he look silly when I bring it out before the family? I don’t know whether I *will* tell them, though! I think I’ll keep it back, so as to have something to hold over his head when he teases me. Yes, that would be far more fun, really. I can hint darkly that I know one of his secrets, and he’ll be so puzzled. I don’t admire his taste much. Queenie detests those Bertrand girls. I don’t know them myself to speak to, but I’m not impressed. Look here, the dust simply *won’t* come off your skirt, Verity!”
“It’ll do as it is, then, and I’ll use the clothes brush afterwards. Don’t worry any more. There’s the Abbey clock striking five! It’s a few minutes fast, fortunately, but we shall simply have to sprint, or we shall be late for tea!”
===Chinese===
“这可真是一场历险！”薇丽蒂喘着气说。
“可不是嘛！尤其是逮住了埃格伯特老兄。等我在全家面前把这桩抖搂出来，瞧他那份窘样！不过——我说不准要不要*说*呢！我想还是先按下不表，往后他招我惹我的时候，手里也好有个把柄。对，这样才更有意思，真的。我可以神神秘秘地暗示，说我知道他的一个秘密，保管让他猜得心痒难熬。要说他的眼光嘛，我可不敢恭维。奎妮就烦透了伯特兰家那几个姑娘。我同她们连话都没搭过一句，可也没什么好印象。瞧，你裙子上的灰简直*死活*掸不掉啊，薇丽蒂！”
“那就先这样吧，回头我再用衣刷刷一刷。别再操心啦。听，修道院的钟敲五点了！幸好快了几分钟，可咱们也得撒腿就跑，不然吃茶点就要迟到啦！”

## XII The Rainbow League / 十二　彩虹联盟

===Original===
Though Lispeth, in her agitation, had not said half the nice things she had intended to say, her little speech had good effect. It reminded the girls of some of the high ideals with which they had started the term, and which, like many high and beautiful things, were in danger of getting crowded out of the way by commoner interests. Everybody suddenly remembered the exhibition and sale which was to come off before Christmas, and made a spurt to send some adequate contribution. The juniors, flattered at having a special branch of their own of the Rainbow League, and time allotted in school to its work, dabbed away blissfully at scrapbook making, with gummy overalls and seccotiny fingers, but complacent faces. The prefects, with intent, dropped in when possible to admire the efforts.
“I believe,” said Lispeth to her special confidante Althea, “that perhaps we were making rather a mistake. You can’t have any influence with those kids unless you keep well in touch with them. I was so busy, I just let them slide before, and I suppose that was partly why they got out of hand, though the little monkeys had no business to get up that impudent strike! They’re as different as possible now, and some of them are quite decent kiddies. Dorrie Barnes brought me a rose this morning. I suppose it was meant as a sort of peace-offering.”
It was arranged to hold what was called “The Rainbow Fête” on breaking-up afternoon, and parents and friends were invited to the ceremony. There was to be both a sale and an exhibition. The best of the toys and little fancy articles were to be at a special stall, and would be sold for the benefit of the “War Orphans’ Fund,” and those that were not quite up to standard would nevertheless be on view, and would be sent away afterwards to help to deck Christmas trees in the slums. *The* stall, as the girls called it, was of course the center of attraction. It was draped with colored muslins in the rainbow tints, and though real irises were unobtainable, some vases of artificial ones formed a very good substitute. The homemade toys were really most creditable to the handicraft-workers, and had been ingeniously contrived with bobbins, small boxes, and slight additions of wood, cardboard, and paper, aided by the color-box. Windmills, whirligigs, carts, engines, trains, dolls’ house furniture, jigsaw puzzles, cardboard animals with movable limbs, black velveteen cats with bead eyes, beautifully dressed rag dolls, wool balls and rattles for babies, and dear little books of extracts, were some of the things set out in a tempting display. Fil, whose slim fingers excelled in dainty work, had contributed three charming booklets of poetry and nice bits cut from magazines and newspapers, the back being of colored linen embroidered with devices in silk. They were so pretty that they were all snapped up beforehand, and could have been sold three times over.
“You promised one to me﻿—you know you did!” urged Linda Slater, much aggrieved at the nonperformance of an order.
“Well, I thought I’d have time to do four, and could only manage three,” apologized Fil. “You see, they really take such ages, and Miss Strong was getting raggy about my prep.”
“You *might* make me one for my birthday!” begged Evie.
===Chinese===
丽丝佩丝（Lispeth）在激动之下，原先备好的一肚子好话连一半都没有说出口，可她这篇小小的演说却收到了极好的效果。它让姑娘们想起了学期初立下的那些崇高理想——而这些理想，正像许多高远美好的事物一样，眼看就要被种种俗常的兴趣挤到一边去了。大家忽然想起圣诞节前便要举办的展览会兼义卖，都赶紧加了一把劲，赶制出像样的物品送去。低年级的姑娘们因为彩虹联盟（The Rainbow League）专门为她们设了一个分部、校方还在课时里专门拨出活动时间，个个受宠若惊，欢天喜地地埋头做剪贴簿，围裙上沾满胶水，手指头黏黏糊糊，脸上却一派志得意满。级长们则存着心思，一得空便踱过来，把她们的成果夸赞一番。
“我看啊，”丽丝佩丝对她的贴心密友阿尔西娅（Althea）说，“咱们先前的做法，恐怕有点失策。你不多跟那些小家伙亲近亲近，就休想对她们有什么影响力。我从前太忙，索性撒手不管，她们后来闹得收不住，我想一部分原因就在这儿——虽说那帮小猴崽子也真不该闹那么一场无法无天的罢工！她们如今简直换了一批人似的，有几个还是相当讨喜的小家伙。今儿早上多丽·巴恩斯（Dorrie Barnes）还给我送来一朵玫瑰呢。我想，那大概是赔罪讲和的意思吧。”
大家商定在散学日的下午举办一场名为“彩虹游园会”（The Rainbow Fête）的活动，并邀请学生家长与亲友前来观礼。会上既有义卖，又有展览。最好的玩具和小巧的手工艺品将摆上特别摊位，出售所得捐给“战争孤儿基金”（War Orphans’ Fund）；稍欠水准的那些也照样陈列，会后将被送去装点贫民区的圣诞树。这座被姑娘们径直唤作“**那座**摊位”的摊子，自然是全场瞩目的中心。摊身披挂着彩虹七色的薄纱，真的鸢尾花虽然无从弄到，摆上几瓶人工假花倒也是很好的替身。那些自制玩具实在给做手工的姑娘们挣足了脸面：全凭线轴、小盒子，添上少量木片、硬纸板和纸，再借颜料盒点染一番，便巧妙地做成了。风车、陀螺、小推车、机车、火车、娃娃屋的家具、拼图、四肢会活动的纸板动物、缀着珠子眼睛的黑绒猫、穿得漂漂亮亮的布娃娃、给婴儿玩的毛线球和拨浪鼓，还有一本本讨人喜欢的小小文选——这些都是摊上诱人展品中的一部分。菲尔（Fil）一双纤细的手指最擅长精巧活计，捐出了三本惹人喜爱的小册子，里面是从报刊上剪下的诗歌与妙文，底衬是彩色亚麻布，用丝线绣着各色纹样。这些册子实在漂亮，还没开卖便被抢订一空，本来足能再多卖上三轮呢。
“你答应过给我一本的——你自己知道你答应过！”琳达·斯莱特（Linda Slater）催促道，为着订单不兑现而大为委屈。
“唉，我原想着来得及做四本，结果只赶出了三本。”菲尔赔不是道，“你是不知道，这东西做起来可真磨人，斯特朗小姐（Miss Strong）都开始为我的晚自习发火了。”
“你**不妨**给我做一本当生日礼物嘛！”伊薇（Evie）央求道。

===Original===
“Certainly not! Those that ask shan’t have!”
“Well, couldn’t you do some during the Christmas holidays?”
“No, I can’t and shan’t!” snapped Fil. “I’m sick to death of making booklets, and I’m not going to touch one of them during the holidays. You seem to think I’ve nothing else to do except cut bits out of magazines for your benefit!”
“There! There! Poor old sport! Don’t get baity!”
“You shouldn’t do them so jolly well, and then you wouldn’t get asked!”
*The* stall occupied a position of importance at the end of the lecture hall, and the rest of the exhibits were put round on trestle tables. They were what Ingred described as “a mixed lot.” Some of the animals were bulgy in their proportions, or shaky in their cardboard limbs, the wheels of the carts did not quite correspond, the windmills were apt to stick, or the puzzles would not quite fit. In spite of their imperfections, however, they looked attractive, and would, no doubt, give great pleasure to the little people who were to receive them, and who were hardly likely to be very critical of their workmanship.
===Chinese===
“才不呢！谁开口要，谁就没有！”
“那，圣诞节假期里你就不能做几本吗？”
“不能，我不干，也不打算干！”菲尔没好气道，“做册子我早就烦透了，假期里一本也不碰。你们倒好，好像我除了从杂志上剪剪贴贴来孝敬你们，就再没有别的事可干了！”
“好了！好了！可怜的老伙计！别急眼嘛！”
“谁让你做得那么漂亮呢？做得差一点儿，不就没人求你了！”
“**那座**摊位”占着讲堂尽头的重要位置，其余展品则摆放在四周支架支起的桌子上。用英格丽德（Ingred）的话说，这些展品是“良莠不齐的一批”。有的动物身量走了形，鼓鼓囊囊；有的纸板四肢摇摇欲坠；推车的几只轮子不太对榫；风车动不动就卡住；拼图也拼不大拢。尽管有这样那样的毛病，它们看上去仍然招人喜欢，而且毫无疑问，会让收礼的小家伙们乐不可支——那帮小家伙多半也不会对做工挑三拣四。

===Original===
To make the afternoon more festive, there was to be a tea stall, to which the girls brought contributions of cakes, and music was to be given from the platform, so that the scene might resemble a café chantant. Ingred had been chosen as one of the artistes, and arrayed in her best brown velveteen dress, with a new pale-yellow hair ribbon, she waited about in her usual agonies of stage fright. Learning from Dr. Linton, however improving it might be to her touch, was hardly conducive to self-complacency, and, after having suffered much vituperation for her imperfect rendering of a piece, it was decidedly appalling to have to play it in public, especially with the horrible possibility that at any moment her master might happen to pop in to view the exhibition and arrive in time for her performance.
“I shall have forty fits if I see him in the room, I know I shall!” she confided to Fil. “You’ve no idea how he scares me. I have my lessons on the study piano generally, and if only he would sit still I shouldn’t mind, but he *will* get up and prowl about the room, and swing out his arms when he’s explaining things; he only *just* missed knocking over that pretty statuette of Venus the other day. I’m sure if Miss Burd knew how he flourishes about, she wouldn’t let him loose among her cherished ornaments!”
“Perhaps he won’t turn up today!”
“Oh yes! He said he should make a point of buying a toy for his little boy. If I break down suddenly in the midst of my piece, you’ll know the reason. I’m shaking now.”
“Poor old sport! Don’t take it so hard!”
By three o’clock the lecture hall was filled with what Lilias Ashby (who had undertaken to write a report for the school magazine) described as “a distinguished crowd.” Fathers indeed were as few and far between as currants in a war pudding, but mothers, aunts, and sisters had responded nobly to the invitations, and were being conducted round by the girls to see their special exhibits.
===Chinese===
为了让这个下午更添喜气，还设了一个茶点摊，姑娘们各自送来蛋糕充作摊上的货品；台上还要奏乐，好教全场像个音乐茶座一般。英格丽德被选中当表演者之一，她穿上自己最好的棕色绒布连衣裙，系上崭新的淡黄色发带，一边干等着，一边照例受着怯场的煎熬。跟林顿医生（Dr. Linton）学琴，不管对指法多么有益，总归难以叫人自信；一首曲子已经为弹得不够好挨过他好一顿痛骂，如今还要当众演奏，实在是够吓人的——更可怕的是，老师随时都可能碰巧进来看展览，不早不晚，恰恰赶上她的演奏。
“他要是在场，我非当场晕厥四十回不可，我知道我一定会的！”她向菲尔吐露道，“你根本想不到他有多吓人。我一般在书房的琴上课，他要是肯安安分分坐着也就罢了，可他偏要站起身来在屋里踱来踱去，讲解的时候还要抡开两条胳膊；前几天差那么一点儿，就把那座漂亮的维纳斯（Venus）小雕像给扫倒了。我敢说，伯德小姐（Miss Burd）要是知道他这么手舞足蹈，绝不敢放他到那些心爱的摆设中间去乱晃！”
“说不定他今天不来呢！”
“嗐，他准来！他说过，今天无论如何要来给他的小儿子买件玩具。要是我半道儿上突然弹砸了，你就知道缘故了。我这会儿已经在发抖啦。”
“可怜的老伙计！别把这事看得那么重嘛！”
到了三点钟，讲堂里已经挤满了莉莉亚丝·阿什比（Lilias Ashby）——她自告奋勇要给校刊写一篇报道——口中的“高朋满座”。父亲们确实寥寥无几，稀罕得就像战时布丁里的葡萄干；母亲们、姑母姨妈们和姐姐妹妹们倒是应邀而至者甚众，气度堂堂，正由姑娘们领着四处参观各自的专项展品。

===Original===
Mrs. Saxon had been unable to come that afternoon, but Quenrede had turned up, looking very pretty in a plum-colored hat, and giving herself slight airs as of one who is now a finished young lady, and no longer a mere schoolgirl. She chatted, in rather mincing tones, to Miss Burd herself, while Ingred stood by in awe and amazement, and when she bought a cup of tea from Doreen Hayward at the refreshment stall, she murmured: “Oh, thanks *so* much!” with the manner of a patroness, though only six months ago she and Doreen had sat side by side in the Science Lectures. It was a new phase of Quenrede, which, though accepted to some extent at home, had never shown itself before with quite such aggravated symptoms.
Ingred, walking as it were in her shadow, was not sure whether to admire or laugh. It was, of course, something to have such a pretty and decidedly stylish sister; she appreciated the angle at which the plum-colored hat was set, and the self-restraint that made the tiny iced bun last such an enormous time, when a schoolgirl would have finished it in three bites, and have taken another. A grand manner was certainly rather an asset to the family, and Queenie was palpably impressing some of the intermediates, who poked each other to look at her.
“It’s my turn to play soon, and I’m just shivering!” whispered Ingred.
“Nonsense, child! Don’t be such a little goose!” declared her sister airily. “It’s only a school party﻿—there’s really nothing to make a fuss about!”
“*Only* a school party!” That seemed to Ingred the absolute limit. Quenrede last term had, in her turn, shivered and trembled when she had been obliged to mount the platform! Could a few short months have indeed effected so magnificent a change of front?
“All the same, it’s I who’ve got to play, not she! It’s easy enough to tell somebody else not to mind,” thought Ingred, as, in answer to Miss Clough’s beckoning finger, she made her way towards the piano to undergo her ordeal.
===Chinese===
萨克森太太（Mrs. Saxon）那天下午来不了，奎恩丽德（Quenrede）却到了。她戴着一顶梅紫色的帽子，模样十分俏丽，还微微端起架子，俨然一位业已成年的大家闺秀，不再是区区女学生了。她拿腔拿调地同伯德小姐本人攀谈，英格丽德在一旁看得又敬又惊；她到茶点摊向多琳·海沃德（Doreen Hayward）买茶时，低声咕哝了一句：“哦，**太**感谢了！”那副派头俨然是个女赞助人——尽管就在六个月前，她跟多琳还并肩坐在科学讲座的课堂上呢。这是奎恩丽德的一个新阶段，在家里虽也多少被默认了，却还从来没有发作得这般来势汹汹。
英格丽德仿佛走在她的影子里，拿不准该佩服还是该发笑。家里有这么一位漂亮而且绝对时髦的妹妹，自然是一桩体面事；她欣赏那顶梅紫帽子斜戴的角度，也欣赏那份自制力——小小一只糖霜圆面包，竟能吃上老大半天；换了女学生，三口就下了肚，还得再拿一只。这份雍容气派对一家子来说确实算得一份资产，而且奎妮（Queenie）分明镇住了几个中年级生，她们你捅捅我、我捅捅你，直朝她张望。
“很快就轮到我弹了，我正抖着呢！”英格丽德悄声道。
“瞎说，孩子！别做只小呆鹅！”她妹妹轻飘飘地宣称道，“不过是学校里的一个游园会罢了——真没什么可大惊小怪的！”
“**不过**是学校游园会！”这话在英格丽德听来简直登峰造极。上学期轮到奎恩丽德上台的时候，她自己不也抖抖索索、战战兢兢！难道短短几个月，真能换来如此气派十足的一百八十度大转变？
“可话说回来，要上台的是我，又不是她！劝别人别在意，倒是一点也不费劲。”英格丽德一面想，一面应着克拉夫小姐（Miss Clough）招唤的手势，朝钢琴走去，去赴那场酷刑。

===Original===
One point in favor of the recital was that the audience moved about the room and went on buying toys or cups of tea and cakes, and even talking, instead of sitting on rows of seats doing nothing but watching and listening. It was rather comforting to think that the concert was really only like the performance of a band, a soothing accompaniment to conversation. Ingred opened her music with an almost “don’t care” feeling. For one delirious moment she felt at her ease, then, alack! her mood suddenly changed. In a last lightning glance towards the audience she noticed among the crowd near the tea-stall the tall thin figure, cadaverous face, and long lank hair of Dr. Linton. The sight instantly wrecked her world of composure. If it had not been for the fact that Miss Clough was standing near, and nodding to her to begin, she would have run away from the platform.
“Oh, the ill luck of it!” she thought. “If I had only played last time, instead of Gertie, I’d have had it over before he came into the room! I know he’ll be just listening to every note, and criticizing!”
With a horrid feeling, as if her breath would not come properly, and her head was slightly spinning, and her hands dithering, Ingred began her “Nocturne,” trying with a sort of “drowning” effort to keep her mind on the music in front of her, instead of on the music-master at the other end of the room. For sixteen bars she succeeded, then came the hitch. She had rejected the offered services of Doris Grainger, and had elected to turn over her own pages. She now made a hasty dash at the leaf, her trembling hand was not sufficiently agile, the sheet slipped, she grabbed in vain, and the music fluttered on to the floor. The performance came to a dead halt. Doris and Miss Clough rushed to the rescue, but they were put politely aside by a tall figure who stepped on to the platform, and Dr. Linton himself picked up the scattered sheets of the unfortunate “Nocturne.” He arranged them together in order, placed them upon the stand, and, addressing his dismayed pupil, said:
“Now, then, begin again, and *I* shall turn over for you. Bring out that forte passage properly! Remember there’s a pedal on the piano!”
It was like having a lesson in public. Ingred felt too scared to begin, and yet she was too much afraid of her master to refuse, so the bigger fright prevailed, and﻿—as a cat will swim to escape an enemy﻿—she dashed at the “Nocturne.” Once restarted, it went magnificently: afterwards, she always declared that Dr. Linton must have hypnotized her, she was sure her unaided efforts could never have rendered it in such style. He behaved as if he were conducting an orchestra, soothing the piano passages and spurring her on to fortissimo efforts, even humming the melody in his eccentric fashion, quite unmindful of the audience. The enthusiastic applause at the end was so evidently for both master and pupil that he bowed instinctively in response.
Ingred, remembering, now the ordeal was over, that she was nervous, melted from the platform, and left him to receive the laurels. He did a characteristic but very kind act, looked round for his pupil, and then, perceiving that she had beaten a retreat, sat down to the piano himself, and, unasked, gave an encore for her. A solo from Dr. Linton was an unexpected treat, especially as he was in the mood for music, and played with a sort of rapture that carried his listeners into an ethereal world of delicate sounds. Ingred, hidden behind a protecting barrier of schoolfellows, could see all the sylphs dancing and the fairy pipers piping as the crisp notes came tripping from his practised fingers. At the end she came back as from a dream, to realize that she was not in elf-land, but in the College Lecture Hall, and that she was sitting on a form next to Miss Strong, who held on her knee a little red-coated, brown-haired boy with Dr. Linton’s unmistakable dark eyes.
===Chinese===
这场演奏有一点可喜：听众并不成排坐在椅子上光看光听，而是在场内走来走去，继续买玩具、买茶点和蛋糕，甚至还聊天。想到这场音乐会其实不过像乐队的演出，是给谈话配的一支抚慰人心的伴奏，倒叫人宽心不少。英格丽德带着近乎“满不在乎”的心情翻开了乐谱。有那么一阵子她陶陶然竟觉自如，可叹哪！心绪陡然一变。她朝台下投去闪电似的最后一瞥，在茶点摊旁的人群里认出了那个瘦高的身形、青白的长脸和稀疏的长发——林顿医生。这一眼顿时把她那方镇定的天地砸了个粉碎。若不是克拉夫小姐就近站着，朝她点头示意开始，她简直要从台上逃下去了。
“唉，真是倒了大霉！”她想，“上回要是换我弹，而不是格蒂（Gertie），早在他进屋之前就弹完了！我知道他准会一个音一个音地听，还要挑剔！”
英格丽德心里一阵发毛，只觉气息不顺，头微微发晕，两手直哆嗦，就这样弹起了《夜曲》（Nocturne），拿出“溺水者挣扎”般的狠劲，硬把自己的心思拴在面前的乐谱上，而不是讲堂那头的音乐老师身上。十六小节都还顺利，随后便出了岔子。她先前婉谢了多丽丝·格兰杰（Doris Grainger）代为翻谱的效劳，决意自己翻页。这时她急急去够那页谱子，颤抖的手不够灵便，纸页一滑，她抓了个空，乐谱飘飘荡荡落到了地板上。演奏彻底停了摆。多丽丝和克拉夫小姐抢上前来救场，却被一个跨上台来的高个身影客客气气地拨到一旁——林顿医生亲手捡起了那首倒霉《夜曲》的散页。他把散页按次序理齐，搁回谱架，对他那吓慌了的学生说道：
“来，重新开始，这回由**我**来替你翻谱。那段强奏要弹足了！记住钢琴上还有踏板呢！”
这简直等于当众上课。英格丽德怕得不敢开始，却又更怕老师，不敢拒绝，于是更大的那重恐惧占了上风——就像猫为了逃命也会泅水一般——她一头扎进了《夜曲》。这一重开头，居然弹得辉煌无比：事后她一直咬定，林顿医生准是对她施了催眠术，她敢担保，凭她独自个儿绝弹不出这等气派。他那架势宛如在指挥一支乐队：弱奏处抚慰，强奏处鞭策，甚至还用他那副古里古怪的腔调哼着旋律，全然不把听众放在心上。曲终时那阵热烈的掌声明明白白是冲着师生二人一齐来的，他便不由自主地鞠躬答谢。
英格丽德这才想起——如今酷刑已过——自己原是怯场的，便悄没声儿地溜下台去，把桂冠留给他一人领受。他做了一件很有其本色、却也极厚道的事：四下环顾寻找他的学生，发觉她已经败退而去，便亲自坐到钢琴前，无人相请，替她加演了一曲。听林顿医生独奏是意想不到的享受，何况他正来了乐兴，弹得如痴如醉，把听众带进了一个精妙音响的缥缈世界。英格丽德躲在同学们筑起的保护墙后面，随着那双娴熟指下流泻出的一串串清脆音符，仿佛看见满眼都是精灵曼舞、仙笛悠扬。一曲既终，她如梦方醒，才意识到自己并非身在仙境，而是坐在学院讲堂的一条长凳上，旁边是斯特朗小姐；小姐膝头抱着一个红外套、棕头发的小男孩，一双深色眼睛一望便知是林顿医生的，错认不了。

===Original===
In that instant, as the music ceased, Ingred received quite a sudden and new impression of Miss Strong; there was a tender look on the mistress’s face, as she held her arm around the child, and she whispered something to him that made the dark eyes dance. He slipped from her lap, and hand in hand they went together towards the toy-stall. It was quite a pretty little scene, one of those tiny glimpses into other people’s lives that we catch occasionally when the veil of their reserve is for a moment held aside. Ingred looked after them meditatively.
“Shouldn’t have thought the Snark capable of it,” she ruminated. “Perhaps she likes boys better than girls. Some people do.”
The toy stall, though half depleted of its contents, was still the center of attraction. Lispeth and Althea were displaying what were left of its windmills and whirligigs to friends who bought with an eye to Christmas presents. Miss Strong, reckless in the matter of expense, purchased the chef-d’œuvre of the whole collection﻿—a wonderful contrivance consisting of two cardboard towers and a courtyard, across which, by means of a tape wound round bobbins, and turned by a handle, walked a miniature procession of wooden soldiers. Little Kenneth Linton received it with open arms.
“Better let me wrap it up in paper,” urged Lispeth. “Somebody said just now that it’s beginning to snow, and you don’t want to have it spoilt before you get it home, do you?”
“N-no,” said Kenneth, relinquishing it doubtfully.
“You’re a lucky boy,” continued Lispeth, as she made up the parcel. “Isn’t that a Teddy Bear in your pocket? And a ball too? There, I believe I’ve used up all the string! What a nuisance! Can anybody get me any from anywhere?”
===Chinese===
音乐停歇的那一瞬，英格丽德对斯特朗小姐骤然生出一个全新的印象：老师一条胳膊搂着那孩子，脸上带着温柔的神色，还凑在他耳边低语了一句什么，逗得那双黑眼睛熠熠生辉。他从她膝头溜下来，两人手拉着手，一道朝玩具摊走去。这是相当动人的一幕小景——偶尔，当旁人那层矜持的帷幕被掀开片刻，我们便能这样窥见他人生活的一鳞半爪。英格丽德若有所思地目送他们远去。
“真想不到‘毒舌精’（the Snark）还有这般能耐。”她寻思道，“说不定她喜欢男孩胜过喜欢女孩。有些人就是如此。”
玩具摊的货虽已去了一半，仍是众人瞩目的中心。丽丝佩丝和阿尔西娅正向朋友们展示剩下的风车和陀螺，那些人都存着买圣诞礼物的念头。斯特朗小姐花起钱来全不心疼，买下了全部藏品中的压卷之作——那是一件奇妙的手工艺品：两座硬纸板塔楼夹着一方庭院，靠着绕在线轴上的一条布带，一摇手柄，一小队木头兵便列队从庭院中走过。小肯尼思·林顿（Kenneth Linton）张开双臂接了过去。
“还是让我用纸把它包上吧。”丽丝佩丝劝道，“刚才有人说开始下雪了，你总不想它到家之前就给毁了吧？”
“呃——好吧。”肯尼思说着，恋恋不舍地撒了手。
“你真是个幸运的小子。”丽丝佩丝一面打包，一面接着说，“你口袋里那只不是泰迪熊吗？还有一个球！哎呀，绳子好像都用光了！真伤脑筋！谁能从哪儿给我弄点绳子来吗？”

===Original===
“I’ll find you some in half a jiff,” said Dorrie Barnes, whisking off immediately.
Since the formation of the Junior Rainbow League, Dorrie had taken a liking to Lispeth which amounted to absolute infatuation. She followed her like a pink-faced shadow, and was always at her elbow, sometimes at convenient and sometimes at embarrassing moments. She fled now, like a messenger from Olympus, with the fixed determination of procuring string for her goddess from somewhere. It was not an easy task, for string was a scarce commodity; what there was of it had mostly been already used, and what was left was jealously guarded by its proprietresses, who refused to part with it, even on the plea that it was for the head prefect. Dorrie, however, was a young person of spirit and resource, and she did not mean to be done. One of the trestles that supported the secondary exhibits of toys had rather come to grief, and had been patched up temporarily with stout twine. Her sharp eyes had noted this fact, so, going down on her hands and knees, she managed to creep unobserved under the table, cut the twine with her penknife, and unwound it. She was just congratulating herself upon the success of her achievement when the unexpected happened, or, rather, what might have been expected by anyone with an ounce of forethought. The damaged trestle, no longer held together, promptly gave way, and the table collapsed, burying a squealing Dorrie amid a shower of toys. She was pulled out, agitated but uninjured, and the scattered exhibits were carried to another table. In the confusion of their transit she managed to secrete the piece of twine, the loss of which had been the cause of the whole upset, and presented it quite innocently to Lispeth, who, not knowing that she was receiving stolen goods, thanked her and tied the parcel. Ingred, who had watched the whole comedy, laughed, but did not give away the secret.
“That child’s an imp!” she said to Quenrede. “But she’s a very accomplished imp. I’ll tell you the joke afterwards, not now! Lispeth little knows where her string comes from, and she’s wrapping up that parcel so placidly! Isn’t the Snark looking quite pretty this afternoon? Never saw her with such a color! Well, if you’re ready, Queenie, we’ll go over to the hostel and get my things. We can just catch the four o’clock train, if we’re quick. Wait half a sec, though! There goes Dr. Linton with Kenneth. I don’t want to walk out under his wing!”
The tall dark figure of the music master was striding through the doorway, carrying his small son, who hugged his toy with one arm, and waved a friendly goodbye with the other.
“What possessed you to drop all your music, child?” said Quenrede, rather patronizingly to Ingred. She was still trying to live up to the plum-colored hat. “You played ever so decently afterwards, though﻿—you did, really! Don’t tell me again that you’re nervous, for it’s all rubbish. You looked as if you enjoyed it.”
“Enjoyed it!” echoed Ingred. “If you’d gone through the palpitations that I felt this afternoon you’d want to go to a specialist, and consult him for heart trouble! I’ve lived through it this once, but if I’m ever asked to play again in public, you’d better go to the cemetery beforehand, and choose a picturesque corner for my grave, and buy a weeping willow ready to plant upon it. Yes, and order a headstone too, with the simple words: ‘Died of fright.’ I mean it! ‘Enjoyed it!’ indeed! Why, I’ve never in the whole of my life been in such an absolutely blue funk!”
===Chinese===
“我眨眼工夫就给你找来。”多丽·巴恩斯说着，一阵风似的转身跑了。
自打低年级彩虹联盟成立以来，多丽对丽丝佩丝生出一股爱慕，简直到了痴迷的地步。她像一道红扑扑的小影子似的跟着她，总黏在她胳膊肘边上，有时来得是时候，有时来得不是时候。这会儿她一溜烟跑了，活像奥林匹斯山上下来的信使，铁了心要从什么地方给她那位女神弄到绳子。这可不是件容易差事，因为绳子是稀罕物件：场里的绳子多半已经用掉，剩下的都被各位主人心疼地守着，哪怕搬出“这是给总级长用的”这样的大道理，人家也不肯放手。不过多丽是个有胆量有主意的小人儿，不打算善罢甘休。支着次要玩具展品的一副支架先前坏得不轻，用粗麻绳临时捆缚过。她的尖眼睛早把这一情势记在心里，于是手脚并用地趴下去，趁着没人瞧见爬到桌底下，用小折刀割断麻绳，一圈圈绕了下来。她正为自己的大功告成洋洋得意，意外发生了——或者不如说，凡是有一星半点先见之明的人都料得到的事发生了。那副伤损的支架没了麻绳的捆束，登时散了架，桌子轰然坍塌，把尖声叫喊的多丽埋在了一阵玩具雨底下。她被拽了出来，惊魂未定，倒没伤着；四散的展品被搬上了另一张桌子。趁搬运忙乱，她把那截麻绳——闯下这场大祸的祸根——悄悄藏好，然后一脸天真地把绳子献了上来；丽丝佩丝不知自己收的是赃物，道了谢，把包裹捆了个结实。英格丽德把整出喜剧看了个全程，直笑，却没有戳穿这个秘密。
“那孩子是个小妖精！”她对奎恩丽德说，“不过是个很有本事的小妖精。这桩笑话回头再讲给你听，现在先不提！丽丝佩丝压根儿不知道她的绳子打哪儿来，还在那儿安安稳稳地打包裹呢！‘毒舌精’今天下午看着是不是怪美的？从没见她气色这么好过！好了，奎妮，你要是准备停当，我们就上寄宿舍去取我的东西。腿脚快些，还正好赶得上四点钟的火车。且慢，稍站半秒！林顿医生带着肯尼思走过去了。我可不想躲在他翅膀底下跟他一道出去！”
音乐老师那瘦长黝黑的身影正大步流星地走出门口，怀里抱着他的小儿子；孩子一只胳膊搂着玩具，另一只胳膊友好地挥着道别。
“你到底中了什么邪，把整本乐谱都掉在地上了，孩子？”奎恩丽德对英格丽德说，语气颇带几分屈尊俯就——她还一直在努力撑住那顶梅紫帽子的气派。“不过后来你弹得相当像样——真像样，真的！以后再别跟我说你怯场，那全是废话。你瞧上去弹得津津有味呢。”
“‘享受’！”英格丽德回敬道，“你要是经受过我今天下午那种心悸，早就得去找专科大夫看心脏病了！这一回我算是死里逃生，可要是再有人请我当众演奏，你最好提前跑一趟公墓，替我的坟地挑个景色别致的角落，再买好一棵垂柳备着栽上。对，还得订上一块墓碑，上头就镌几个简简单单的字：‘吓毙’。我说的是真心话！亏你说得出‘享受’！哼，我这辈子还从来没有像刚才那样彻底地吓破过胆！”

## XVII The Rivals / XVII 对手

===Original===
This book does not propose to extol an ideal heroine, only to chronicle the deeds and thoughts of a girl, who, like most other girls, had her pleasant and her disagreeable moods, her high aspirations and good intentions, and her occasional bursts of bad temper. Ingred had been very passionate as a child, and, though she had learnt to put on the curb, sometimes that uncomfortable lower self would take the bit between its teeth and gallop away with her. It is sad to have to confess that the enjoyment of her walking tour was entirely spoilt by an ugly little imp who kept her company. In plain words she was horribly jealous of Bess. Ingred liked to be popular. She was gratified to be warden of “The Pioneers” and a member of the School Parliament. She felt she had an acknowledged standing not only in her own form but throughout the college. Her official position, her cleverness in class, her aptitude for music, her skill at games, made her an all-round force and a referee on most subjects. There is no doubt that Ingred would have had the undivided post of favorite in her form had it not been for Bess Haselford. Not that Bess was in any way a self-constituted rival﻿—on the contrary she was rather shy and retiring, and made no particular bid for popularity. Perhaps that was one reason why the girls liked her. She was generous in lending her property, invited her form-mates to charming parties at Rotherwood, and often persuaded an indulgent father to include some of her special chums in motoring expeditions on Saturday afternoons. She had, indeed, taken up the exact role that Quenrede had played years ago, before the war, and which Ingred would have followed had Rotherwood and a car still been in the Saxons’ possession. In spite of several overtures from Bess, Ingred had thrust away all idea of friendship, and had steadily refused any invitations to her old home. The reports which the girls brought back of the renewed glories of Rotherwood made her feel like a disinherited princess. She considered it rough luck that her supplanter should be at the same school and in the same form as herself, and decided that Bess had ousted her from both house and favor. It made it only the more aggravating that Bess’s musical talent was quite equal, if not superior, to her own. Bess had improved immensely on the violin, and her performance at the end-of-term recital had received quite a little ovation.
When the question of the walking tour was broached, Bess, owing to home engagements, had at first reluctantly refused, then had managed to rearrange her holidays and had joined the party after all. To Ingred her presence utterly marred the enjoyment. It was extremely unreasonable of Ingred, for Bess was most unassuming and really very long-suffering. She put up with snubs that would have made most girls retaliate indignantly. Nobody likes to be sat upon too hard, however, and even the proverbial worm will turn at last.
As the walking party, much urged by Miss Strong straggled along towards Ryton-on-the-Heath, Bess made a lightning dive up a bank and came back with a blue flower plainly of the Labiate species.
“Bugle!” she remarked with satisfaction.
===Chinese===
本书无意颂扬一位十全十美的女主角，只想如实记述一个女孩的言行与心思——她和大多数女孩一样，有招人喜欢的时候，也有惹人厌烦的时候，有远大的抱负和向善的心愿，偶尔也会发一阵坏脾气。英格丽德（Ingred）从小就性子急烈，虽然学着给自己套上了笼头，可有时那个沉在心底、不体面的自我仍会咬紧嚼子，驮着她一路狂奔而去。说来遗憾，这次远足的乐趣，就是被一个同她形影不离的丑陋小恶魔给搅坏的。说白了，她对贝丝（Bess）嫉妒得要命。英格丽德喜欢受人欢迎。能当上“先锋队”（The Pioneers）的班代表、跻身学校议会（School Parliament），她颇为得意，觉得自己不但在本班、而且在全校都有公认的地位。她的职务、课堂上的聪慧、音乐上的悟性、运动场上的身手，使她成为面面俱到的人物，在大多数问题上都是一言九鼎的“裁判”。毫无疑问，倘若没有贝丝·哈泽尔福德（Bess Haselford），“全班最受宠”这把交椅本可以由英格丽德独坐。倒不是说贝丝存心自立山头来争——恰恰相反，她相当腼腆怕生，从不刻意博取人缘。也许这正是姑娘们喜欢她的原因之一。她借东西出手大方，常请同班同学到罗瑟伍德（Rotherwood）参加精彩的聚会，还常常哄得溺爱她的父亲点头，周六下午驾车出游时捎上她的几个密友。她担当的，其实正是多年前、战前奎恩丽德（Quenrede）扮演过的那个角色；倘若罗瑟伍德和一辆汽车仍归萨克森家（the Saxons）所有，这本该由英格丽德承袭下去。尽管贝丝几番示好，英格丽德却把交朋友的念头一概推开，对她回旧宅做客的邀请一概婉拒。姑娘们带回的关于罗瑟伍德重焕荣光的种种传闻，使她觉得自己像个被夺去继承权的公主。顶了她位子的人偏偏和她同校又同班，她认为这运气实在糟透了，认定贝丝既霸了她的宅子，又抢了她的风光。更叫人窝火的是，贝丝的音乐天分即便不比她强，也和她不相上下。贝丝的小提琴突飞猛进，学期末演奏会上的一曲还赢得了一阵不小的喝彩。
远足一事提上日程的时候，贝丝因为家里有事，起初只得勉强回绝，后来又设法重新安排了假期，终究还是入了伙。对英格丽德来说，有她在场，兴致全消。这实在是英格丽德蛮不讲理，因为贝丝为人低调，又实在能忍。换了大多数女孩早就愤然还击的抢白，她都默默咽下了。不过，谁也受不了总被人踩在脚下——俗话说得好，虫子逼急了，最后也会翻身。
远足的队伍在斯特朗小姐（Miss Strong）不住声的催促下，松松散散地朝荒原畔赖顿（Ryton-on-the-Heath）迤逦而行。贝丝忽然一个箭步蹿上路旁的土坡，回来时手里多了一朵蓝花，一望便知是唇形科的花。
“筋骨草！”她满意地说。

===Original===
“Bugle?” echoed Ingred scornfully. “Shows how much you know about botany! That’s self-heal!”
“Oh no; it’s certainly bugle.”
“I tell you it’s self-heal. I found some at Lynstones last August and looked it up in the flower-book.”
“Very likely you did, but that doesn’t prove that this is self-heal.”
“It does, for anybody with a pair of eyes. I’ve been studying botany.”
“And so have I!”
===Chinese===
“筋骨草？”英格丽德轻蔑地学着舌，“可见你的植物学学得有多好！这是夏枯草！”
“才不是呢，这分明就是筋骨草。”
“告诉你这就是夏枯草。去年八月我在林斯通斯（Lynstones）见过这种花，还专门查了花卉图鉴。”
“你查过归查过，可证明不了这一朵就是夏枯草。”
“怎么证明不了？只要长着一双眼睛就看得出来。我可是一直在学植物学。”
“我也在学！”

===Original===
“You may think you know everything, Bess Haselford, but you don’t know this.”
“I didn’t say I knew everything; but I’m certain this is bugle all the same, and I stick to it!”
Bess’s usually sweet voice had an obstinate note in it for once. She seemed determined to defend her botanical trenches.
“Go it﻿—hammer and tongs!” laughed Kitty. “I’ll back the winner!”
“And I’ll take the case into court,” said Linda, snatching the flower from her schoolfellow’s hand and running on to show it to Miss Strong, who was an authority on the subject.
The mistress paused to let the others overtake her.
===Chinese===
“贝丝·哈泽尔福德，你也许自以为无所不知，可这朵花你偏偏不认得。”
“我没说自己无所不知，可我敢肯定这就是筋骨草，说什么我也不改口！”
贝丝平日温软的嗓音，这回头一次带上了执拗的调子。她似乎铁了心，要死守自己的植物学战壕。
“吵呀——放开手脚大吵一场！”凯蒂（Kitty）笑道，“我押赢家赢！”
“我来把这个案子送上公堂。”琳达（Linda）说着，一把从同学手里抢过那朵花，跑上前去请斯特朗小姐过目——这方面她可是权威。
老师停下脚步，等其余的人赶上来。

===Original===
“Bugle, certainly,” she decided emphatically. “The first bit we’ve found this year. It’s out early. Self-heal? Oh dear no! The two are rather alike and are sometimes mistaken one for another, but no botanist would dream of confusing them. Bugle is a spring and early summer flower, and self-heal blooms much later. Make a note in your nature diaries that you found bugle on 15th April.”
Considerably squashed, Ingred had for once to acknowledge her botany to be at fault, and, though Bess did not triumph, Francie gave Kitty a poke and the pair giggled.
“Well, of course, one can’t be always right,” said Ingred airily.
“So it seems; though some people set themselves up for wiseacres!” sniggered Kitty.
Ingred fell behind with Verity and let the others walk on. It was only a trifling incident, but she was annoyed to notice how openly and instantly the girls had sided with Bess. She felt too glum for speech, and as Verity was tired and disinclined to talk, they tramped along in silence.
===Chinese===
“筋骨草，没错。”她斩钉截铁地下了断语，“这是今年头一回见着，开得算早的。夏枯草？哎呀，才不是呢！这两种花是有点相像，有时会认混，可随便哪个植物学家也决不会把它们弄错。筋骨草是春天和初夏的花，夏枯草要晚得多才开。在你们的自然日记里记上一笔：四月十五日采得筋骨草。”
英格丽德碰了个结结实实的钉子，只得破天荒头一遭承认自己的植物学出了岔子。贝丝倒没有耀武扬威，弗朗茜（Francie）却捅了捅凯蒂，两个人咯咯直笑。
“呃，当然啦，人嘛，总不能永远是对的。”英格丽德轻飘飘地说。
“可不是嘛——不过有些人总爱自封万事通！”凯蒂嗤嗤地笑。
英格丽德落到后面，同薇丽蒂（Verity）走在一处，任其余的人先行。这只是件鸡毛蒜皮的小事，可姑娘们那样公开、那样不假思索地站在贝丝一边，她瞧在眼里，心里老大不痛快。她闷得懒得开口，薇丽蒂也乏了，不想说话，两人便一路无言地跋涉。

===Original===
They had been winding steadily uphill for some miles and were now on the heath from which Ryton took its name. The ground fell steeply to the west, showing glimpses of a great river in the valley below, where the still-leafless woods had burst here and there into faint tokens of spring. Beyond the river rose the characteristic grey hills of the neighborhood, with their stone walls and sheepfolds and stretches of moorland, looking a little hazy in the afternoon light, but with patches of yellow gorse catching the sunshine. Ryton was a delightful little village. Its cottages, built long ago by local craftsmen, seemed absolutely in harmony with the landscape: walls, dormers, and mullions and long undulating roofs were all of limestone and conveyed an impression of sturdy self-respect. The rain-worn, lichen-covered roofs had weathered to charming irregularities of form and lovely tones of color. Ivy and clematis climbed over the porches and twisted themselves round the low chimneys. The little gardens were bright with daffodils, mezereon, and flowering currant.
To the girls, somewhat tired and decidedly hungry, the main focus of the village was a long iron post which stretched out over the street and supported a rudely-painted sign of a bird, whose species might have been a puzzle to an ornithologist but for the words “Pelican Inn” that appeared beneath it.
In the long-ago days before railroads, the little hostelry had been a stopping-place for stagecoaches, and a wooden board still set forth that it supplied “Posting in all its branches.” The landlord would no doubt have been much dismayed if any wag had entered and demanded a chaise and post-horses to drive to Gretna Green, and a shabby motor in his stable-yard showed that he marched with the times.
===Chinese===
她们盘旋着稳步爬坡，走了好几英里，如今已登上赖顿因之得名的荒原。地势向西陡然跌落，谷底一条大河时隐时现；还未生叶的树林里，处处绽出春日淡淡的讯息。河对岸耸起这一带特有的灰色山峦，石垒的围墙、羊圈和连绵的沼地，在午后的光线里显得有些朦胧，唯有几丛金黄的荆豆迎着阳光灼灼耀眼。赖顿是个讨人喜欢的小村子。村中农舍是当年本地工匠所建，与四周风景浑然一体：墙身、屋顶窗、窗棂和绵长起伏的坡顶一色的石灰岩，透着一股朴拙自重的气派。风雨剥蚀、苔藓斑驳的屋顶，已风化出种种可爱的参差形态和悦目的色泽。常春藤和铁线莲爬满门廊，又缠绕上低矮的烟囱。小花园里，水仙、欧瑞香和花茶藨子开得正盛。
在这些略感疲乏、肚子又确确实实饿了的姑娘眼里，全村瞩目的中心是一根横伸到街面上方的长铁杆，杆上挑着一块画工粗糙的鸟儿招牌——多亏底下写着“鹈鹕客栈”（Pelican Inn）几个字，否则那鸟属于哪一科，恐怕连鸟类学家也要犯难。
在铁路尚未出现的久远年代，这家小客栈曾是公共马车打尖歇脚之处，如今一块木板上还标明本店“承办各色马车租赁生意”。倘若有哪个促狭鬼进门要雇一辆双轮轻马车、配上驿马直奔格雷特纳格林（Gretna Green），掌柜的准会大惊失色；不过马厩院子里停着的一辆寒碜汽车，倒表明他也与时代同步前进。

===Original===
Miss Strong, on consulting her watch, decided that her party might safely indulge in a halt of half an hour, and ordered tea for nine persons. The inn, built on a type common in the district, was entered by an archway leading straight into a courtyard. A door on the right led to the bar, and a door on the left to the coffee-room. To this latter more aristocratic quarter Miss Strong conducted her pupils. Some of them had never before been in a small village hostelry, and were much amused at the quaint old parlor with its sporting prints, its glass cases of stuffed squirrels and badgers, and its horsehair-seated chairs with crochet antimacassars hung over the backs. The atmosphere was certainly rather redolent of stale beer and tobacco, but a bunch of crimson wallflowers on the table did their best to spread a pleasant perfume. The tea, when, after much delay, it arrived, was delicious. The Pelican was a farm as well as an inn, and the rosy-faced servant girl carried in cream, fresh butter, and red-currant jam to the coffee-room. She apologized for the absence of cake, but it was an omission that nobody minded. Upland air gives good appetites, and, though Miss Strong reminded her flock that this was only a meal by the way, and that supper was ordered for them at Dropwick, they set to work as if they would taste nothing more till midnight. There was something so delightfully fresh and out of the common in having tea at a wayside inn; they felt true pilgrims of the road, and civilization and school seemed to have faded into a far background. The love of travel is in the blood of both Celt and Anglo-Saxon; our forefathers visited shrines for the joy of the journey as well as for religious motives, and maybe our Bronze Age ancestors, who flocked to the great Sun Festivals at Stonehenge or Avebury Circles, derived pleasure from the change of scene as well as a blessing from the Druids. The Romans, those great pioneers of travel, had opened out the district eighteen centuries ago, and laid a straight, paved road from Wendcester to Pursborough; the remains of their fortified camps and of their villas were still left to mark their era. The foss-way, leading from Ryton-on-the-Heath to Dropwick, was their handiwork, and our pilgrims were to march on the identical track of some old Roman legion.
It must be owned that when tea was finished they were very unwilling pilgrims, and would gladly have spent the night at The Pelican and have slept in the funny, musty, low-ceiled little bedrooms upstairs.
“Couldn’t we possibly stop here?” implored Verity.
But Miss Strong, having booked rooms in Dropwick, was adamant.
===Chinese===
斯特朗小姐看了看表，断定全队尽可放心歇上半个钟头，便吩咐备九个人的茶点。这家客栈是本地常见的格局，进身先是一道拱门，径直通入院落；右手一扇门通酒吧间，左手一扇门通咖啡室。斯特朗小姐把学生们领进了后面这间格调较高的雅座。有几个姑娘从前从没进过乡村小客栈，见着这间古雅的老客厅只觉趣味盎然：墙上挂着狩猎版画，玻璃柜里陈列着松鼠和獾的标本，马鬃垫底的椅背上搭着钩针织的椅罩。屋子里的空气确乎有股走汽的啤酒和烟草味儿，好在桌上一束绯红的桂竹香竭力散出清馨。茶点左等右等才端上来，味道却美极了。鹈鹕客栈兼营农场，那位脸蛋红扑扑的帮工姑娘给咖啡室端来奶油、新鲜黄油和红醋栗酱。她为没有糕点连声道歉，不过这点缺憾没有一个人放在心上。高地的空气最开胃口。虽然斯特朗小姐提醒她这一群羊羔，这不过是路上的便餐，晚饭已在德罗普威克（Dropwick）订好，姑娘们照样大快朵颐，仿佛过了这一顿，不到半夜再没得吃似的。在路边客栈用茶，自有一种说不出的新鲜别致；她们自觉成了道地的朝圣客，文明与学校仿佛都退到遥远的天边去了。对旅行的热爱，流淌在凯尔特人和盎格鲁-撒克逊人共同的血液里；我们的祖先千里迢迢朝拜圣地，既为虔敬，也为旅途本身的欢愉；或许青铜时代的先民成群结队赶赴巨石阵（Stonehenge）或埃夫伯里石圈（Avebury Circles）的盛大太阳节，既为领受德鲁伊祭司的祝福，也为贪图换一换风景的乐趣。罗马人，那些旅行事业的伟大先驱，早在十八个世纪之前便开通了这片地方，从温德切斯特（Wendcester）到珀斯伯勒（Pursborough）铺了一条笔直的石板大道；他们设防营垒和别墅的残迹至今犹存，标示着他们那个时代。由荒原畔赖顿通往德罗普威克的福斯古道（foss-way）正是他们的手笔，我们的朝圣者们，就要踏着某一支古罗马军团走过的同一条旧路行进了。
必须承认，茶一用完，她们便成了一群满心不情愿的朝圣者，巴不得就在鹈鹕客栈投宿，去睡楼上那些古怪有趣、霉味扑鼻、天花板低矮的小卧室。
“我们就不能在这儿住下吗？”薇丽蒂央求道。
可斯特朗小姐已在德罗普威克订好了房间，说什么也不松口。
===Original===
“Besides which I wouldn’t trust the beds here,” she remarked. “So early in the year they’re almost bound to be damp, and we don’t want any of you laid up with rheumatic fever as the result of our trip. I prefer to give a wayside inn a week’s notice if I mean to sleep there in April. Nobody has had enough coal during the winter to keep fires going in spare bedrooms. That front room was as chilly as a country church! You won’t feel so tired, Verity, when you’re on your feet again, and it’s all downhill to Dropwick.”
The Temperance Hotel, where the girls finally stayed their weary feet, was quite modern and unromantic, though well aired and fairly comfortable. Ingred, whom the fates had placed to sleep with Nora, had a trying night, for her obstreperous bedfellow had a habit of flinging out her arms, and of appropriating the larger half of the clothes, leaving poor Ingred to wake shivering. Also, the bed sloped towards the middle, so that both girls had to poise themselves on a kind of hillside, and were constantly rolling down and colliding. These troubles, however, were only incidental in the Pilgrimage, and certainly might have been worse.
On comparing notes at breakfast nearly everybody had had similar experiences. Miss Strong confessed to a patent mattress with a broken spring jutting up in the center, round which she had been obliged to lie in a curve. Linda and Francie had slept near the water-cistern, which alarmed them with weird noises, and Bess and Kitty, trying to open their window wider, had found it lacked sash-cords, and descended like a guillotine, sending the prop that had upheld it, flying into the street. Though they groused at the time, the girls laughed as they discussed these details over the eggs and bacon. The sun was shining and they felt rested, and quite ready once more to shoulder their kit and set out on the march.
===Chinese===
“再说了，这儿的床铺我信不过。”她说，“年头这么早，褥子十有八九是潮的，我可不想我们这趟出门，倒害得哪个孩子染上风湿热躺倒。要是打算四月在路边客栈过夜，我总得提前一个星期打招呼。这个冬天谁家的煤都不够烧，还能在闲置的卧室里整日生火不成？方才那间前屋，冷得跟乡下教堂一个样！薇丽蒂，等你重新上了路，就不觉得这么累了，到德罗普威克可是一路下坡呢。”
姑娘们最终歇下疲惫双脚的那家禁酒旅馆（Temperance Hotel）十分新式，毫无诗意可言，好在透风良好，也还舒服。命运安排英格丽德同诺拉（Nora）合睡，这一夜真够她受的：这位不安分的同床素来爱抡胳膊，还要霸去大半床被子，害得可怜的英格丽德屡屡冻醒。加之那张床往中间塌陷，两个女孩只得各自稳踞一侧“山坡”，却又不住地滚落相撞。不过这些苦处，不过是朝圣途中的小小枝节，比起可能遭的罪，实在算不得什么。
早餐时一交换情报，几乎人人都有同等的遭遇。斯特朗小姐承认自己那张新式床垫正中断了一根弹簧，直挺挺翘起，她只得绕着它弓身而卧。琳达和弗朗茜睡在蓄水池近旁，被池中奇怪的声响吓得不轻；贝丝和凯蒂想把窗户推大些，才发现窗扇没挂吊带，唰地一声像断头台似的砸下来，把撑窗的木棍崩到了街上。当时虽然牢骚满腹，此刻就着鸡蛋和培根谈论这些细节，姑娘们却笑个不停。阳光正好，她们也歇足了，重新背起行囊，又精神抖擞地上路了。

===Original===
There was nothing of very great interest to see in Dropwick itself, though it was a quaint enough old-fashioned market-town, with a fifteenth-century church tower, and a few black and white houses. Miss Strong decided not to waste any time there, but to push on as fast as possible across the hills to Sudbury, where there was a fine Romano-British villa that was well worth a visit. So the foss-way took them up, and up, and up, through fir-woods where the new cones were showing like candles on Christmas trees, and alongside a quarry where they pounced upon some quite interesting fossils in the heaps of stones by the road, and over a craggy weatherworn peak, where, again, they caught the magnificent view of the valley and the river and hills beyond. Then down again, through more fir-woods, where the timber was being felled, and great tree-trunks lay piled in rows one above another, and past banks that were a dream, with starry blackthorn blossom and primroses growing beneath, to where the crossroads met and the signpost pointed an arm to Sudbury.
The Romans might take their roads straight as an arrow across moor and hill, but they chose out the beauty spots of the land on which to build their villas, and were careful to fix upon a southern aspect and shelter from the prevailing winds. The remains of the old settlement lay behind a farm, and had been carefully excavated by a local antiquarian society. Visitors applied at the farmhouse, entered their names in a book, paid their admission money, and were escorted round by a guide.
Time, and successive conquests, had demolished the greater part of the villa, but its foundations and some of the old brick walls could be plainly traced. The great bath, that indispensable feature of a Roman establishment, could still be seen, with its beautiful tesselated pavement, inlaid with mosaics of doves, cupids, and designs of fruit and flowers. The heating system also, with the leaden pipes and remains of furnaces, was a testimony to the civilization of the period, and the amount of comfort that the legions brought with them into their foreign exile. A large shed had been fitted up as a museum, and held a number of objects that had been dug up during the excavations. The girls, poring over the glass cases, looked with interest at a Roman lady’s silver hand-mirror, toilet pots, and tiny shears that must have been the early substitute for scissors. More fascinating still were the toys from a little child’s grave, small glass bottles, roughly-made animals of clay, and a carved object that no doubt had been at one time a treasured doll, though now it was crumbling into dust.
===Chinese===
德罗普威克本地虽说是个够古雅的老式集镇，有一座十五世纪的教堂钟楼和几栋黑白相间的木骨架房屋，却没有多少特别可看的东西。斯特朗小姐决定不在镇上耽误工夫，尽快翻山越岭赶往萨德伯里（Sudbury）——那里有一座出色的罗马-不列颠别墅，很值得一看。于是福斯古道领着她们向上，向上，再向上，穿过冷杉林——新生的球果像圣诞树上的蜡烛一样立在枝头；沿着一处采石场走——她们在路边石堆里翻出几块颇有意趣的化石；又翻过一座嶙峋的风蚀峰顶——山谷、大河和远处群山的壮景再度尽收眼底。接着又是下坡，穿过更多的冷杉林——林中正在伐木，粗大的树干一层压着一层码成垛；路旁的坡岸美得如梦似幻，缀满星星点点的黑刺李花，花下生着丛丛报春花——一直走到十字路口，路牌伸出一条臂膀，直指萨德伯里。
罗马人修路，尽可以笔直如箭地横穿沼地与山冈；可挑选建别墅的地点，他们却专拣风景绝佳之处，还精心选定朝南的坡向，避开常刮的风。古老聚落的遗址坐落在一座农庄后头，已由当地的古物学会细心发掘。参观者到农舍登记姓名、付过门票钱，便有导游领着四处讲解。
岁月和一次次征服早已毁掉别墅的大半，但房基和几段古砖墙仍清晰可辨。大浴池——罗马宅第里不可或缺的设施——依然可见，美丽的镶嵌地面上，拼嵌着鸽子、小爱神以及花果纹样的马赛克。那套供暖系统，连同铅管和炉灶的残迹，也见证着彼时的文明，见证着军团将士随军带到异乡戍地的种种安逸。一座大棚屋布置成了陈列馆，收藏着发掘出土的众多物件。姑娘们俯在玻璃柜上，兴致勃勃地观看一位罗马夫人的银手镜、化妆小罐，还有一把小小的铰剪——想必是剪刀的早期替身。更引人入胜的，是出自一座幼儿坟墓的玩具：小小的玻璃瓶、捏得粗糙的泥塑小兽，还有一件雕出来的物件——想必当年是孩子心爱的玩偶，如今却渐渐碎成齑粉。

===Original===
Among the pile of broken statues or fragments of ornamental stonework in the corner was a monumental tablet, cracked across in two places, but pieced together for preservation with iron rivets. The inscription ran:
> “*D.M. Simpliciæ Florentinæ Animæ Innocentissimæ quæ vixit menses decem. Felicius Simplex Pater fecit. Leg. vi, V.*”
> (To the Divine Shades. To Simplicia Florentina, a most innocent soul, who lived ten months. Felicius Simplex of the Sixth Legion, the Victorious, the father, erected this.)
Some of the girls glanced at the tablet, and the English translation of the inscription which lay near, and turned away without much notice. But Ingred stood gazing at them with a catch in her throat. They brought a whole pathetic human story to life again. She could picture the noble Roman father, leader of the victorious legion, sent over from Italy and making his home here in a conquered foreign land, as our officers do in India, and bringing with him his lady with her Roman customs and her slaves. Those few brief words﻿—“a most innocent soul who lived ten months”﻿—told the tragedy of the cherished little daughter whose frail life faded in the fogs of the British climate about eighteen hundred years ago. Hearts are the same all the world over, and the pretty dark-eyed Roman baby must have been laid to its rest with as much grief and sadness as the fair-haired darlings whom British mothers sometimes bury in Indian soil.
===Chinese===
墙角一堆残破的雕像和装饰石件中，立着一方纪念碑，两处断裂，用铁铆钉拼合起来加以保存。碑文是：
> “*D.M. Simpliciæ Florentinæ Animæ Innocentissimæ quæ vixit menses decem. Felicius Simplex Pater fecit. Leg. vi, V.*”
> （致神圣的亡灵。致西姆普利齐娅·弗洛伦蒂娜——一个至为无辜的灵魂，她在世间活了十个月。第六“胜利”军团的费利修斯·辛普莱克斯，她的父亲，立此碑。）
有几个姑娘朝石碑和旁边陈列的碑文英文译文扫了一眼，不甚在意地走开了。英格丽德却伫立凝望，喉咙里一阵发紧。这几行字，让一段惹人酸楚的人间旧事重新活了过来。她可以想见那位高贵的罗马父亲、胜利军团的统帅，从意大利渡海而来，在征服的异邦安家落户——正如我们的军官如今驻在印度那样——身边带着他的夫人，连同她的罗马规矩和她的奴隶。那寥寥数字——“一个至为无辜的灵魂，在世间活了十个月”——道出了一出悲剧：约一千八百年前，掌上明珠般的小女儿娇弱的生命，就在不列颠的雾气中凋零了。人心四海皆同，那个黑眼睛的漂亮罗马娃娃下葬时，必定也满怀悲恸与哀伤，正如英国母亲有时把金发爱女安葬在印度土地上时一样。

===Original===
“It’s a sweet name, too﻿—Simplicia Florentina!” mused Ingred. “I wonder what she would have grown up like. And what her history would have been! I’d give worlds to know more about her!”
“Aren’t you coming, Ingred?” called Verity from the doorway. “Miss Strong says we ought to be getting on now.”
Ingred brought her thoughts back with an effort to the twentieth century, and joined the waiting party outside. Miss Strong was talking to their guide, who was describing a shortcut across the fields that would save them several miles on their way to Pursborough.
Verity, after calling to her friend in the museum, had run out. Ingred followed her, to find her with her arm locked closely through Bess’s. There was no reason why she should not display such a mark of affection, but to Ingred it seemed little short of an insult to herself. Verity, her particular chum, to have openly gone over to the enemy! She stared at her in surprise. Verity did not appear to notice the stare, however, and walked on quite calmly.
Miss Strong had decided that they should find a quiet place along the lane where they could eat their lunch before beginning the second part of their march. She fixed on a lovely spot with a high wooded bank at the back and in front fields that sloped to the river. There were specks of yellow in these fields, and Kitty who finished her sandwiches first, ran to inspect nearer and reported cowslips. Instantly most of the girls went scrambling over the stile.
===Chinese===
“这名字也真美——西姆普利齐娅·弗洛伦蒂娜（Simplicia Florentina）！”英格丽德出神地想道，“她长大后会是什么模样？又会有怎样的一生啊！要是能多知道一点她的事，让我拿什么去换都愿意！”
“你还不来吗，英格丽德？”薇丽蒂在门口喊道，“斯特朗小姐说我们该动身了。”
英格丽德费了番劲，才把思绪拽回二十世纪，出门归入等候的队伍。斯特朗小姐正和导游说话，导游在指点一条穿越田野的近道，去珀斯伯勒能省下好几英里路。
薇丽蒂在陈列馆里喊过她这位朋友，便先跑出去了。英格丽德跟出来，却见她紧紧挽着贝丝的胳膊。她原可以随意对人表示亲热，谁也挑不出错来，可落在英格丽德眼里，这简直近于对她的侮辱。薇丽蒂，她的贴心密友，竟然公开投到“敌方”去了！她惊讶地直盯着她。薇丽蒂却似乎全然不觉这道目光，神色泰然地继续前行。
斯特朗小姐决定，先在小路上寻个清静处吃午饭，然后再开始第二程行军。她选定了一处可爱的所在：背后是林木葱茏的高坡，面前是一片缓缓斜向河边的田野。田野里点点金黄，凯蒂头一个吃完三明治，跑近去探看，回来报告说是黄花九轮草。姑娘们登时大半翻过阶梯栅门，采花去了。

===Original===
Miss Strong, who had bought picture-postcards of the Roman villa, and was addressing them with a stylo-pen, did not follow the exodus. She called to Ingred, however, who was last.
“Warn the girls,” she said, “not on any account to go into that meadow where there is a horse with a young foal. The guide at the farm said it is a savage beast and will attack people. Be sure to tell them *all*!”
“I’ll run after them now,” answered Ingred, calling “Cuckoo!” to attract their attention.
She told Belle and Linda and Verity, who were near to the stile, and Linda passed the news on to Francie and Kitty. Bess was quite a long distance down the field, gathering blackthorn from the hedge.
“I’m not going to tear all that way after her!” thought Ingred crossly. “Verity will be sure to tell her. They seem inseparable today. Besides which nobody’s particularly likely to go into that other meadow. There are plenty of cowslips here.”
It took Miss Strong a much longer time to write her postcards than she had originally intended, and while she was thus employed her girls spread themselves out in quest of flowers. It is always amazing when you start rambling in company with others how quickly you can find yourself alone. By the time Ingred had gathered a fragrant, sweet-smelling bunch and looked round for somebody to admire it, her schoolmates were gone. She hunted about for them, and noticed Verity’s green jersey and Kitty’s brown tam-o’-shanter in the wood above. Surely they must all be up there together.
===Chinese===
斯特朗小姐买了几张罗马别墅的图画明信片，正用铁笔在上面写地址，没有随这股人流而去。不过，她招呼了落在最后的英格丽德。
“去提醒姑娘们，”她说，“千万别进那块牧场，里面有匹带着小驹子的母马。农庄的导游说那牲口性子凶，会伤人。务必一个不落地全都告诉到！”
“我这就去追她们。”英格丽德应道，喊了一声“布谷！”招引大家注意。
她告诉了栅门近旁的贝尔（Belle）、琳达和薇丽蒂，琳达又把话传给弗朗茜和凯蒂。贝丝却在田野很远的那一头，正沿着树篱采黑刺李花。
“我才不跑那么远去追她呢！”英格丽德气恼地想，“薇丽蒂准会说给她的。她们俩今天简直形影不离。再说，谁会偏偏跑进那块牧场去？这儿的黄花九轮草多的是。”
斯特朗小姐写明信片，费去的工夫远比原先料想的长；趁她埋头书写，姑娘们已四散寻花去了。同人一起信步闲逛，居然转眼间就能落了单，这总是件奇事。等英格丽德采好一束香气袭人的花，环顾四周想找人赞赏一番，同学们早已无影无踪。她四处寻找，瞥见上方树林里有薇丽蒂的绿毛衣和凯蒂的棕色苏格兰便帽。想必她们全都聚到那上头去了。

===Original===
She was just going to follow, when a qualm of conscience seized her. She had not delivered Miss Strong’s message to Bess, and it would perhaps be as well to ascertain that the latter had not strayed unwarned into the danger zone.
“It’s not at all likely,” Ingred kept repeating to herself, as she walked briskly along the meadow to the fence. “I’m really only going on a wild goose chase.”
Likely or unlikely, it was the very thing which had happened. The cowslips on the other side of the railings were larger and finer, and Bess, having no fear of horses, had climbed over and wandered some way down the field. Only about twenty yards from her the lanky foal was gambolling round its mother, a big draught mare, cropping the grass innocently enough at present, and apparently not perceiving trespassers.
If Bess could retreat quietly and unnoticed from the field all might be well. Ingred did not dare to call for fear of attracting the mare’s attention. If Bess would only turn round she might wave to her. But Bess kept her back to the fence and had no idea of danger. There was only one course open to Ingred. She slipped over the railings and went along the meadow to warn her schoolfellow. In a few quiet words she explained the situation.
“Don’t run,” she whispered. “Let us walk back and perhaps it will take no notice of us.”
The girls went as softly as possible, looking over their shoulders every now and then to see that all was safe. Of bulls they had a wholesome terror, but they had had no previous experience of a savage horse.
===Chinese===
她正要跟上去，忽然良心一阵不安。斯特朗小姐的口信她还没有传给贝丝，最好还是去看个明白，别让贝丝毫无警觉地误入险地。
“根本不可能出事。”英格丽德一面快步沿牧场走向围栏，一面不住地对自己念叨，“我这分明是白跑一趟。”
可能也好，不可能也罢，偏偏就出了这档子事。围栏那边的黄花九轮草长得更大更鲜亮，贝丝又素来不怕马，便翻了过去，朝田野深处走了好一程。离她不过二十来码，那匹细脚伶仃的小马驹正绕着妈妈撒欢——那是一匹高大的役用母马，眼下一派安分地啃着草，似乎并未察觉闯进来的生人。
只要贝丝能悄没声儿、不惹眼地退出牧场，便万事无虞。英格丽德不敢喊，生怕惊动母马。倘若贝丝肯回一回头，她原可以朝她挥手的。可贝丝始终背对着围栏，浑然不觉大祸临头。英格丽德别无他法，只得翻过围栏，穿过牧场去提醒同学，低声三言两语讲明了处境。
“别跑。”她悄声道，“咱们慢慢走回去，说不定它根本不理会咱们。”
两个女孩放轻脚步，尽量悄声地走，不时回头张望，确认一切无恙。对公牛，她们素来心怀戒惧；恶马伤人，她们却还从未领教过。

===Original===
They were about fifteen yards from the railings, when the mare, which hitherto had been feeding quietly, raised her head and lumbered round. She saw strangers in her territory; her primeval instinct was to protect her foal, and she came tearing across the field with wild eyes and lip turned back from gleaming teeth. The girls fled for their lives. It was a question of which could reach the railings first, they or the dangerous brute whose huge hoofs thundered behind them. Ingred, who was the taller and the stronger of the two, seized Bess by the hand and literally dragged her along. Together they tumbled over the fence somehow and rolled down the bank into the safe shelter of some gorse bushes. For a moment they were afraid the mare would leap after them, but the height of the rails balked her; apparently she was satisfied with routing the enemy and returned across the field to her foal. The girls, with shaking knees, got up and hurried towards the lane where they had left Miss Strong.
“You’ve saved my life, Ingred!” gasped Bess, as they went along.
“No, I haven’t!” choked Ingred. “At least, it was my fault you ever went into the field at all. Miss Strong told me to tell you the horse was savage, and you were such a long way off picking cowslips that I didn’t trouble to go after you. I trusted to Verity telling you.”
“Verity ran the other way with Kitty.”
“I know. Well, at any rate, it was my fault and I’m ready to take the blame. Precious row I shall get into with the Snark!”
“Why should we say anything about it?”
===Chinese===
离围栏还有十五码上下，那匹一直安静吃草的母马忽然抬起头，沉重的身子转了过来。它看见自己的地界里闯进了生人；护驹的原始本能猛地攫住了它，它双眼狂乱，上唇翻起，露出森亮的牙齿，横过田野狂奔而来。两个女孩没命地逃。谁先摸到围栏成了性命交关的问题——是她们俩先到，还是那头巨蹄如雷、紧追在后的可怕畜生先到。英格丽德是两人中个子高、气力大的那个，一把攥住贝丝的手，几乎是拖着她跑。两个女孩连滚带爬地翻过围栏，滚下土坡，躲进一丛荆豆里，才算脱了险。有一瞬，她们真怕母马会跟着跃过来，好在栏木的高度叫它却了步；它显然以为把敌人逐出领地便已心满意足，就转身穿过田野，回到驹子身边去了。两个女孩膝盖打颤地爬起来，匆匆朝留下斯特朗小姐的小路赶去。
“你救了我一命，英格丽德！”贝丝一路走，一路喘着气说。
“哪的话！”英格丽德嗓音发哽，“再说，你进那块牧场，本来就全怪我。斯特朗小姐吩咐我告诉你那马性子凶，可你采黄花九轮草采得那么远，我就懒得追过去了，一心指望薇丽蒂会说给你听。”
“薇丽蒂跟凯蒂往另一边跑了。”
“我知道。呃，反正错在我，要罚我认罚就是。这事儿要是让‘毒舌精’（the Snark）知道了，我可要挨好一顿克！”
“咱们何必提这件事呢？”

===Original===
“Not say anything?”
“There’s really no need. It’s over and done with now. I don’t want to get you into a scrape. I vote we just keep it to ourselves.”
Ingred paused, with her hand on the gate, and gazed with unaffected astonishment at her companion.
